Assistant2026-09-19T17:22:33.463Z
Thinking
Assistant2026-09-19T17:22:43.803Z

Confirmed WIP — it does not work. Three independent signs:

1. The branch doesn't even build. Its workspace Cargo.toml lists certora/merkle_distributor as a member; that directory doesn't exist on the branch. Cargo fails at manifest resolution before compiling anything.

2. No .conf runs a single macro-generated rule. ownable_invariants.conf lists exactly the nine hand-written rules from the old ownable_invariants.rs (after_constructor_owner_is_set, …). The generated names — ownable_invariant_transfer_ownership, pending_owner_invariant_*, sanity_invariant_* — appear in no conf on any branch. They were never submitted to the prover.

3. The code carries unfinished scars I read past. pending_owner_invariant.rs's check_post ends in an unconditional cvlr_assert!(false), with three different cvlr_inv! formulations commented out above a note about vacuity; sanity_invariant.rs is only cvlr_assert!(false); CvlrProp::assume_post is declared and never called; and every invariant hand-writes impl CvlrLog under "// trait implementation don't look here" — bypassing the impl_cvlr_log! macro in the same file that exists to generate it. The file is literally named ownable_invariant2.rs, sitting beside the original.

And a related thing I should have checked at the same time: cvlr-spec 0.6's own rule-generation DSL has no worked example either. cvlr_rules! → cvlr_rule_for_spec! expands to a call to cvlr_impl_rule!, which the spec author must supply. It is defined only inside cvlr's own tests (cvlr-spec/tests/test_spec.rs:1138, cvlr-macros/tests/expand/) — zero occurrences across all five stellar-contracts branches.

So the corrected picture: the only idiom with evidence of passing the prover is the hand-written assume_pre_X / assert_post_X pair on port — those rule names appear in confs and carry // status: verified. Both the parametric-property sketch and cvlr-spec's cvlr_rules! are unexercised.

That changes the plan rather than killing it. Redis's cross-product is far worse than ownable's — order of dozens of invariants × ~240 commands — so hand-writing every pair isn't viable, and some generation layer is needed. But it's now a thing we'd have to build and validate ourselves, with its own risk, not a pattern to copy. I'll write it into CLAUDE.md that way, and it's worth an early throwaway milestone: get one generated rule through the prover before committing to the structure.

The survey is still running; I'll fold this in when it lands.

AREA: CVLR library (/home/apanda/code/certora/cvlr) — actual public API surface, macro grammars, and what the Certora Prover consumes

SUMMARY: CVLR v0.6.1 is a 12-crate Rust workspace (8 `#![no_std]` libs + 4 proc-macro crates) whose entire verification semantics is a set of `extern "C"` declarations of `CVT_*` symbols (`CVT_assert`, `CVT_assume`, `CVT_satisfy`, `CVT_sanity`, `CVT_nondet_*`, `CVT_nativeint_u64_*`, `CVT_calltrace_*`, `CVT_rule_location`, `CVT_u128_*`, `memhavoc_c`) that the Certora Prover interprets symbolically; the `rt` feature supplies `#[no_mangle]` concrete stubs so the same code runs natively for testing. Rules are plain Rust `fn`s marked `#[rule]`, which only adds `#[no_mangle]`, prepends `cvlr::log::cvlr_rule_location!()` and appends `cvlr::cvlr_vacuity_check!()` — so the Prover locates rules by exported symbol name in a compiled artifact. The toolchain is unambiguously tied to compiled Rust: every `link(wasm_import_module = "env")` is gated on `target_family = "wasm"`, `NativeInt`'s doc says an SBF word maps to a 256-bit symbolic integer, and the sibling stellar-contracts repo builds `cargo +nightly-2024-11-22 build --target=wasm32-unknown-unknown --release --features certora` and hands the Prover `target/wasm32-unknown-unknown/release/*.wasm` via `certora_build.py`. There is **zero** evidence of a C-language frontend anywhere in the local files — no clang/LLVM/`.bc`/bpf references, no C headers, no build scripts outside cargo. `cvlr-spec` adds a formula/spec/lemma algebra (`CvlrFormula`, `CvlrPredicate`, `CvlrSpec`, `CvlrLemma`) plus macros, but `cvlr_rules!` / `cvlr_invariant_rules!` / `cvlr_rule_for_spec!` bottom out in a macro named `cvlr_impl_rule!` that CVLR **does not define** — the user must supply it, and no real (non-test) example exists in this workspace. The `assert_that` DSL is a proc-macro that pattern-matches on `syn::Expr`: a

Rules are plain Rust `fn`s marked `#[rule]`, which only adds `#[no_mangle]`, prepends `cvlr::log::cvlr_rule_location!()` and appends `cvlr::cvlr_vacuity_check!()` — so the Prover locates rules by exported symbol name in a compiled artifact. The toolchain is unambiguously tied to compiled Rust: every `link(wasm_import_module = "env")` is gated on `target_family = "wasm"`, `NativeInt`'s doc says an SBF word maps to a 256-bit symbolic integer, and the sibling stellar-contracts repo builds `cargo +nightly-2024-11-22 build --target=wasm32-unknown-unknown --release --features certora` and hands the Prover `target/wasm32-unknown-unknown/release/*.wasm` via `certora_build.py`. There is **zero** evidence of a C-language frontend anywhere in the local files — no clang/LLVM/`.bc`/bpf references, no C headers, no build scripts outside cargo. `cvlr-spec` adds a formula/spec/lemma algebra (`CvlrFormula`, `CvlrPredicate`, `CvlrSpec`, `CvlrLemma`) plus macros, but `cvlr_rules!` / `cvlr_invariant_rules!` / `cvlr_rule_for_spec!` bottom out in a macro named `cvlr_impl_rule!` that CVLR **does not define** — the user must supply it, and no real (non-test) example exists in this workspace. The `assert_that` DSL is a proc-macro that pattern-matches on `syn::Expr`: a top-level `Expr::Binary` with a comparison op routes to `cvlr_assert_<op>!`, everything else to `cvlr_assert!`, with a special-cased `Expr::If` that recurses into branches and a literal `true` that compiles to `()`.
5	
6	GOTCHAS:
7	  - cvlr_rules! / cvlr_invariant_rules! / cvlr_rule_for_spec! are UNUSABLE out of the box: they expand to `cvlr_impl_rule!{name, spec, base}` (cvlr-macros/src/rule_for_spec.rs:97) and CVLR never defines that macro. The only two definitions in the workspace are test stubs explicitly commented 'this would be provided by the user in real usage'. For a Redis spec you must either write your own cvlr_impl_rule! (it receives an ident, a CvlrSpec expr, and a base fn ident; it must emit a #[rule]-style #[no_mangle] fn that builds a pre-state ctx, calls assume_requires, calls the base, builds a post-state ctx, and calls check_ensures) or skip the cvlr_rules! layer and write #[rule] fns by hand. Writing them by hand is the path every real example in this workspace takes.
8	  - The macro_rules! spec macros take the context type as `$ctx: ident`, NOT a type. So cvlr_def_predicate!, cvlr_def_states_predicate!, cvlr_predicate!, and cvlr_lemma! (arm 1) all reject `Foo<T>`, `crate::Foo`, `&Foo`, or any path. Only the #[cvlr_predicate] attribute macro accepts arbitrary types (it parses a syn::Type behind a reference, cvlr-macros/src/predicate.rs:41-49). Plan Redis context structs as bare, non-generic, in-scope idents.
9	  - Generated code hard-codes absolute crate paths: `::cvlr::asserts::...` (assert_that.rs:66), `::cvlr::spec::CvlrFormula` (predicate.rs:191), `cvlr::log::log_scope_start` and `cvlr::clog!` (asserts.rs:9-13), `::cvlr::nondet::nondet()` (derive/lib.rs:18). The umbrella crate MUST be nameable as exactly `cvlr` at the crate root — no renaming in Cargo.toml, and in a 2018+ edition crate that only depends on sub-crates the macros will not compile. Tests work around this with `extern crate cvlr;` (cvlr-spec/tests/test_spec.rs:3).
10	  - #[rule] inserts `cvlr::log::cvlr_rule_location!()` which expands to `$crate::log_rule_location(std::file!(), std::line!() as u64)` (cvlr-log/src/core.rs:243-248). That names `std`, not `core`. In a `#![no_std]` crate with no `extern crate std`, #[rule] will fail to compile. This is likely why the Soroban (no_std) contracts use cvlr_soroban_derive::rule instead of cvlr's.
11	  - The optional `$desc: literal` third argument on cvlr_assert!/cvlr_assume!/cvlr_satisfy! and on every comparison macro is parsed and then thrown away — it appears nowhere in the expansion (cvlr-asserts/src/core.rs:71-75). Descriptions will NOT show up in counterexamples. Use clog! for anything you want to see.
12	  - cvlr_assume! emits no add_loc!() (cvlr-asserts/src/core.rs:78-83) while cvlr_assert! and cvlr_satisfy! do. A failing path will not point at the assumption that constrained it.
13	  - Inside cvlr_assert_that!(if g { a < b }), the comparison does NOT become cvlr_assert_lt! — the if-branch recursion goes straight to the boolean handler, so you lose the per-operand operand logging (assert_that.rs:102; confirmed in test_cvlr_assert_that_true_and_if.expanded.rs:21-26). If you want operand values in the counterexample, use cvlr_assert_lt_if!(g, a, b) instead.
14	  - cvlr_assert_that!(true) compiles to `()` — a literal-true assertion is silently deleted (assert_that.rs:83-90). Also, if-branches must contain exactly one expression statement or you get a compile_error! (assert_that.rs:111-116).
15	  - cvlr_def_states_predicate! and two-parameter #[cvlr_predicate] poison their single-state methods with `cvlr_assert!(false); panic!(...)` (macros.rs:155-166, predicate.rs:219-232). If you hand a two-state predicate to something that calls .assert() (e.g. as the `invariant` of cvlr_invar_spec!, whose check_ensures calls self.1.assert(post_state) at spec.rs:158-161) the rule fails unconditionally. Invariants must be SINGLE-state; only the `ensures` slot of cvlr_spec! gets two states.
16	  - CvlrLemma::apply() is the logical dual of verify_with_context(): it ASSERTS requires and ASSUMES ensures (spec.rs:311-314). Mixing them up turns a proof obligation into an unsound assumption.
17	  - Converting NativeInt back to u64/u128 silently injects `cvlr_assume!(is_u64())` / `cvlr_assume!(is_u128())` (nativeint_u64.rs:225, 529). Every such conversion prunes the state space. Do the arithmetic entirely in NativeInt and only compare; avoid round-tripping.
18	  - cvlr-fixed's rt stubs for u128_max/u256_max are `assert!(false); todo!()` (nativeint_u64.rs:140-150), so is_u128()/is_u256() cannot be exercised natively under `rt` — only symbolically.
19	  - `nondet::<bool>()` costs a symbolic u64 (`cvlr_nondet_u64() > 0`, scalars.rs:85), and #[derive(Nondet)] on an enum draws a full u64 per value (derive/lib.rs:135). Nondet-heavy Redis contexts will be expensive; prefer deriving Nondet only on small structs.
20	  - havoc (memhavoc/alloc_havoced) needs cvlr-nondet's `std` feature. cvlr's default feature enables it, but a consumer that writes `cvlr = { default-features = false }` — which is exactly what every stellar-contracts package does (packages/*/Cargo.toml:17) — loses havoc entirely. Also its rt stub lacks #[no_mangle] so it is not resolvable natively even with rt (havoc.rs:9-17).
21	  - #[cvlr_hook_on_exit(f())] inserts at index len-1 when the function has a return type (cvlr-hook/src/lib.rs:96-99). For a body whose last statement IS the tail expression, the hook lands BEFORE it, not after — verified in cvlr-hook/tests/expand/test_hook_combined.expanded.rs:40-48 where hook_end() appears as the first line of abs2. 'on_exit' is really 'before the tail expression'.
22	  - cvlr_and! only handles 2..=6 arguments (macros.rs:914-940). Seven conjuncts is a macro-match failure with a confusing error. Nest manually or use cvlr_eval_all!/cvlr_assert_all!.
23	  - The `rt` and `vacuity` features are mutually incompatible in spirit: with `vacuity` on, #[rule] appends cvlr_assert!(false), which under `rt` is a real assert! and will panic every test. `vacuity` is a Prover-side mode where a PASSING rule means the rule was vacuous.
24	  - No f32/f64 anywhere: no Nondet impl, no CvlrLog impl, no NativeInt conversion. Any Redis behavior involving doubles (ZSET scores, INCRBYFLOAT) cannot be modeled directly in CVLR primitives.
25	  - CVLR has no notion of threads, locks, memory ordering, or interleaving. There is no happens-before, no atomic, no scheduler primitive anywhere in the 15k lines. Concurrency properties must be encoded structurally — e.g. as two-state predicates over an explicit abstract state, or by writing a rule that nondeterministically picks an interleaving and asserts a linearizability witness — not with any built-in CVLR facility.
26	####################################################################################################
27	AREA: Redis 8.9.241 build/toolchain reality and verifier-ingestible targets (/home/apanda/code/certora/redis)
28	
29	SUMMARY: Redis 8.9.241 is a 211,424-line C tree (194,638 .c + 16,786 .h across 135 .c / 84 .h files) plus 141,137 lines of vendored deps (jemalloc, lua 5.1.5, hiredis 1.2, tre, xxhash 0.8.3, hdr_histogram, linenoise, fpconv). It builds with plain GNU make: top-level `make` delegates to `scripts/build.sh` (Makefile:114-115), which recurses into `src/` where `redis-server` is linked from 118 objects plus 3 in-tree vector-set objects; `redis-sentinel`, `redis-check-rdb`, `redis-check-aof` are literal copies of `redis-server`, and `redis-cli`/`redis-benchmark` are separate binaries. There is ZERO formal-methods, model-checking, or verification artifact anywhere in the tree — a grep for certora|cvlr|cbmc|klee|frama-c|verifast|seahorn|smack|dafny|alloy|TLA+ over the whole repo returns 0 hits; the only assurance infrastructure is CodeQL (cpp), Coverity, valgrind/helgrind, ASan/MSan/UBSan/TSan, and one tcl fuzzer. Whole-program Redis-to-wasm is not realistic: `server.h` (4,976 lines) is included by 72 of 127 top-level .c files and itself pulls `<pthread.h>`, `<sys/socket.h>`, `<netinet/in.h>`, `<syslog.h>` and `<lua.h>`; the program depends on fork() for BGSAVE/AOF-rewrite, epoll/kqueue/evport event loops, 3 bio worker threads plus N io-threads, ucontext/backtrace crash handling, an autoconf'd jemalloc, and a Lua 5.1 interpreter that uses setjmp/longjmp. A narrow carve-out IS different in kind, but not the one the task names: 53 of 127 src/*.c (dict.c, listpack.c, ziplist.c, intset.c, quicklist.c, rax.c, kvstore.c, ebuckets.c, sds.c, zmalloc.c…) never include server.h and already carry in-binary unit tests reachable via `./src/redis-server test <name>`; by contrast t_string.c and db.c DO include server.h, and even plain SET reaches server.dirty, notifyKeyspaceEvent, keyModified, rewriteClientCommandVector, addReply and the `shared` globals, so {t_string.c, db.c, dict.c} is not a closed subsystem. LLVM bitcode is the far more plausible ingest path — with CC=clang at the default -O3 the Makefile already turns on `-flto` (src/Makefile:29-36), so the .o files are LLVM bitcode. Crucially, CVLR itself is a Rust-only library whose primitives link as `wasm_import_module = "env"` extern "C" CVT_assume/CVT_assert (cvlr-asserts/src/core.rs:2-11): there is no C frontend, so any CVLR spec for Redis must be written against a Rust model, with Redis's 464 command JSON files (433 carrying JSON-Schema reply_schema) and its 5,148 tcl tests serving as the semantic oracle.
30	
31	GOTCHAS:
32	  - README.md:204 says "These instructions apply to Redis 8.10 and above" but src/version.h:1 says REDIS_VERSION "8.9.241". The README documents the 8.10 build flow (make bootstrap / make modules-update / scripts/build.sh) and the tree already has that machinery, so follow the README — but do not cite 8.10-only features (e.g. BUILD_COMPRESSION zstd, README:395) as shipped-in-8.9 behaviour without checking src/.
33	  - `make bootstrap` and `make modules-update` both require network access and bootstrap installs system packages that can OVERRIDE your compiler/CMake/LLVM (README.md:264). Neither is needed for a core-only build: plain `make` in this tree builds redis-server with no modules because modules/*/src is absent.
34	  - src/cluster_asm.c (3,942 lines) is 'Atomic Slot Migration', NOT assembly language. The only real inline asm in src/ is 3 lines in monotonic.c:269,279,306 (aarch64 cntvct_el0, riscv csrr time), and those are behind arch/USE_PROCESSOR_CLOCK guards.
35	  - `make 32bit` silently forces SKIP_VEC_SETS=yes (src/Makefile:561), and so does any compiler without C11 _Atomic (src/Makefile:65-70). The command surface of the binary therefore depends on the build, not just the version.
36	  - src/commands.def (13,236 lines) is checked in AND generated. It regenerates only if python3 is on PATH (src/Makefile:502-510). Treat src/commands/*.json as the source of truth, not commands.def.
37	  - redis-sentinel, redis-check-rdb and redis-check-aof are the SAME binary as redis-server (src/Makefile:468-477) — behaviour is selected by argv[0]. Do not model them as separate programs.
38	  - A grep for 'formal'/'verif' in this tree will produce false positives from tcl test helper names (run_cmd_verify_hist) and from Lua/tre/hiredis upstream prose. The true answer is that there is no verification artifact of any kind.
39	  - CVLR's assertion primitives are Rust `extern "C"` symbols linked as `wasm_import_module = "env"` (cvlr-asserts/src/core.rs:2-11). There is no C or bitcode ingest path in the cvlr tree. Compiling Redis C to wasm would not by itself make it CVLR-specifiable; the spec would have to sit on a Rust model.
40	  - LTO is on by default at -O3, which means a naive `make CC=clang` already produces bitcode .o files — convenient, but it also means -O3 has run over the code before the bitcode exists. For verification you would want `make noopt CC=clang REDIS_CFLAGS='-flto'` (src/Makefile:567-568 sets OPTIMIZATION=-O0, which disables ENABLE_LTO at :29, so LTO must be re-added explicitly).
41	  - The test suite starts real redis-server processes on ports 21111+ (tests/test_helper.tcl:50-51) and needs tclsh 8.5-9.0. It is an execution oracle, not a static artifact — extracting semantics from it means reading the .tcl, not running it, unless you can afford the runtime.
42	  - `tests/unit/moduleapi/*.tcl` (48 files) and `tests/modules/*.c` (48 files) test the module API, not user-visible command semantics. They are noise for a behavioural spec.
43	  - Vector sets (VADD/VSIM/VREM) are core commands in this build, not a loadable module — so 'core Redis command surface' in 8.9 is larger than the classic data types. Conversely the four pinned modules (search/json/timeseries/bloom) are absent from this checkout, so their commands are NOT in src/commands/*.json.
44	####################################################################################################
45	AREA: CVLR spec practice as written and run in /home/apanda/code/certora/stellar-contracts (rule taxonomy, invariant idiom, nondet/ghost/munge idioms, .conf fields, certora_build.py + justfile build path)
46	
47	SUMMARY: The stellar-contracts corpus is 484 `#[rule]` functions across 55 spec files in 13 `specs/` directories, driven by 56 `.conf` files. It is 100% hand-written raw `cvlr_assert!`/`cvlr_assume!` — there is not a single use of the cvlr-spec DSL (`cvlr_lemma!`, `cvlr_def_predicate!`, `cvlr_spec!`, `cvlr_rules!`, `cvlr_invariant_rules!`) anywhere, because the repo pins `cvlr = "0.4.0"` while cvlr-spec first shipped in cvlr 0.6.0. Rule files fall into five rule categories (`_integrity`, `_invariants`, `_panics`, `_non_panics`, plus vault-specific `_conversions`/`_rounding`) and two non-rule support categories (`_contract` harness contracts, and `helper.rs`/`nondet.rs`/`ghosts.rs`/`*_munged.rs` scaffolding). Integrity relates post-state to pre-state for one function with no invariant assumption; invariants prove one predicate inductively with one rule per (invariant, mutating-function) pair using a hand-written `assume_pre_X` / `assert_post_X` helper pair. Panic rules end in `cvlr_assert!(false)` and pass iff the call always traps; non-panic rules end in `cvlr_assert!(true)`, prime storage with `storage_setup_*` helpers, and require `-trapAsAssert true`. Nondeterminism comes from `cvlr::nondet::Nondet` + `cvlr_soroban::nondet_*`, with bounded (MAX=5) hand-rolled vector/map builders, `#[cfg(feature = "certora")] impl Nondet` blocks placed next to the domain types, `static mut` GhostVar/GhostMap for cross-contract calls, and trivial mock trait impls returning `nondet()`. The whole harness is gated behind a `certora` cargo feature that also deletes event emission; the prover ingests `target/wasm32-unknown-unknown/release/stellar_<pkg>.wasm` built by `just build` via `certora_build.py`. Nothing in the repo documents how to actually invoke the prover — no README section, no CI job, no runbook.
48	
49	GOTCHAS:
50	  - The .conf files are NOT strict JSON — several have trailing commas inside arrays (packages/tokens/confs/fungible_invariants.conf ends its rule list with `"fungible_after_burn_balance_geq_zero",` before `]`; packages/access/confs/access_control_non_panics.conf has `"true",` before `]`). Any tooling that reads them must use a relaxed/JSON5 parser. Timeout values are inconsistently typed too: `"global_timeout": "7200"` (string) in packages/access/confs/access_control_invariants.conf:26 vs `"global_timeout": 7200` (int) in packages/tokens/confs/vault_64_invariants.conf:6.
51	  - Rule names are GLOBAL across the whole compiled wasm, so collisions are real. The corpus avoids them with manual prefixes: `fungible_after_transfer_total_supply_geq_zero` (fungible_invariants.rs:41) vs the unprefixed `after_transfer_total_supply_geq_zero` (vault_64_invariants.rs:49); `wt_` for weighted_threshold, `sl_` for spending_limit, `rwa_`, `enumerable_`, `nft_`. Pick a prefix convention up front.
52	  - One conf references a rule that does not exist in source: packages/tokens/confs/non_fungible_integrity.conf lists `nft_token_uri_injective`, which has no `#[rule] pub fn` anywhere in the tree. Confs and source drift and nothing checks them.
53	  - `cvlr_satisfy` is imported in 51 of the 55 spec files and invoked ZERO times (`grep -rn 'cvlr_satisfy!'` returns nothing). It is cargo-cult boilerplate in the use-statement; do not copy it.
54	  - packages/tokens/src/vault_64/specs/helpers.rs and vault_64_invariants.rs BOTH define `safe_assumptions`, `assume_pre_total_supply_geq_zero`, and `assert_post_total_supply_geq_zero` (helpers.rs:51-72 vs vault_64_invariants.rs:17-44). Different files import different copies: vault_64_integrity.rs:7 and vault_64_conversions.rs:7 use the invariants copy, vault_64_solvency.rs:11 uses the helpers copy. Silent divergence risk.
55	  - packages/tokens/src/fungible/specs/fungible_invariants.rs:110 places an assumption AFTER the mutating call: `Base::burn(&e, &from, amount);` then `assume_balance_leq_total_supply(&e, &from);` then the assert. Assuming a property of the POST-state can silently exclude exactly the counterexample the rule was meant to find. The file admits the underlying invariant is unprovable as written (`// helper assumption -- this is an invariant that we cannot prove without ghosts and hooks`, lines 12-13). Do not copy this.
56	  - `storage_setup_*` does not havoc storage — it defines one key with one arbitrary value. It cannot express 'this key may or may not be present'; forcing absence needs an explicit set-then-remove (ownable_non_panics.rs:74-79). The authors flag their own uncertainty about Option-valued keys at access_control_non_panics.rs:24-25.
57	  - Bounded nondet containers (MAX=5) interact with `loop_iter` and `optimistic_loop`. Confs use loop_iter 1-4, below the MAX=5 the builders can produce, and `optimistic_loop: true` ASSUMES AWAY every execution that would iterate past the bound. So container-length coverage is silently narrower than the nondet helper suggests. spending_limit_integrity.rs:27-30 states this explicitly: `// note we verify the rules in this file with: "loop_iter": 1 or 2, "optimistic_loop": true, meaning we consider only runs where the loops are iterated at most 1/2 times.`
58	  - Events are compiled OUT of the verified build (`#[cfg(not(feature = "certora"))] emit_paused(e);`, pausable/storage.rs:61). No rule in this corpus can observe or assert anything about emitted events.
59	  - The checked-in Cargo.lock is STALE with respect to the certora deps: it contains no `cvlr`, `cvlr-soroban`, `cvlr-soroban-macros` or `cvlr-soroban-derive` entry at all, and the `stellar-tokens` package entry (Cargo.lock:1944-1955) omits them even though packages/tokens/Cargo.toml:16-19 declares them unconditionally. `just build` will have to re-resolve and fetch the git dependency.
60	  - The math_rounding proof is about a DIFFERENT program than the one that ships: vault_64/specs/helpers.rs:17-26 comments out the real `virtual_offset = 10^decimals_offset` and hardcodes `let virtual_offset = 1_i64;`, and the file header says `// status: verified for a simplified version` (math_rounding.rs:9). Simplifications like this are invisible unless you read the helper.
61	  - packages/tokens/src/vault_64/specs/vault_64_solvency.rs declares the solvency invariant in a comment (`// invariant: effective total assets >= effective total supply`, line 17) and defines `assume_pre_solvency`, but contains ZERO `#[rule]`s and no conf mentions solvency. The headline safety property of the vault is assumed, never proved.
62	  - packages/tokens/src/rwa/specs/helpers/ghosts.rs is a verbatim copy of packages/accounts/src/smart_account/specs/ghosts.rs, self-labeled `// DUPLICATE CODE` (line 5), but with a behavioral divergence: the rwa `GhostMap::get` takes `&mut self` and MEMOIZES on first read (lines 83-98, `// notice this is stronger than what we had before`), while the accounts one takes `&self` and returns a fresh nondet each time. Two ghosts with the same name and different semantics.
63	  - packages/tokens/src/rwa/specs/helpers/nondet.rs:119-120 computes `let country_data = CountryData::nondet();` then discards it and pushes a separately-drawn `nondet()`. Dead binding; the loop is still sound but the code is misleading.
64	####################################################################################################
65	AREA: Redis 8.10 (v8.9.241) in-tree executable + documentary specification: the TCL test suite as an oracle, src/commands/*.json as a machine-readable command inventory, MANIFESTO/redis.conf/README as the documentary contract, and src/config.c as the authoritative list of semantics-altering knobs.
66	
67	SUMMARY: Redis already ships a large executable specification: ~29.2k lines of tests/unit, ~29.0k lines of tests/unit/type, ~13.2k of tests/integration, ~8.4k of tests/unit/cluster, plus 48 moduleapi suites. The DSL is small and spec-shaped: assert_equal/assert_error/assert_encoding/wait_for_condition (tests/support/test.tcl), plus three oracles that are directly reusable as specification predicates — `DEBUG DIGEST`/`debug_digest_value` (whole-dataset and per-key state equality, tests/support/util.tcl:1161-1173), `csvdump` (a canonical serialization of every key across 16 DBs, tests/support/util.tcl:496-570), and `attach_to_replication_stream`/`assert_replication_stream` (the exact command sequence the master emits, tests/test_helper.tcl:787-864). A real master/replica consistency checker already exists and is the closest thing in-tree to a linearizability harness: tests/integration/replication-psync.tcl runs 3 concurrent random-workload writers while repeatedly killing the replication link, then asserts `$master debug digest == $slave debug digest` across 20 config combinations (diskless sync × diskless load × rdbchannel); replication-3.tcl, replication-4.tcl and block-repl.tcl do the same for expires, multi-DB parallel writers and blocking list/zset ops respectively. Concurrency contracts are asserted explicitly: blocked-client FIFO fairness under pipelining (unit/type/list.tcl:2306), nested-unblock execution order verified through the replication stream (unit/type/list.tcl:2422), MULTI/EXEC propagated as an atomic `multi ... exec` block only when it contains ≥2 effective writes (unit/multi.tcl:398 and :412), and lazy expiry propagated as a bare `del` NOT wrapped in MULTI/EXEC (unit/expire.tcl:809, :830). src/commands/ holds 464 JSON files / 464 command entries (173 of them subcommands under 21 containers) with command_flags, key_specs (begin_search/find_keys), command_tips and a JSON-Schema `reply_schema` on 433 of them — and utils/req-res-log-validator.py already machine-checks every reply against its schema when the suite is run with `--force-resp3 --log-req-res`. The MANIFESTO states explicit non-guarantees (single-threaded core by design; multi-key API deliberately NOT made transparently distributed), and redis.conf documents the durability/consistency tradeoffs (`appendfsync`, `min-replicas-to-write` "does not GUARANTEE", `replica-serve-stale-data`, `cluster-allow-reads-when-down`). src/config.c defines 222 configs; roughly 40 of them change observable behavior rather than performance and must be fixed or quantified over by any spec.
68	
69	GOTCHAS:
70	  - DEBUG DIGEST is the suite's state-equality oracle but it is NOT a full state equality: csvdump (tests/support/util.tcl:496-570) covers only string/list/set/zset/hash/stream, and both digest helpers degrade to a constant dummy string when --ignore-digest is passed or the needs:debug tag is denied (tests/support/util.tcl:1161-1173). A spec that says 'states are equal' must say which projection of state it means.
71	  - The test suite does NOT run with default production settings. tests/assets/default.conf sets notify-keyspace-events KEA, propagation-error-behavior panic, save '' (RDB off), appendonly no, latency-monitor-threshold 1, repl-diskless-sync-delay 0, and enable-debug-command/enable-protected-configs/enable-module-command yes. Anything the suite pins is pinned under that config, not under the shipped redis.conf.
72	  - Redis's replication is asynchronous and there is no in-tree statement of a consistency model. The only executable consistency property is eventual digest equality after the workload STOPS and offsets converge (wait_for_ofs_sync, tests/support/util.tcl:168). No test asserts any property about intermediate replica states. A CVLR spec claiming linearizability of master+replica reads would be claiming something Redis never claims.
73	  - Expiry makes reads non-deterministic in a way that is role-dependent: on a master a read can delete the key and emit a DEL; on a read-only replica the same read reports the key as gone but leaves it stored (src/db.c:2973-2990). Worse, a command arriving FROM the master never sees keys as expired. Any 'GET after EXPIRE' spec must be parameterized by role and by whether the client is the master link.
74	  - MULTI/EXEC is isolation, not rollback. A command that fails at runtime inside EXEC does not abort the transaction; only queue-time errors do (tests/unit/multi.tcl:59-96, execCommandAbort in src/multi.c:110-125). And the atomic MULTI...EXEC wrapper only appears in the replication stream when >1 effective write occurred (tests/unit/multi.tcl:398 vs :412) — so 'transaction' means something different to a client than to a replica.
75	  - Blocking commands inside MULTI do not block: they return the empty/nil result immediately (tests/unit/multi.tcl:725-739). A spec must treat the 13 BLOCKING commands as having two distinct semantics depending on execution context (direct client vs EXEC vs Lua/FUNCTION).
76	  - Keyspace notifications are OFF by default in production (redis.conf:2235 notify-keyspace-events "") but ON in the test suite. Notification delivery is pubsub, i.e. fire-and-forget with no persistence and no delivery guarantee for disconnected subscribers; the tests only pin content and relative order for a connected subscriber.
77	  - maxmemory-policy is the biggest hidden branch in Redis semantics: under the 9 eviction policies, keys the client never deleted can vanish at any time, and the algorithms are explicitly approximate ('LRU, LFU, LRM and volatile-ttl are implemented using approximated randomized algorithms', redis.conf:1277-1279). A spec must either fix noeviction or admit arbitrary key loss as a legal step.
78	  - src/commands/README.md:3-9 says the JSON files are internal and third parties must use COMMAND INFO / COMMAND DOCS, which contain implicitly-populated fields not present in the JSON. If the spec's coverage matrix is generated from the raw JSON it will diverge from what the server actually reports.
79	  - 7 commands have INCOMPLETE key specs (GEORADIUS, GEORADIUSBYMEMBER, MIGRATE, XREAD, XREADGROUP) and 3 have VARIABLE_FLAGS (BITFIELD, DELEX, SET). For these, the JSON cannot tell you which keys are touched or how; you must call COMMAND GETKEYS or read the get_keys_function (e.g. setGetKeys, src/commands/set.json:9).
80	  - 31 of 464 command entries have no reply_schema, and reply_schema is declared immature and not externally exposed (src/commands/README.md:13-14). Container commands (e.g. object.json) carry almost no metadata at all — just summary/complexity/group/since/arity.
81	  - io-threads is IMMUTABLE_CONFIG (src/config.c:3554) and, when >1, moves protocol parsing off the main thread. Redis's single-threaded-execution assumption (MANIFESTO:72-73) therefore holds for command EXECUTION but not for I/O; tests/integration/replication-iothreads.tcl exists precisely because this changes timing-visible behavior.
82	  - This is a pre-release (8.10-RC2, v8.9.241). Several commands a spec might target are brand new and their semantics may still move: HIMPORT/compact hashes, LMOVEM/BLMOVEM, SUNIONCARD, SDIFFCARD, BACKUP, XREAD MAXCOUNT/MAXSIZE, plus in-tree-only features with no public docs (BLESS, GCRA, the ARRAY type, HOTKEYS).
83	  - tests/unit/cluster/ and tests/sentinel/ are not run by ./runtest — they use separate entry points (runtest-cluster, runtest-sentinel, tests/instances.tcl). Cluster-mode semantics (MOVED/ASK, CROSSSLOT, slot migration atomicity) are therefore a separate spec surface from the standalone one.
84	  - There is no in-tree linearizability checker, no Jepsen-style history recorder, and no fault-injection framework beyond `client kill` of the replication link, pause_process/resume_process (SIGSTOP/SIGCONT, tests/support/util.tcl:823-833) and DEBUG SLEEP/DEBUG QUICKLIST-PACKED-THRESHOLD style hooks. Any real concurrency property will need new machinery.
85	####################################################################################################
86	AREA: Concurrency and threading architecture of Redis 8.9.241 (/home/apanda/code/certora/redis/src), for CVLR spec authoring
87	
88	SUMMARY: This Redis is NOT the classic single-threaded server, nor the Redis 7.x "io-threads help with read/write" model. It runs N-1 additional IO threads (`io-threads` config, max 128, IMMUTABLE), each with its OWN full `aeEventLoop`, owning a partition of client connections: they accept socket reads, do full RESP parsing, command-table lookup, arity check and cluster key/slot extraction (`preprocessCommand`, server.c:4604), and socket writes including replication-stream compression. They never execute a command: `processInputBuffer` explicitly refuses and hands the client back to the main thread (networking.c:3927-3934). Plus 3 fixed `bio` worker threads (close-file, aof-fsync, lazy-free) and optionally module threads under a GIL. So: exactly 2 `pthread_create` sites in the server (iothread.c:1041, bio.c:172), and command execution + all keyspace mutation remains strictly single-threaded on the main thread. The user-visible atomic unit is the *execution unit* (`server.execution_nesting`, server.c:1420-1432): one top-level `call()`, one whole EXEC, or one whole script; replication/AOF propagation, module post-notification jobs and keyspace-invalidation flushes are deferred to unit exit (`postExecutionUnitOperationsEx`, server.c:4060) and multi-op units are wrapped in MULTI/EXEC on the wire (server.c:4010-4043). Blocking commands never block inside MULTI/EXEC/scripts (`CLIENT_DENY_BLOCKING`, multi.c:164, eval.c:246, functions.c:416) — they degrade to an immediate null reply — and woken blocked clients re-execute in their *own* separate execution unit after the waker's unit closed (server.c:5037-5039 → blocked.c:753). Two genuinely shared-memory hazards exist and are documented in-tree: (a) reply buffers can hold refcounted pointers into live keyspace `robj`s (copy-avoidance, `BULK_STR_REF`), which IO threads dereference at write time — async FLUSH must `pauseAllIOThreads()` and deep-copy (lazyfree.c:216-232); (b) `c->flags` CLIENT_DIRTY_CAS is written by the main thread while IO threads read other bits of the same word — suppressed with `REDIS_NO_SANITIZE("thread")` (multi.c:384-388, multi.c:439). A large user-visible surprise: FLUSHALL/FLUSHDB **SYNC** is silently converted into a blocking-ASYNC operation that empties the keyspace immediately but only replies after the bio lazy-free worker drains (db.c:1366-1392).
89	
90	GOTCHAS:
91	  - Do NOT write the spec as 'Redis is single-threaded'. In 8.9.241 there are up to 128 threads doing protocol work on client sockets, each with its own event loop. The correct invariant is narrower: *command execution and keyspace mutation* are single-threaded on the main thread. Everything else (parse, write, compression, fsync, close, object freeing, kvstore release) is off-main.
92	  - io-threads defaults to 1, so a default-configured server has 1 main + 3 bio = 4 threads and no IO threads at all (config.c:3554, `initThreadedIO` returns immediately at iothread.c:1026 when io_threads_num <= 1). A spec should be parameterized on io_threads_num, because several code paths (db.c:721 deferred RAW-string free, networking.c:5888 pending-command pool) behave DIFFERENTLY when io-threads > 1. This is an observable configuration-dependent semantic, not just a performance knob.
93	  - The atomic unit is NOT the command. It is the execution unit (server.execution_nesting). A single command that triggers a keyspace notification that triggers a module callback that calls RM_Call is ONE unit, and nothing propagates until the whole chain unwinds (server.c:4046-4059). Modeling atomicity per-command will produce wrong replication/observability predicates.
94	  - Script atomicity is conditionally broken. After busy-reply-threshold ms a script re-enters the event loop and other clients' commands execute BETWEEN the script's redis.call()s (script.c:158-167). A spec claiming 'scripts are atomic' is false for this source; the true statement is 'scripts are atomic with respect to write visibility on the replication stream (effects are buffered to unit exit) but NOT with respect to concurrent readers of the keyspace once the busy threshold is crossed'.
95	  - A partially-applied script cannot be killed (script.c:371-377 SCRIPT_WRITE_DIRTY ⇒ -UNKILLABLE). So there is no rollback primitive anywhere in Redis: neither EXEC nor EVAL can be undone once a write lands.
96	  - FLUSHALL SYNC does NOT mean 'reply after memory is reclaimed synchronously on the main thread'. db.c:1370-1373 silently upgrades it to ASYNC and blocks the client. The keyspace becomes empty immediately (observable by other clients) but the issuing client's +OK is deferred until the bio lazy-free queue drains. Inside MULTI/script/module the upgrade does NOT happen (CLIENT_AVOID_BLOCKING_ASYNC_FLUSH, server.h:483) — so FLUSHALL SYNC has two different reply-timing behaviours depending on context.
97	  - Blocking commands silently change semantics inside MULTI/script: BLPOP with timeout 0 returns a null array instead of blocking forever (t_list.c:1364-1368). WAIT returns the current ack count rather than waiting (replication.c:4912). This is a real correctness trap for a spec: the same command has two contracts.
98	  - WATCH is key-name-based, not value-based. A write that sets a key back to its original value still invalidates the WATCH (touchWatchedKey never inspects values, multi.c:388-425). But there IS one exemption that breaks naive models: a key that was already logically expired when WATCH ran does NOT dirty the CAS when it is later physically deleted (multi.c:397-406, and the wk->expired bit set at multi.c:330).
99	  - EXEC has three distinct outcomes that must not be conflated: -EXECABORT (queue-time error, CLIENT_DIRTY_EXEC), RESP null array (WATCH invalidated, CLIENT_DIRTY_CAS), and a normal array where individual elements may be errors (runtime errors do NOT abort). multi.c:148-158 vs multi.c:184-238.
100	  - handleClientsBlockedOnKey unconditionally calls listRotateHeadToTail (blocked.c:645) BEFORE checking whether the client's blocked type matches the key's current type. The header comment at blocked.c:337-345 still describes an older 'mismatching clients are moved to the other side of the list' mechanism that no longer literally exists in the code. Trust the code (bounded pass of listLength() iterations, rotate-then-test), not that comment, when specifying fairness.
101	  - Non-main threads DO touch memory that was keyspace memory: the bio lazy-free worker calls kvstoreRelease/decrRefCount on detached kvstores and objects (lazyfree.c:33-50). The safety argument is temporal (main thread substituted a fresh structure first), not lock-based. And IO threads dereference LIVE keyspace robj values when writing copy-avoidance replies, which is why async flush must pauseAllIOThreads() and deep-copy (lazyfree.c:216-232). 'Only the main thread touches the keyspace' is therefore too strong as stated; the precise invariant is 'only the main thread MUTATES the keyspace, and any object an off-main thread may still read is kept alive by a refcount taken on the main thread'.
102	  - atomicIncr/atomicGet/atomicSet are memory_order_relaxed (atomicvar.h:100-110). Only atomicGetWithSync/atomicSetWithSync are seq_cst. So INFO counters gathered across threads (stat_net_repl_output_bytes, lazyfree_objects, io_reads_processed) carry no cross-thread ordering guarantee and should not be used as synchronization or as a basis for any happens-before claim in a spec.
103	  - The IO-thread pause handshake is a BUSY-WAIT spin on both sides (iothread.c:432-436 and 474-476) and asserts it is not called reentrantly on the underlying state (iothread.c:422), even though the refcount PausedIOThreads allows nesting. Any spec of CLIENT LIST, CONFIG SET, client eviction, or async flush must model these as globally-quiescing operations.
104	  - processClientsFromIOThread is explicitly documented as re-entrant (iothread.c:597-602): a script that calls processEventsWhileBlocked can re-enter it for the same IO thread. It defends by popping exactly one client per loop iteration. Any model that treats the main-thread drain as a non-overlapping critical section is wrong.
105	  - Only 13 commands are allowed while a script/module is busy (grep ALLOW_BUSY in src/commands/: discard, auth, hello, unwatch, function-kill, script-kill, multi, quit, function-stats, reset, shutdown, replconf, watch). Note MULTI, WATCH, UNWATCH and DISCARD are allowed but EXEC is NOT — a transaction can be opened and queued during a busy script but cannot be committed until it ends.
106	  - cluster_asm.c (Atomic Slot Migration) is a 3942-line subsystem that combines fork, bio lazy-free, forced client pinning to the main thread, a dedicated BLOCKED_POSTPONE_TRIM state, and wholesale WATCH invalidation over a slot range. If the spec covers cluster mode at all, this is a much larger concurrency surface than anything in classic Redis and needs its own pass.
107	####################################################################################################
108	AREA: Redis 8.9.241 durability / replication / cluster guarantees as user-visible contracts, plus the command-dispatch and effect-propagation pipeline (src/server.c call(), aof.c, replication.c, cluster*.c, pubsub.c, notify.c, tracking.c, commands/*.json)
109	
110	SUMMARY: Every user-visible effect in Redis flows through one funnel: `call()` runs `c->cmd->proc(c)` (server.c:4235), which writes the client reply into an in-memory buffer and fires keyspace notifications *inline*, then `call()` decides propagation purely from the delta of the global `server.dirty` counter (server.c:4180, 4257, 4356) and queues the command (or the command's rewritten/effect form) via `alsoPropagateEx()`. At the end of the top-level execution unit, `propagatePendingCommands()` flushes the queue: it wraps >1 op in MULTI/EXEC (server.c:4021/4040) and calls `propagateNow()`, which feeds the AOF buffer first and the replication buffer second (server.c:3817-3821). Nothing is on disk or on the wire yet: `beforeSleep()` then does `flushAppendOnlyFile(0)` at server.c:2070 *before* `handleClientsWithPendingWrites()` at server.c:2117 — that ordering is the entire durability contract, and it only means "fsynced before reply" under `appendfsync always`; under the default `everysec` (config.c:3539) the reply can precede the fsync by up to ~1s, and up to ~2s more when the flush is postponed behind a busy bio fsync (aof.c:1448-1460). Replication is unconditionally asynchronous — `replicationFeedSlaves()` only appends to a buffer, no ACK is awaited — so any acknowledged write can be lost on failover; `WAIT` (replication.c:4894) counts replicas whose `repl_ack_off` reached the client's `c->woff`, which means *applied to the replica's memory*, not fsynced and not "will survive election", while `WAITAOF` (replication.c:4928) uses the separate `repl_aof_off`/`fsynced_reploff` fsync offsets. Cluster is legacy-only (`cluster.o cluster_asm.o cluster_legacy.o` in Makefile:404, no second mechanism), but this version adds a genuinely new file, `cluster_asm.c` (159KB, "Atomic slot migration"), which replaces per-key MIGRATE resharding with a fork+snapshot+stream+write-pause handoff (`pauseActions(PAUSE_DURING_SLOT_HANDOFF, LLONG_MAX, PAUSE_ACTIONS_CLIENT_WRITE_SET)` at cluster_legacy.c:6697) and introduces two new user-visible errors (`-TRYAGAIN Slot is being trimmed`, cluster.c:1543). The 464 files in src/commands/ are a substantial machine-readable partial spec (442 have command_flags, 433 reply_schema, 240 key_specs, 110 command_tips) but they describe only success replies and key positions — no error conditions, no state transitions, no cross-command invariants.
111	
112	GOTCHAS:
113	  - 'Durability' in Redis is a property of the EVENT LOOP, not of the command. The guarantee is purely positional: flushAppendOnlyFile runs at server.c:2070 and handleClientsWithPendingWrites at server.c:2117. A CVLR spec must model the beforeSleep boundary as the atomic commit point, not the return of the command function.
114	  - server.dirty is a hand-maintained global counter, not a derived quantity. A command that mutates state without incrementing it is silently not replicated and not persisted; a command that increments it spuriously is replicated verbatim. Any spec of 'writes are durable/replicated' is really a spec about ~135 scattered increments. This is the single highest-value invariant to formalize: (keyspace changed) <=> (server.dirty delta > 0).
115	  - appendfsync=always does NOT mean 'fsync per command' — it means 'fsync per event-loop iteration before any reply is written'. Pipelined commands are batched into one fsync. The guarantee is still sound (no reply precedes its fsync) but the fsync count is not 1:1 with writes.
116	  - The everysec window is not 1 second. With a slow disk it is up to ~1s of fsync interval PLUS up to 2s of write postponement (aof.c:1443-1461), and it is UNBOUNDED while no-appendfsync-on-rewrite=yes and a child is active (aof.c:1583).
117	  - WAIT's return value is counted over slaves with replstate == SLAVE_STATE_ONLINE only (replication.c:4866). A replica that acked and then disconnected still 'counted' at the moment WAIT returned, and a replica that is syncing is invisible. WAIT therefore guarantees nothing about the set of replicas eligible to be elected.
118	  - WAIT and WAITAOF use c->woff, the offset after the client's LAST PROPAGATING command (server.c:4438). If the client's last write did not propagate (not dirty, or prevented), c->woff is stale and WAIT trivially succeeds against an older offset.
119	  - The replica's ACK offset advances only after execution (networking.c:3609-3614) and is skipped entirely while the replica's master client is blocked. During the ASM trim-block (server.c:4938) the master client is deliberately blocked, so ACK offsets freeze and WAIT can stall without any error.
120	  - CROSSSLOT is enforced per slot, not per node, and is computed over ALL commands in a MULTI (server.c:5037+). A MULTI containing two single-key commands whose keys hash differently is rejected as a whole, even on a single-node-per-shard cluster.

120	  - CROSSSLOT is enforced per slot, not per node, and is computed over ALL commands in a MULTI (server.c:5037+). A MULTI containing two single-key commands whose keys hash differently is rejected as a whole, even on a single-node-per-shard cluster.
121	  - -TRYAGAIN has two distinct meanings now: 'Multiple keys request during rehashing of slot' (classic resharding, cluster.c:1526) and 'Slot is being trimmed' (ASM aftermath, cluster.c:1543). A client retry loop must treat both, and the second one can persist for the duration of a background trim job.
122	  - Atomic slot migration pauses ALL client writes on the source (PAUSE_ACTIONS_CLIENT_WRITE_SET = WRITE|EXPIRE|EVICT|REPLICA) for up to asm-write-pause-timeout, with LLONG_MAX as the nominal deadline (cluster_legacy.c:6697). Paused clients are postponed, not errored — a write can block silently for seconds. Expiration and eviction are also frozen, so TTLs appear to stretch.
123	  - Keyspace notifications are NOT part of the write's atomic effect. They are emitted inside proc() (before propagation) and are never replicated. A subscriber can observe an event for a write that is then lost on crash or failover; conversely a replica emits its own event for the same logical write, so an application consuming notifications from both master and replica sees duplicates with different timing.
124	  - Notifications are silently suppressed when there are zero subscribers (notify.c:161-163) — so 'a notification is produced' is conditional on subscriber existence, which makes any 'every write emits an event' spec false as written.
125	  - Pub/Sub 'at-most-once' is worse than it sounds: a subscriber exceeding client-output-buffer-limit pubsub is freed asynchronously mid-delivery (networking.c:5527), so a subscriber can receive a PREFIX of a message stream and then a disconnect, with no gap indication.
126	  - In a single PUBLISH, exact-channel subscribers are served before pattern subscribers (pubsub.c:703 vs 729). A client subscribed both ways to the same channel receives 'message' before 'pmessage'. Shard pub/sub ignores patterns entirely.
127	  - CSC invalidations fire for non-write reasons (tracking-table eviction under memory pressure, tracking.c:517-541) and FLUSHALL sends a single RESP NULL meaning 'all keys'. A spec of 'invalidation implies the key changed' is false; only the converse holds.
128	  - A RESP2 client with CLIENT TRACKING on and no REDIRECT receives NOTHING — sendTrackingMessage falls through to `goto done` (tracking.c:305-310). The feature silently no-ops.
129	  - MULTI/EXEC has no rollback. Only queue-time errors abort. Per-sub-command ACL is re-checked at EXEC time and a denial produces a NOPERM element inside the reply array while the other sub-commands still execute (multi.c execCommand).
130	  - EXEC never appears in the replication stream. What replicas see is propagatePendingCommands' MULTI/EXEC wrapper around whatever effects happened to be queued — which may be fewer, more, or different commands than the client queued.
131	  - On a read-only replica, a logically expired key is hidden from clients but still physically present (db.c:2804-2808, 3045). DBSIZE, RANDOMKEY, SCAN and memory accounting see it; GET does not. This is a genuine master/replica observable divergence that a spec must model as two different 'exists' predicates.
132	  - Losing a slot deletes its keys locally (cluster_legacy.c:2543-2547). From a client's view, a MOVED redirection and a mass key deletion are the same event observed from two nodes.
133	  - commands/*.json is explicitly disclaimed as non-authoritative (commands/README.md): fields are implicitly populated during codegen, so the JSON is a SOURCE for commands.def, not a mirror of runtime behavior. Anything generated from it should be cross-checked against COMMAND INFO / COMMAND DOCS output, and 31 commands have key extraction only in C (get_keys_function).
134	  - reply_schema covers success replies only — 433/464 files have one, and none enumerate error conditions. So the JSONs can seed a spec's TYPE signature and key-effect matrix but cannot supply preconditions, error contracts, or any cross-command invariant.
135	####################################################################################################
136	AREA: Redis 8.9.241 (/home/apanda/code/certora/redis) — user-visible keyspace data model, object model, key lifecycle, expiration, eviction, and per-type command semantics; the abstract state a CVLR spec must model.
137	
138	SUMMARY: This tree is NOT stock open-source Redis 7.x: the value stored under a key is a `kvobj` (`struct redisObject` with `iskvobj=1`) that *embeds the key string and the expire timestamp as object metadata*, and `db->keys`/`db->expires` are `kvstore`s (slot-partitioned dict arrays) that hold pointers to the *same* kvobj (object.h:31-66, object.h:103-116, server.h:1250-1266, db.c:2783-2825). That makes two classic invariants structural rather than asserted: `expires ⊆ keys` holds by pointer identity, and `kv ∈ db->expires ⟺ kvobjGetExpire(kv) != -1` is maintained jointly by setExpire/removeExpire. There are 16 logical DBs by default, forced to 1 under cluster mode, selected per-client via `c->db` (config.c:3552, config.c:706-709, db.c:1182-1187). The single most important user-visible fact for a spec is that **"a key whose TTL is in the past is never observed" is FALSE as stated**: expiry is enforced only on the `lookupKey*` path (db.c:296-354), so DBSIZE, INFO keyspace, DEBUG OBJECT, DEBUG DIGEST, RANDOMKEY-after-100-tries, and HLEN all can and do report logically-expired keys/fields (db.c:3148-3150, server.c:7275-7282, debug.c:688-694, debug.c:329-345, db.c:855-865, t_hash.c:5602 + tests/unit/type/hash-field-expire.tcl:454). Expiry is also *client-role dependent* (a replica hides but does not delete; a CLIENT_MASTER client sees expired keys as valid) and *time-frozen per execution unit* via `server.cmd_time_snapshot` (db.c:3004-3072, server.c:262-276, server.c:1420-1428). Eviction is genuine user-visible nondeterminism: `performEvictions()` runs *before* each command in `processCommand` and may delete arbitrary sampled keys, and returning EVICT_FAIL rejects any CMD_DENYOOM command with `-OOM` (server.c:4848-4871, evict.c:543-833). Encodings (object.h:76-91) are supposed to be invisible except through OBJECT ENCODING / DEBUG, but three leak: LISTPACK_EX orders hash fields by TTL rather than insertion (t_hash.c:1596-1609), hash template encodings TMPL_LP/TMPL_ARRAY are new and opt-in, and this build adds OBJ_ARRAY (type name "array") as a seventh user-visible type (db.c:1776-1788).
139	
140	GOTCHAS:
141	  - This is NOT stock Redis 7/8 OSS — src/version.h says 8.9.241 and the tree has a Redis-Ltd/Valkey-contributor hybrid license header. Do not write a spec from memory of upstream Redis. The value object embeds the key AND the TTL (kvobj, object.h:31-66); db->keys and db->expires are kvstores holding the SAME pointers; there are new types (OBJ_ARRAY), new encodings (TMPL_LP, TMPL_ARRAY, SLICED_ARRAY, LISTPACK_EX), new policies (volatile-lrm, allkeys-lrm), and new commands (DELEX, INCREX, MSETEX, SET IFEQ/IFDEQ, BLESS, HSETEX/HGETEX/HGETDEL, AR* array family).
142	  - "A key with a TTL in the past is never observed by a client" is FALSE as an unconditional invariant. It holds ONLY for the lookupKey* path. It is violated by DBSIZE (db.c:3148), INFO keyspace (server.c:7275-7277), DEBUG OBJECT (debug.c:688), DEBUG DIGEST (debug.c:329-345), RANDOMKEY after 100 tries (db.c:855-865), HLEN for hash fields (t_hash.c:5602), and everywhere when DEBUG SET-ALLOW-ACCESS-EXPIRED 1 is set (db.c:2948). The correct spec-level statement is: 'no command that reaches its key through lookupKey*() returns the value of a key whose expire < commandTimeSnapshot()'.
143	  - Two different expiry comparisons coexist. Lazy: `now > when` (db.c:2955, strict). Active: `now < expire → not expired` i.e. `now >= expire` (expire.c:41). Hash field: `expiredAt >= now → valid` i.e. expires when `expiredAt < now` (t_hash.c:2118-2120). RDB load drop on master: `expiretime < now` (rdb.c:4986). A spec that picks one comparison will disagree with at least one code path at exactly t == expire.
144	  - Expiry is a function of WHO is asking, not just of the state. Same key, same clock: a normal client on a master sees it gone (and it is deleted); a normal client on a replica sees it gone (but it is NOT deleted); the master replication link client sees it as VALID; a client during loading sees it as valid. Any spec of 'exists' must be parameterized by (role, client flags, loading, PAUSE_ACTION_EXPIRE) — see db.c:3004-3055.
145	  - Encoding is NOT a pure function of the current contents. Quicklist→listpack uses HALF the threshold when shrinking (t_list.c:80-83), so two lists with identical contents can have different encodings depending on history. Similarly a set that went intset→HT never returns to intset via the add path. Any invariant of the form 'encoding(o) = f(contents(o))' must be weakened to a one-sided bound: listpack/intset ⇒ contents are within the thresholds, but not the converse.
146	  - LISTPACK_EX orders hash fields by TTL, not insertion order (t_hash.c:1605-1608). HGETALL/HRANDFIELD field ordering therefore changes observably the moment the first HEXPIRE is issued. Field order in hashes is nominally unspecified in Redis, but if the spec asserts anything about ordering or about HRANDFIELD distribution, HFE breaks it.
147	  - Eviction is not an optional background detail: performEvictions() runs synchronously in processCommand BEFORE every command when maxmemory != 0 (server.c:4848), can delete arbitrary keys across ALL databases (not just the client's), and its victim choice depends on a pool that PERSISTS across calls (evict.c:589 `struct evictionPoolEntry *pool = EvictionPoolLRU;`). A spec either sets maxmemory=0 to exclude it, or must model it as nondeterministic environment-driven deletion between commands.
148	  - A read command can be a write. SCAN calls expireIfNeeded which deletes and propagates DELs on a master (db.c:1725); GET lazily expires; plain lookups mutate LRU/LFU counters observable via OBJECT IDLETIME/FREQ (db.c:327-343). 'Read-only' in the command table means 'does not modify user data', not 'does not modify state'.
149	  - dbSetValue has an in-place mutation fast path that swaps type/encoding/ptr inside the OLD robj when both refcounts are 1 and neither is EMBSTR (db.c:653-673). Do not model a value write as 'allocate new object, replace pointer' — object identity may or may not be preserved, and lazyfree / IO-thread deferred free may free the displaced object later.
150	  - OBJECT REFCOUNT is not the classic shared-integer signal here. kvobjSet always builds a private kvobj for the keyspace (copying the INT pointer, dup'ing RAW strings), so shared.integers[] objects never land in db->keys, and OBJ_SHARED_REFCOUNT is 8388607 (2^23-1), not 2147483647 (object.h:98). Verify empirically before asserting anything about refcounts.
151	  - src/chk.c is a CuckooHeavyKeeper top-K sketch for the hotkeys feature, not a consistency checker. The only in-tree structural self-check is the KEYSIZES/alloc-size histogram assertion in db.c:161-263, enabled by DEBUG KEYSIZES-HIST-ASSERT 1 / DEBUG ALLOCSIZE-SLOTS-ASSERT 1. There is no existing 'verify the keyspace is internally consistent' routine to reuse.
152	  - SET's expire argument and EXPIRE's argument have different validity domains: SET rejects TTL<=0 with an error (t_string.c:250-255) while EXPIRE accepts a negative/past time and deletes the key replying 1 (expire.c:817-836). GETEX with a past EXAT/PXAT also deletes. A single 'setTTL' abstraction across these commands will be wrong.
153	  - DEL of a logically expired key returns 0 on a master but 1 on a writable replica, because delGenericCommand calls expireIfNeeded with flags=0 (not LOOKUP_WRITE) and only skips counting on KEY_DELETED, which replicas never return (db.c:1442 + db.c:3044).
154	  - Empty-collection deletion uses TWO different helpers — dbDelete and dbDeleteSkipKeysizesUpdate — and picking the wrong one silently corrupts the KEYSIZES histogram (bin #0 decremented twice). If the spec models the histogram as part of observable state (INFO keysizes), this distinction matters; if not, treat both as the same abstract delete.
155

2	NAME: Catalog-first: a numbered CVLR property catalog over an explicit abstract Redis state machine, with the scheduler reified into the state | effort: large | confidence: medium
