4	SKETCH: DELIVERABLE 1 (the artifact): `/home/apanda/code/certora/redis-cvlr/PROPERTIES.md` — a numbered catalog in the stellar-contracts header style (verified format at packages/access/src/ownable/specs/ownable_invariants.rs:11-13: `// property: P-04. Ownable-Invariants.` / `// description:` / `// status:`). Each entry carries: ID, one-sentence English statement, CVLR form (integrity | invariant | panic | non-panic | *interleaving* | satisfy | NOT-EXPRESSIBLE), the redis file:line evidence, the scope caveats (role, caller kind, maxmemory, cluster), and `status: unproven`. This is reviewable by a Redis engineer who never reads Rust, because every entry is anchored to cited C. Note stellar has NO properties document at all — 41 of 55 headers say the placeholder `P-XX` — so this is the piece that corpus is missing, not a copy of it.
5	
6	DELIVERABLE 2 (the encoding): `redis-cvlr/src/model/` — an explicit abstract state machine in Rust, and `redis-cvlr/src/specs/` — one `#[rule]` fn per (property x command) pair.
7	
8	THE ABSTRACT STATE. Bounded and flat (forced: cvlr-spec's macro_rules take the context as `$ctx: ident`, verified cvlr-spec/src/macros.rs:51,129 — no generics, no paths):
9	```rust
10	pub struct RedisState {
11	  stored:  [Slot; K],        // PHYSICAL keyspace: present, type, val, expire_at
12	  now:     u64,              // frozen per execution unit (server.c:1420-1432)
13	  role:    Role,             // Master | ReadOnlyReplica
14	  caller:  Caller,           // Normal | MasterLink | AofLoader
15	  watch:   [WatchEntry; C],  // per-client watched set, dirty_cas, expired_at_watch
16	  blocked: [BlockedEntry; C],// btype, key, block_seq  (FIFO witness)
17	  replog:  OpLog,            // the PROPAGATED command stream, bounded
18	  nesting: u32,
19	}
20	```
21	Two design decisions carry the whole strategy.
22	(a) `stored` is physical, and *visibility* is a derived function `visible(s,k,caller)` — not a field. Forced by evidence: DBSIZE returns `kvstoreSize(db->keys)` with zero expiry filtering (I read db.c:3148-3174 myself: the only filter is cluster slot ownership), while the lookup path filters via expireIfNeeded (db.c:3004-3072). A model with one `HashMap<Key,Value>` cannot state P-02 at all.
23	(b) `replog` makes the *externally observable effect stream* part of the state. This is what turns atomicity from an untestable adjective into a state predicate, and it is exactly what Redis's own suite already captures via `attach_to_replication_stream`/`assert_replication_stream` (tests/test_helper.tcl:787-864) — so every replog property has a free differential twin.
24	
25	THE RULE HARNESS. The survey calls `cvlr_rules!` "UNUSABLE out of the box." That is an overstatement and I can disprove it: `CvlrSpec::assume_requires`/`check_ensures` are ordinary public trait methods (I read cvlr-spec/src/spec.rs:32-54, 63-78), and the three-token contract is visible in the test stub at cvlr-spec/tests/test_spec.rs:1138. You supply this once, ~12 lines:
26	```rust
27	macro_rules! cvlr_impl_rule {
28	  {$name:ident, $spec:expr, $base:ident} => {
29	    #[rule] pub fn $name() {
30	      let spec = $spec;
31	      let pre  = RedisState::nondet();
32	      spec.assume_requires(&pre);
33	      let mut post = pre.clone();
34	      $base(&mut post);
35	      spec.check_ensures(&post, &pre);   // -> assert_with_states(post, pre)
36	    }
37	  };
38	}
39	```
40	Argument order verified end to end: check_ensures(post, old) calls assert_with_states(post_state, old) (spec.rs:63-78), and `cvlr_def_states_predicate!{ pred P([a,b]: Ctx) }` binds a=ctx0=post, b=ctx1=pre (macros.rs:131-135).
41	
42	EIGHT RULES, FOUR OF THEM CONCURRENT.
43	
44	P-01 String-Integrity (two-state, sequential). Baseline.
45	```rust
46	cvlr_def_states_predicate! { pred SetEstablishes([post, pre]: RedisState) {
47	  { let k = post.arg_key; post.stored[k].present && post.stored[k].val == post.arg_val };
48	  { post.unrelated_keys_equal(&pre) }
49	}}
50	cvlr_rules!{ name: "string_integrity", spec: cvlr_spec!{ensures: SetEstablishes}, bases:[base_set, base_setex, base_getset] }
51	```
52	
53	P-02 Expiry-Visibility-Is-Role-Dependent (two-state, parameterized). The catalog's flagship correction to folklore: "a key past its TTL is never observed" is FALSE as stated. It holds only for the lookup path. Statement: for caller=Normal on a Master, `visible(post,k)==false` AND `post.stored[k].present==false`; on a ReadOnlyReplica, `visible==false` but `stored[k].present==true`; for caller=MasterLink, `visible==true` even past the TTL. Evidence I read directly: db.c:3043-3045 (`if (server.current_client && (flags & CLIENT_MASTER)) return KEY_VALID; if (server.masterhost != NULL && !(flags & EXPIRE_FORCE_DELETE_EXPIRED)) return KEY_EXPIRED;`) and db.c:2953 (`return now > when;`, strict). CORRECTION TO THE SURVEY: its account of expireIfNeeded lists only the replica and CLIENT_MASTER escapes; reading db.c:3049-3062 there are at least three more paths by which a MASTER returns KEY_EXPIRED without deleting — `!confAllowsExpireDel()` (gated on the `lazyexpire-nested-arbitrary-keys` config), the `EXPIRE_AVOID_DELETE_EXPIRED` flag, and `isPausedActionsWithUpdate(PAUSE_ACTION_EXPIRE)`. P-02 must be conditioned on all five or it is false on a master.
54	
55	P-03 No-Empty-Aggregate (single-state invariant, one rule per shrinking command). `cvlr_invariant_rules!{ invariant: NoEmptyAggregate, bases:[base_lpop, base_srem, base_zrem, base_hdel, base_ltrim] }`. Must be a SINGLE-state predicate — `cvlr_invar_spec`'s check_ensures calls `self.1.assert(post)` (spec.rs:158-161) and a states-predicate's `assert()` is poisoned with `cvlr_assert!(false); panic!()` (macros.rs:155-166, read directly). Handing it a two-state predicate fails unconditionally.
56	
57	P-04 [CONCURRENT] Execution-Unit-Atomicity. The atomic unit visible to a replica is the execution unit, not the command, and it is MULTI-framed iff it emitted >=2 ops and the direct command is not TOUCHES_ARBITRARY_KEYS. I read server.c:4005 (`transaction_target = numops > 1 ? targets : PROPAGATE_NONE`), 4014-4019 (the TOUCHES_ARBITRARY_KEYS escape) and 4021/4040 (the MULTI/EXEC bracketing); the only two such commands are SCAN and RANDOMKEY (`grep -l TOUCHES_ARBITRARY_KEYS commands/*.json`).
58	```rust
59	cvlr_def_states_predicate! { pred UnitFramedIffMultiOp([post, pre]: RedisState) {
60	  { let n = post.replog.len() - pre.replog.len();
61	    let framed = post.replog.tail_is_multi_exec(n);
62	    framed == (n > 1 && !post.last_cmd_touches_arbitrary_keys) }
63	}}
64	```
65	bases: [base_exec_txn, base_eval_script, base_spop_with_count, base_scan_lazy_expire, base_randomkey_lazy_expire]. The last two are the interesting ones: they must emit *bare, unwrapped* DELs, pinned by tests/unit/expire.tcl:809 and :830.
66	
67	P-05 [CONCURRENT] WATCH-Is-Value-Blind-And-Complete. Two obligations. Completeness: any interleaved step that calls keyModified on a watched key forces EXEC to abort. Value-blindness: this holds even when the step restores the original value. I confirmed value-blindness by reading multi.c:389-425 — `touchWatchedKey` never inspects the value; it only takes the key name.
68	```rust
69	#[rule] pub fn p05_watch_invalidation_is_value_blind() {
70	  let mut s = RedisState::nondet();
71	  let (w, o) = (nondet_client(), nondet_client()); cvlr_assume!(w != o);
72	  let k = nondet_key();
73	  watch(&mut s, w, k);
74	  let pre = s.clone();
75	  let step = Step::nondet(); cvlr_assume!(step.client == o);
76	  apply_step(&mut s, step);
77	  cvlr_assume!(s.stored[k] == pre.stored[k]);          // value RESTORED
78	  let r = exec(&mut s, w);
79	  cvlr_assert_if!(step.touches_key(k), r == ExecResult::AbortedNullArray);
80	}
81	```
82	The deliberate `cvlr_assume!` of value equality is the point: it makes the rule state something a value-comparison CAS model would get wrong. This rule is also the one that would target what looks like a real defect — multi.c:415-417, inside the `wk->expired` branch, `break`s out of the whole watcher list rather than `continue`ing, so when a key that was already logically expired at WATCH time is later overwritten (and still present), watchers positioned after that entry appear never to receive CLIENT_DIRTY_CAS. I have not constructed the end-to-end scenario, so I flag this as suspicious, not confirmed.
83	
84	P-06 [CONCURRENT] Blocked-Client-FIFO. Among waiters on k whose btype matches the resulting object type, the earliest-blocked is served first in one wakeup pass, and the pass serves at most `listLength(clients)` waiters. Verified against blocked.c:620-677 read in full, including `long count = listLength(clients)`, `listRotateHeadToTail`, and the match condition at 658-670 (`native_match || BLOCKED_MODULE || unblock_on_nokey`). The type condition is load-bearing: without it the property is simply false, and that is precisely the kind of thing catalog review catches before any prover runs.
85	```rust
86	#[rule] pub fn p06_wakeup_is_fifo_among_type_matching_waiters() {
87	  let mut s = RedisState::nondet();
88	  let k = nondet_key(); let (a,b) = (nondet_client(), nondet_client());
89	  cvlr_assume!(a != b);
90	  cvlr_assume!(s.blocked[a].on(k) && s.blocked[b].on(k));
91	  cvlr_assume!(s.blocked[a].seq < s.blocked[b].seq);
92	  cvlr_assume!(s.blocked[a].btype == BType::List && s.blocked[b].btype == BType::List);
93	  push_then_run_wakeup_pass(&mut s, k, /*elems=*/1);
94	  cvlr_assert!(!s.blocked[a].is_blocked());
95	  cvlr_assert!( s.blocked[b].is_blocked());
96	}
97	```
98	
99	P-07 [CONCURRENT, permitted anomaly] Replica-Read-Divergence-Is-Reachable. Stated with `cvlr_satisfy!` rather than assert: there exists a reachable state where the same key is absent to a normal client on both master and replica, yet DBSIZE differs, because the master's read deleted it and the replica's did not (db.c:3045 vs db.c:3066). Catalogs that only contain "nothing bad happens" entries mislead; permitted-anomaly entries are what make a spec usable by an application author. Honest caveat: `cvlr_satisfy!` has ZERO invocations across all of stellar-contracts (I counted: 51 files import it, 0 call it), so there is no local precedent for how the Prover reports satisfy outcomes.
100	
101	P-08 Eviction-Is-An-Environment-Not-A-Postcondition. performEvictions runs *before every command* inside processCommand when maxmemory != 0 (I read server.c:4848-4866), so it cannot be a postcondition of anything. It becomes a nondeterministic pre-step in every rule's harness: `maybe_evict(&mut s)`. The statable property is "eviction only removes keys and never alters a surviving key's type or value; under noeviction it removes nothing." "Eviction picks the LRU key" is NOT statable — redis.conf:1277-1279 says the algorithms are approximated randomized.
102	
103	CATEGORIES. Five, extending stellar's four (verified as the actual corpus taxonomy): `_integrity`, `_invariants`, `_panics`, `_non_panics`, plus the new `_interleaving`. The last is the contribution: CVLR has no concurrency vocabulary whatsoever, so the schedule is reified into `RedisState` and each interleaving rule quantifies over a bounded number of nondet `Step`s by nondet clients.
104	
105	VERIFIES:
106	  + Per-command functional correctness against an explicit abstract state (P-01 class): what SET/GET/INCR/LPUSH establish, and that unrelated keys are unchanged — the stellar `_integrity` shape, two-state via `cvlr_def_states_predicate!`.
107	  + Structural invariants over the keyspace (P-03 class): no empty aggregate is reachable; `kv in db->expires <=> kvobjGetExpire(kv) != -1`; type/encoding well-formedness. One `#[rule]` per (invariant x mutating command) pair, as `cvlr_invariant_rules!` with a single-state predicate.
108	  + The expiry visibility split (P-02): that the model's `visible()` and `stored` genuinely diverge, and diverge in exactly the role-, caller- and config-dependent way db.c:3004-3072 prescribes. Highest-value sequential property, because it is the one practitioners get wrong.
109	  + Bounded-schedule safety for four concurrency properties: execution-unit MULTI framing (P-04), WATCH completeness + value-blindness (P-05), FIFO wakeup among type-matching waiters (P-06), and reachability of the master/replica read anomaly (P-07). Bounded means: fixed K keys, C clients, N interleaved steps.
110	  + Consistency of the catalog with itself: because every property is a predicate over one shared `RedisState`, contradictory entries fail to compile or fail vacuity. That alone catches a class of spec error that prose catalogs never catch.
111	  + Vacuity of each rule: `#[rule]` appends `cvlr_vacuity_check!()` (verified cvlr-macros/src/lib.rs:20-36), so an over-assumed rule is detectable — important here because the interleaving harness is assumption-heavy.
112	
113	DOES NOT:
114	  - ANY property of redis/src/*.c. CVLR is Rust-only: its entire vocabulary is `extern "C" CVT_*` symbols with `link(wasm_import_module="env")` (cvlr-asserts/src/core.rs:1-12) and its README says Solana and Soroban. There is no C or LLVM-bitcode frontend anywhere in the cvlr tree. Everything proven is proven about a hand-written Rust model. This sentence belongs on page one of PROPERTIES.md.
115	  - All liveness. CVLR has assert/assume/satisfy over one terminating path, no temporal operator, no fairness, no 'eventually'. So 'a blocked BLPOP is eventually woken', 'WAIT eventually returns', 'master and replica digests eventually converge', 'an unkillable script eventually ends' are NOT-EXPRESSIBLE and must be catalogued as such and delegated to the tcl suite (tests/integration/replication-psync.tcl:116-128 asserts convergence only after quiescence, via wait_for_condition — Redis itself only ever states it as liveness-after-quiescence).
116	  - Real linearizability. You cannot quantify over all schedules — only over N nondet steps with C clients and K keys. Worse, the bound is lossy in the direction that HIDES bugs: stellar's confs set `loop_iter` 1-4 with `optimistic_loop: true`, which ASSUMES AWAY every execution exceeding the bound (spending_limit_integrity.rs:27-30 says so explicitly). A passing interleaving rule means 'no counterexample within the bound', not 'no counterexample'.
117	  - Anything involving floats. CVLR has no `Nondet` and no `CvlrLog` impl for f32/f64 (the complete list is cvlr-nondet/src/scalars.rs:84-97). ZSET scores, INCRBYFLOAT and GEO must be catalogued with an integer-scaled surrogate or marked out of scope. Stellar's precedent is munging (vault_64 re-implements storage at i64) — honest, but it changes what was proven.
118	  - Eviction victim selection, LRU/LFU/LRM ordering, HyperLogLog error bounds, SRANDMEMBER distribution — explicitly approximate/randomized (redis.conf:1277-1279); 56 commands carry NONDETERMINISTIC_OUTPUT in src/commands/*.json. Statable only as 'the reply is some member of the set', never as an equality.
119	  - Real memory-level concurrency. The two documented data hazards — reply buffers holding refcounted pointers into live keyspace robjs which IO threads dereference at write time (lazyfree.c:215-232), and `c->flags` CLIENT_DIRTY_CAS written under `REDIS_NO_SANITIZE("thread")` (multi.c:386-389, which I read) — are properties of C memory, not of any abstract state. Permanently out of reach.
120	  - Cluster / atomic slot migration. cluster_asm.c is ~3900 lines introducing a new blocking state, an indefinite write pause (LLONG_MAX), and a new `-TRYAGAIN Slot is being trimmed`. A second project. Scope the catalog to standalone (cluster_enabled=0) explicitly — which also removes KEY_TRIMMED from expireIfNeeded and from dbSize (db.c:3151-3170) and fixes dbnum semantics.
121	
122	CONCURRENCY: CVLR supplies nothing: no threads, no locks, no memory ordering, no happens-before, no scheduler primitive — I confirm the survey on this. So concurrency is expressed by REIFYING the schedule into the abstract state and quantifying over it with nondet. Three encodings, in increasing strength. (1) Two-state predicate over the effect log: atomicity becomes a property of `post.replog - pre.replog` — P-04 says that delta is MULTI-framed iff it has >=2 ops and the direct command is not TOUCHES_ARBITRARY_KEYS, which is exactly what server.c:4005/4014-4019/4021/4040 implements and exactly what Redis's own assert_replication_stream checks. This is the strongest trick available, because it converts "atomic" from an adjective about time into a predicate about a list. (2) Explicit bounded interleaving: the harness draws `Step::nondet()` values tagged with a client id, constrains them (`step.client != watcher`), applies them between the operations of interest, and asserts the property — P-05's WATCH rule, where the value-restoration assume is what makes the property bite. (3) Ordering witnesses carried in state: `blocked[c].seq` is a ghost field with no counterpart in the C (which uses list position, blocked.c:428), and FIFO becomes `seq[a] < seq[b] => a served first` — P-06. What this buys: the three things users actually get wrong (what is atomic, when WATCH aborts, who wakes first) become first-class checkable statements instead of prose. What it costs, stated plainly: the harness now IS the specification of the scheduler, and it is a hand transcription of handleClientsBlockedOnKey (blocked.c:620-677) and propagatePendingCommands (server.c:3993-4045) — reviewable side-by-side against the cited C, but unverified against it. And the quantification is bounded, so these are bug-finding obligations, not theorems. Liveness — the half of concurrency that says something good eventually happens — is simply absent from CVLR and stays in the tcl suite.
123	
124	BLOCKERS:
125	  ! HARD, UNAVOIDABLE: no C frontend. CVLR's primitives are Rust extern "C" CVT_* symbols linked as wasm imports (cvlr-asserts/src/core.rs:1-12); the one worked example hands the Prover a wasm32-unknown-unknown .wasm built by cargo (stellar packages/*/certora_build.py:15-17, justfile:6). Consequence: the CVLR artifact can never be a statement about Redis's C. It is a statement about a Rust model. The strategy is only defensible if that is accepted up front and model fidelity is defended by other means — differential testing against a real redis-server using the oracles the tree already ships (assert_replication_stream, debug_digest, csvdump, utils/req-res-log-validator.py).
126	  ! The model is the real work and its fidelity is unverified. `struct redisServer` is 676 lines of fields (server.h:2078-2753); server.h is 4976 lines included by 72 of 127 .c files; setGenericCommand alone touches server.dirty, notifyKeyspaceEvent, keyModified, rewriteClientCommandVector, addReply and the `shared` globals (t_string.c:161-191). Every abstraction choice is a soundness assumption nobody checks. Budget model REVIEW as heavily as model writing.
127	  ! No local precedent for cvlr's own `#[rule]`. I counted: stellar-contracts uses `cvlr_soroban_derive::rule` in all 55 spec files and `cvlr::rule` in exactly 0. Nothing in this workspace demonstrates cvlr's `#[rule]` reaching the Prover, and cvlr's own CI runs only cargo check/fmt/clippy/test with no wasm step (.github/workflows/main.yml:22-47). Expect an unbudgeted integration phase just to get one trivial rule to verify. Related: `#[rule]` inserts `cvlr_rule_location!()` which expands to `std::file!()` (cvlr-log/src/core.rs:243-248, read directly) — redis-cvlr must be a std crate, and the generated code hard-codes `::cvlr::` paths so the umbrella crate must be nameable as exactly `cvlr`.
128	  ! The entire cvlr-spec DSL has zero production usage. stellar pins cvlr 0.4.0; cvlr-spec shipped in 0.6.0; the formula/predicate/lemma layer is exercised only by its own unit tests. I DID disprove the survey's claim that `cvlr_rules!` is 'UNUSABLE out of the box' — `CvlrSpec::assume_requires`/`check_ensures` are ordinary public methods (spec.rs:32-54) and a ~12-line user-supplied `cvlr_impl_rule!` makes the DSL work, with the exact signature visible at cvlr-spec/tests/test_spec.rs:1138. But 'usable' is not 'used'; you would be the first.
129	  ! Bounded-schedule state explosion. `nondet::<bool>()` costs a full symbolic u64 (`cvlr_nondet_u64() > 0`, scalars.rs:85) and `#[derive(Nondet)]` on an enum draws a u64 per value. K=3 keys x C=3 clients x N=3 steps is already large before any loop bound. Stellar's heaviest confs already need global_timeout 7200 / smt_timeout 6000 / `-split false` for far smaller problems. Expect to tune K, C, N downward until rules close, and expect some interleaving rules never to close.
130	  ! Scope discipline is the main failure mode. Redis 8.9.241 is not the Redis anyone remembers: the value object embeds key and TTL (kvobj), there are new types (OBJ_ARRAY), new encodings (LISTPACK_EX, TMPL_LP), new policies (volatile-lrm/allkeys-lrm), new commands (DELEX, INCREX, HSETEX, BLESS, AR*), and 222 configs of which ~40 change observable semantics. Without a written scope fence (standalone, maxmemory=0, a named fsync mode, strings+lists only, K/C/N fixed), the catalog grows without bound and nothing is ever proven.
131	
132	FIRST MILESTONE: Eight properties, strings-only, K=2 keys, C=2 clients, N=1 interleaved step, Master role, maxmemory=0, cluster off: P-01 (SET/GET integrity, two-state), P-02 (expiry visibility split, with the DBSIZE-vs-GET divergence as an explicitly asserted difference), P-04 (execution-unit MULTI framing over `replog`, bases = EXEC-with-1-write vs EXEC-with-2-writes vs SCAN lazy-expire), P-05 (WATCH value-blindness). Written as PROPERTIES.md entries FIRST, reviewed against the cited C by someone who knows Redis, and only then encoded. Success = three things in order of importance: (1) a Redis engineer reads PROPERTIES.md and either agrees or corrects an entry — this is the deliverable's actual value and it lands before any prover run; (2) `cargo +nightly build --target=wasm32-unknown-unknown --release` with `RUSTFLAGS=-C strip=none` produces a .wasm whose exported symbols include the four rule names, and certoraRun on a conf modelled on packages/contract-utils/confs/pausable_non_panics.conf returns a verdict on at least P-01 — this is the unprecedented step (no `cvlr::rule` usage exists anywhere locally) and is where the schedule risk lives; (3) at least one of P-02/P-04/P-05 FAILS and the counterexample forces a modelling decision to be written down. If (1) succeeds and (2) fails, the catalog still stands on its own and the execution strategy can be re-chosen — which is the entire reason for putting the catalog first.
133	##########################################################################################
134	NAME: C-as-target: Redis keyspace core compiled to MVP wasm, driven by a C reimplementation of the CVLR CVT_* ABI | effort: research-project | confidence: low
135	
136	SKETCH: THE ARTIFACT. A single MVP WebAssembly reactor module built from unmodified Redis C sources plus a harness, handed to the Certora Prover through the same build_script/conf contract stellar-contracts uses (/home/apanda/code/certora/stellar-contracts/packages/tokens/certora_build.py:81-88 emits {project_directory, sources, executables, success, return_code, log}; executables is just a path to a .wasm — nothing in that contract is Rust-specific).

138	COMPILER AND TARGET (verified by me, not inferred). zig 0.16.0-dev at /home/apanda/zig bundles wasi-libc + LLVM + wasm-ld. Command: `zig cc --target=wasm32-wasi -mcpu=mvp -O1 -I redis/src -I redis/deps/{lua/src,hiredis,fpconv,xxhash,hdr_histogram} -D_WASI_EMULATED_SIGNAL -c <file>.c`. I ran this over all 127 files in /home/apanda/code/certora/redis/src: 104 compile with ZERO source modification. The 23 failures are exactly ae_epoll/ae_kqueue/ae_select/ae_evport, anet, aof, bio, config, debug, eval, iothread, memtest, module, rdb, release, sentinel, server, syscheck, tls, cli_common, redis-cli, redis-benchmark, redis-check-rdb — i.e. only files that actually call fork/exec/signal/pthread/dlopen. db.c, object.c, expire.c, t_string.c, t_hash.c, t_list.c, t_set.c, t_zset.c, t_stream.c, multi.c, blocked.c, notify.c, pubsub.c, networking.c, kvstore.c, evict.c, lazyfree.c, dict.c, sds.c, listpack.c, quicklist.c, ziplist.c, intset.c, rax.c all compile. `-mcpu=mvp` strips the extension features (bulk-memory-opt, multivalue, nontrapping-fptoint, sign-ext, extended-const) that clang 22 emits by default and that a frontend written against Rust 1.84's wasm32-unknown-unknown output may not accept.

140	CVT_* IN C (verified). One header, redis-cvlr/include/cvlr_c.h:
141	  #define CVT_IMP(n) __attribute__((import_module("env"), import_name(n)))
142	  CVT_IMP("CVT_assume") void CVT_assume(int);
143	  CVT_IMP("CVT_assert") void CVT_assert(int);
144	  CVT_IMP("CVT_satisfy") void CVT_satisfy(int);
145	  CVT_IMP("CVT_nondet_u64") unsigned long long CVT_nondet_u64(void);  ... etc.
146	This produces import records in module "env" that are byte-identical in shape to what CVLR's Rust `#[cfg_attr(all(target_family="wasm", target_os="none"), link(wasm_import_module="env"))] extern "C" { pub fn CVT_assume(bool); ... }` (/home/apanda/code/certora/cvlr/cvlr-asserts/src/core.rs:1-12) produces. The full symbol set to declare is the 60 CVT_* names I extracted from the cvlr tree (CVT_assert/assume/satisfy/sanity, CVT_nondet_{u8..u128,i8..i128,usize}, 17 CVT_calltrace_*, 22 CVT_nativeint_u64_*, CVT_rule_location, 3 CVT_u128_*).
147	
148	RULES. CVLR's #[rule] does only three things (/home/apanda/code/certora/cvlr/cvlr-macros/src/lib.rs:20-36): adds #[no_mangle], prepends cvlr_rule_location!(), appends cvlr_vacuity_check!(). The C equivalent is exact: `__attribute__((used, visibility("default"))) void rule_foo(void) { CVT_calltrace_print_location(__FILE__, __LINE__); ...; CVT_sanity(1); }` plus `-Wl,--export=rule_foo`. The .conf lists bare symbol names (pausable_non_panics.conf:4-8), so nothing else is needed.

150	END-TO-END PROOF I ACTUALLY RAN. I built /tmp/.../scratchpad/ctest/poc.wasm (1.38 MB): harness.c + stubs.c + UNMODIFIED redis/src/{sds.c,zmalloc.c,util.c}, linked with `zig cc --target=wasm32-wasi -Wl,--no-entry -Wl,--export=rule_sds_cat_len -mexec-model=reactor`. I parsed its sections: EXPORTS = [memory, _initialize, rule_sds_cat_len]; IMPORTS include ('env','CVT_assert'), ('env','CVT_assume'), ('env','CVT_nondet_u64'). The rule body is `n,m = nondet; assume n<=32; assume m<=32; s = sdsnewlen(buf,n); s = sdscatlen(s,buf,m); assert(sdslen(s) == n+m)`. So: real Redis C, real CVLR ABI, real wasm, real exported rule. That part is not speculative.

"Keyspace core": db.c object.c expire.c kvstore.c dict.c sds.c listpack.c ziplist.c quicklist.c intset.c t_string.c t_hash.c t_list.c t_set.c t_zset.c util.c zmalloc.c notify.c multi.c rax.c ebuckets.c estore.c entry.c keymeta.c endianconv.c siphash.c sha256.c crc16.c crc64.c adlist.c. 154	WHAT MUST BE STUBBED OR TRANSPLANTED (measured, not guessed):
155	- Transplanted verbatim from server.c (which does not compile): the dictType tables at server.c:634 (setDictType), :648 (dbDictType), :664 (dbExpiresDictType), :690 (hashDictType) plus their sds hash/compare/destructor callbacks; createSharedObjects() (177 lines) for the `shared` global; a cut-down initServerConfig for `struct redisServer server` (676 fields — zero-init and set the ~30 the carve-out reads: dirty, dbnum, db, maxmemory_policy, mstime, cmd_time_snapshot, execution_nesting, masterhost, repl_slave_ro, lazyfree_lazy_*, notify_keyspace_events, hash/set/zset/list thresholds, loading, allow_access_expired).
156	- Reply layer (~30 addReply* + setDeferredArrayLen/SetLen): a model reply recorder appending (type, value) into a fixed array, so rules can assert on the actual client reply rather than ignoring it.
157	- Propagation layer (alsoPropagate, alsoPropagateForced, forceCommandPropagation, preventCommandPropagation, shouldPropagate, postExecutionUnitOperations, enterExecutionUnit, exitExecutionUnit): record into an array. This is the C analogue of tests/test_helper.tcl:787-864 assert_replication_stream and is what makes "effects are propagated as PEXPIREAT/DEL, wrapped in MULTI/EXEC only when >1 op" (server.c:4003-4041) a provable property.
158	- Time: mstime, ustime, commandTimeSnapshot, getMonotonicUs, LRU_CLOCK -> CVT_nondet_u64 constrained monotone and frozen per execution unit (mirroring server.c:1420-1428).
159	- Blocking: blockClient, blockForKeys, signalKeyAsReady, signalDeletedKeyAsReady, unblockClient, updateStatsOnUnblock -> a model FIFO queue.
160	- No-op returning 0: all module* (23 syms), all asm*/cluster* (12), bless*/hotkeys*/tracking*/latency*/bio*/rdb*/functions*/eval* hooks.
161	- Allocator: zmalloc.c compiles as-is and calls libc malloc/free. Replace with a deterministic bump-plus-freelist in the shim (needed anyway to drop wasi).
162	- Never linked: ae*.c, anet.c, socket.c, tls.c, iothread.c, bio.c, aof.c, rdb.c, replication.c, server.c, module.c, eval.c, sentinel.c.
163	- ubsan: zig emits __ubsan_handle_* calls; route every one to CVT_assert(0). Free bonus — C undefined behaviour becomes a verification failure.
164	
165	DROPPING WASI. The PoC still imports 29 wasi_snapshot_preview1 functions (fd_write, path_open, clock_time_get, ...). If the prover requires "env" to be the only import module (a Soroban contract's only non-CVT imports are Soroban host fns in "env"), switch to `--target=wasm32-freestanding -nostdlib` and supply a ~500-line libc shim: bump malloc/free/realloc/calloc, memcpy/memset/memmove/memcmp, strlen/strcmp/strcasecmp/strchr, a snprintf/%lld/%s subset for sdscatprintf, and stubs for the long-double paths (__addtf3/__subtf3/__multf3/__gttf2 come from compiler-rt and can be linked or stubbed; INCRBYFLOAT/ZINCRBY long-double arithmetic would have to be excluded from scope). I did not build this; it is the single largest piece of un-derisked plumbing after the prover gate itself.

167	SPEC FILE LAYOUT. /home/apanda/code/certora/redis-cvlr/{include/cvlr_c.h, harness/{server_init.c, reply_model.c, prop_model.c, time_model.c, stubs_noop.c, alloc_shim.c}, specs/{string_integrity.c, keyspace_invariants.c, expire_integrity.c, multi_watch.c, type_panics.c}, build.sh, certora_build.py, confs/*.conf}. Rule style copies stellar-contracts exactly: nondet inputs -> snapshot pre-state -> call the real Redis function -> snapshot post-state -> assert the relation, with the /home/apanda/code/certora/stellar-contracts/packages/access/src/ownable/specs/ quartet (integrity / invariants / panics / non_panics) as the file taxonomy and assume_pre_X / assert_post_X as the invariant idiom.
168	
169	VERIFIES:
170	  + Properties of the ACTUAL Redis C implementation, not a model of it. This is the strategy's only real advantage over writing a Rust model, and it is a large one: every claim is about the shipped bytes.
171	  + String-command integrity over real t_string.c: SET/SETNX/SETEX/GETSET/GETDEL/GETEX/APPEND/SETRANGE/GETRANGE/INCR-family pre/post relations, including the exact off-by-one normalization at t_string.c:645-688 (GETRANGE's both-negative short-circuit, which differs from LRANGE's at t_list.c:762-786) and the overflow rejection at t_string.c:896-935.
172	  + Keyspace invariants over real db.c/object.c/kvstore.c: no empty aggregate is reachable from the keyspace (every shrink path calls dbDelete/dbDeleteSkipKeysizesUpdate — t_list.c:981, t_set.c:751, t_zset.c:2121, t_hash.c:6660); kv in db->expires <=> kvobjGetExpire(kv) != -1 (db.c:2757-2825); dbSetValue's in-place mutation fast path (db.c:653-673) preserves the abstract value.
173	  + Expiry semantics over real expire.c/db.c: EXPIRE NX/XX/GT/LT mutual-exclusion and the +infinity treatment of a persistent key (expire.c:671-812); that EXPIRE with a past time deletes and propagates DEL only when checkAlreadyExpired holds (expire.c:660-668); TTL/PTTL/PERSIST return codes; the lazy-vs-active one-millisecond disagreement at t == expire (db.c:2953 `now > when` vs expire.c:41 `now < when`).
174	  + The propagation contract, which is the closest thing Redis has to a user-visible correctness spec: an execution unit that queued >1 op propagates wrapped in MULTI/EXEC (server.c:4003-4041), a single-write transaction does not, and lazy expiry propagates bare DELs. This is directly checkable against tests/unit/multi.tcl:398/412 and tests/unit/expire.tcl:809/830.
175	  + WRONGTYPE totality: for each (command, stored type) pair, either the command replies WRONGTYPE or it does not read the value (checkType at object.c:906-913).
176	  + Panic / non-panic rules in the stellar-contracts style, using `-trapAsAssert true` (the prover_arg used in all 5 *_non_panics confs): 'SET with a well-formed argv never traps', 'GETRANGE with any int64 start/end never traps'. These are memory-safety proofs of real C and are the cheapest genuinely valuable output of this strategy.
177	  + Undefined behaviour in the covered C, for free, by routing clang's __ubsan_handle_* into CVT_assert(0): signed overflow, shift-out-of-bounds, misaligned/null pointer loads, out-of-bounds array indexing.
178	  + MULTI/WATCH CAS at the execution-unit level over real multi.c: touchWatchedKey sets CLIENT_DIRTY_CAS for any write to the key regardless of value (multi.c:388-425), and the already-expired-at-WATCH exemption (multi.c:397-406, wk->expired set at multi.c:330).
179	
180	DOES NOT:
181	  - Real thread-level concurrency. Wasm MVP is single-threaded and has no threads, atomics or memory model. iothread.c and bio.c do not even compile to wasm (pthread_cancel, pthread_sigmask, sigemptyset). So the two races Redis actually documents in-tree are unreachable by this strategy: (a) IO threads dereferencing live keyspace robj pointers held in copy-avoidance reply buffers (networking.c:605-631, mitigated by pauseAllIOThreads + deep copy at lazyfree.c:215-232), and (b) the CLIENT_DIRTY_CAS / c->flags word race annotated REDIS_NO_SANITIZE("thread") at multi.c:381-388. The benign-race list in src/tsan.sup is likewise out of reach.
182	  - Anything above one process: replication, AOF/RDB durability, WAIT/WAITAOF, failover, cluster slot migration. replication.c, aof.c, rdb.c, cluster_legacy.c's fork paths do not compile, and forking has no wasm representation. The strongest durability claim in the tree ('appendfsync always fsyncs before the reply', from the beforeSleep ordering at server.c:2070 vs :2117) is an event-loop property and the event loop is not linked.
183	  - The event loop itself, hence: blocking-command FIFO fairness across event-loop iterations (tests/unit/type/list.tcl:2306), handleClientsBlockedOnKeys' non-reentrancy (blocked.c:348-353), and script yielding via processEventsWhileBlocked (networking.c:5759-5805). These can only be modelled by a hand-written scheduler in the harness, at which point you are verifying the harness, not Redis.
184	  - Eviction as it actually behaves. performEvictions() runs before every command (server.c:4848) and its victim choice depends on a pool that persists across calls (evict.c:589) and samples across all DBs. Modelling this faithfully is possible but each rule then carries arbitrary nondeterministic key deletion, which will make most invariants unprovable. Realistic scope: set maxmemory=0 and exclude it.
185	  - Long-double arithmetic: INCRBYFLOAT, ZINCRBY, ZADD scores, GEO. These pull __addtf3/__multf3/__gttf2 (128-bit soft float). Even if linked, symbolic reasoning over soft-float is hopeless, and CVLR has no f32/f64 Nondet or CvlrLog impl at all.
186	  - Lua/EVAL and FUNCTION: eval.c does not compile and script_lua.c drags in the entire Lua 5.1 interpreter with setjmp/longjmp (deps/lua/src/luaconf.h:615), which has no wasm representation.
187	  - Anything requiring unbounded iteration over a data structure: full dict rehash, listpack/ziplist scan to an arbitrary index, quicklist traversal, rax walks. See blockers.
188	  - The CVLR spec DSL. cvlr-spec's CvlrFormula/CvlrLemma/cvlr_def_predicate!/cvlr_rules! are Rust macros. A C harness gets none of them. You would be writing raw CVT_assert/CVT_assume — which is, notably, exactly what all 484 rules in stellar-contracts do anyway (zero uses of the DSL there), so the loss is smaller than it sounds. But calling the result 'a CVLR specification' is a stretch: it is a C reimplementation of CVLR's wasm ABI.
189	
190	CONCURRENCY: Honest version: this strategy expresses Redis's *execution-unit* concurrency and nothing below it.
191	
192	The grounding fact (I re-read and confirmed it) is that Redis 8.9 command execution is strictly main-thread. networking.c:3927-3934 reads verbatim: "If we are in the context of an I/O thread, we can't really execute the command here. All we can do is to flag the client as one that needs to process the command." — followed by `if (c->running_tid != IOTHREAD_MAIN_THREAD_ID) { c->io_flags |= CLIENT_IO_PENDING_COMMAND; enqueuePendingClientsToMainThread(c, 0); break; }`. IO threads parse; they never call processCommand. The user-visible atomic unit is the execution unit (server.c:1420-1432 enterExecutionUnit/exitExecutionUnit, which also freezes server.cmd_time_snapshot at nesting 0), and effects become externally visible only at postExecutionUnitOperationsEx (server.c:4060-4075).
193	
194	Consequence: *sequential composition is the correct concurrency model for the layer this strategy can reach.* A two-client interleaving rule is just: havoc the db; run command A's C function to completion; snapshot; run command B's C function; assert. There is no interleaving to explore inside a command, because Redis guarantees there is none. That makes the rules cheap and, more importantly, makes them faithful — which a thread-interleaving model would not be.
195	
196	What that buys, concretely:
197	- WATCH/EXEC CAS: nondet a key, WATCH it, run an arbitrary nondet write command, then EXEC, and assert CLIENT_DIRTY_CAS is set iff the write touched the key (multi.c:388-425) — including the value-blindness (a write-back of the identical value still dirties) and the already-expired exemption.
198	- Expiry racing a read: a rule that calls lookupKeyRead at a nondet clock and asserts the 4-valued keyStatus contract (db.c:39-45, 3004-3072), parameterized over (masterhost, CLIENT_MASTER, repl_slave_ro, loading), which is the actual shape of the rule — expiry is client-role-dependent, not time-dependent alone.
199	- Execution-unit atomicity of propagation: run a command that queues N effects and assert the MULTI/EXEC wrapping rule (server.c:4003-4041) holds for N>1 and does not for N==1.
200	
201	What it categorically cannot buy: any happens-before, any claim about the IO-thread pause handshake (iothread.c:408-436 busy-waits on a seq_cst atomic), any claim about the bio lazyfree worker touching detached kvstores (lazyfree.c:33-50), any statement about the races in src/tsan.sup. CVLR itself has no threading vocabulary either — there is no atomic, lock, or scheduler primitive anywhere in its ~15k lines — so this is a limitation of the whole CVLR approach, not just of the C-target variant. If thread-level concurrency is a requirement, this strategy does not meet it and no amount of engineering inside it will.
202	
203	BLOCKERS:
204	  ! HARD GATE, UNTESTABLE LOCALLY: there is no evidence the Certora Prover ingests a C-derived wasm module, and no Certora tooling is installed on this machine to find out. I checked: `certoraRun`, `certoraSolanaProver`, `certoraSorobanProver`, `solc` are all absent from PATH; `import certora_cli` fails; there is no ~/.certora directory; `find /` turns up no certora install. The only local evidence about the prover's input is stellar-contracts' conf/build_script pair, and every artifact it has ever been given is a Rust-compiled Soroban contract .wasm. WHAT WOULD HAVE TO BE TRUE: (a) the wasm frontend is language-agnostic, i.e. it does not assume Soroban host-function ABI, Rust panic/unwind shape, or Rust's allocator; (b) it accepts a module whose only non-CVT import is nothing (freestanding) rather than a Soroban 'env'; (c) it accepts a ~1-5 MB module with a real heap. None of (a)(b)(c) is confirmable from these files. This must be answered by Certora before any of the work below is worth starting.
205	  ! SYMBOLIC MEMORY AT BYTE GRANULARITY. A Soroban contract's wasm linear memory is small and mostly stack; Redis's is a real heap with byte-level pointer tagging. sds reads its header type from a 1-byte flags field at s[-1] and selects one of five header layouts by size (sds.h); listpack encodes integers inline with a variable-length backlen field scanned backwards (listpack.c); dict stores type-tagged pointers. Proving anything about these requires a decision procedure over a symbolic-size, byte-addressed heap with aliasing. This is the most likely failure mode and it fails as a timeout, not as a clean error. There is no local evidence about the prover's wasm memory model.
206	  ! LOOP BOUNDS. Every real conf in this workspace uses loop_iter in {1,2,3,4} and 30 of 56 set optimistic_loop:true (which ASSUMES AWAY every execution exceeding the bound — stated explicitly at stellar-contracts/packages/accounts/src/policies/specs/spending_limit_integrity.rs:27-30). Redis data structures need dozens to thousands of iterations: dictRehash, lpSeek, quicklistIndex, raxSeek. With optimistic_loop the rules go silently vacuous; without it they do not terminate. There is no evidence CVLR or the wasm frontend supports loop invariants (that machinery is CVL-for-Solidity). Mitigation: bound every rule to tiny inputs (<=4 elements) with cvlr_assume, which is honest but sharply limits what is provable.
207	  ! INDIRECT CALLS. dict vtables (dbDictType.hashFunction, keyCompare, keyDestructor) and the command table are function pointers, compiled to wasm `call_indirect` through a table. Whether the frontend resolves these precisely or havocs them is unknown. If it havocs, every dict operation becomes unsound/useless.
208	  ! WASI MUST BE ELIMINATED. My PoC imports 29 wasi_snapshot_preview1 symbols. Removing them means writing a ~500-line freestanding libc shim (allocator, mem*/str*, a printf subset for sdscatprintf, compiler-rt soft-float or exclusion of long-double paths) and revalidating the whole 30-object compile under --target=wasm32-freestanding, which I did NOT do — sds.c fails immediately there because <stdio.h> is absent.
209	  ! HARNESS FIDELITY IS THE SOUNDNESS BOUNDARY. ~120 stubs plus a transplanted server.c fragment (dictType tables at server.c:634-760, createSharedObjects' 177 lines, a cut-down 676-field `struct redisServer`) sit between the rules and the code. Every stub is an unverified assumption, and a stub that is too permissive makes a rule vacuous while a stub that is too strict makes it unprovable. There is no mechanism to check the stubs against the real server.c, because server.c does not compile.
210	  ! TOOLCHAIN PROVENANCE. zig 0.16-dev bundling clang 22 is not a toolchain Certora has ever seen; their Soroban path pins nightly-2024-11-22 (Rust 1.84, roughly LLVM 19). Feature-set skew is manageable via -mcpu=mvp (I verified the target_features section empties out), but codegen-shape skew (how clang lays out structs, emits memcpy, handles varargs) is not something I can de-risk here.
211	
212	FIRST MILESTONE: Two steps, the first of which is a go/no-go that costs about a day.
213	
214	STEP 0 (the gate, ~1 day of work plus a question to Certora). Take /tmp/claude-1000/-home-apanda-code-certora/2280ea4e-7521-457a-ac11-8d004b8438f6/scratchpad/ctest/poc.wasm — which already exists and already exports `rule_sds_cat_len` over unmodified redis/src/sds.c while importing env.CVT_assert/CVT_assume/CVT_nondet_u64 — rebuild it under --target=wasm32-freestanding with a minimal libc shim so that `env` holds only CVT_* and there are zero wasi imports, wrap it in a certora_build.py clone and a conf copied from stellar-contracts/packages/contract-utils/confs/pausable_non_panics.conf, and run certoraRun. Success criterion, both halves required: (1) the rule reports VERIFIED, and (2) flipping the assert to `sdslen(s) == n + m + 1` produces a real counterexample with concrete n and m. Half (2) is essential — a VERIFIED on a rule the prover silently could not analyse is worthless. If the prover rejects the module, ask Certora precisely: does the wasm frontend accept a module not produced by rustc, and what must the import surface look like? If the answer is no, this entire strategy is dead and the fallback is a Rust model of Redis semantics, which is a different proposal.
215	
216	STEP 1 (the smallest slice that proves the approach is useful, ~2-4 weeks, only if Step 0 passes). One real user-visible command end to end: SETRANGE. Link t_string.c + object.c + sds.c + util.c + zmalloc.c + db.c + the harness (server/shared globals, dbDictType, reply recorder, propagation recorder, time model, ~60 no-op stubs). Write three rules in the stellar-contracts idiom: (a) integrity — for nondet key, nondet offset in [0,64], nondet value of length <=16 over an existing string of length <=32, assert the post-value equals pre-value with the range overwritten and the gap zero-filled (t_string.c:575-643); (b) panic — assert `SETRANGE k -1 v` always traps, with `-trapAsAssert true`; (c) propagation — assert exactly one op is queued and it is not MULTI-wrapped. Success criterion: all three terminate under a loop_iter small enough that optimistic_loop is off, and (a) catches an injected off-by-one when you mutate the sdsgrowzero length by one. That last check is the one that tells you the symbolic heap is actually being reasoned about rather than abstracted away, and it is the point at which you will find out whether the memory-model blocker is fatal.
217	##########################################################################################
218	NAME: Dual-mode Rust reference model of Redis user-visible behavior, CVLR-specified, tied to redis-server by differential fuzzing | effort: large | confidence: medium
219	
220	SKETCH: WHAT IS BUILT. /home/apanda/code/certora/redis-cvlr becomes a Cargo workspace holding an executable *reference model* of Redis's user-visible semantics in plain Rust, plus CVLR rules over it. The C tree is read-only reference material and an oracle process; it is never compiled, linked, or seen by the Prover.
221	
222	  redis-cvlr/
223	    model/      state.rs (World/Db/Slot/Value/ClientState/ReplLog), cmd.rs (Cmd, Reply),
224	                step.rs (fn step(&mut World, Step) -> Vec<Event>), commands/*.rs
225	                -- pure Rust, zero cvlr deps except `Nondet` derives; every fn carries `// ref: src/t_string.c:87-190`
226	    specs/      #[rule] fns, cfg(feature="certora"): integrity.rs invariants.rs errors.rs
227	                concurrency.rs replication.rs -- every rule carries `// oracle: tests/unit/expire.tcl:809`
228	    drivers/    concrete/  our own #[no_mangle] CVT_* over a seeded byte stream

Tool reply2026-09-19T17:38:13.389Z
233	TOOLCHAIN — I VERIFIED THIS END TO END, DO NOT TAKE IT ON FAITH FROM THE SURVEY. I wrote a probe crate (3-slot keyspace, TTLs, three #[rule]s) depending on the local cvlr 0.6.1 by path and built it:
234	  RUSTFLAGS="-C strip=none -C link-arg=--import-undefined" cargo build --release --target wasm32-unknown-unknown
235	The resulting .wasm has exactly the shape stellar-contracts ships to the Prover. I parsed its section tables: imports = env::CVT_assert, CVT_assume, CVT_sanity, CVT_rule_location, CVT_nondet_u64/i64/usize, CVT_calltrace_attach_location, CVT_calltrace_print_u64_1; exports = memory + the three #[no_mangle] rule symbols by name. Code section is 16.6 KB; the 1.6 MB file is DWARF. Artifacts left at <scratchpad>/probe.
236	  Two corrections to the survey here. (1) The wasm_import_module attribute is gated on `all(target_family="wasm", target_os="none")` (cvlr-asserts/src/core.rs:2-6), and wasm32-unknown-unknown has target_os="unknown" — so on a modern nightly the CVT_* symbols are plain undefined and rust-lld errors out. `-C link-arg=--import-undefined` fixes it and yields the correct env:: imports. stellar never needed the flag because nightly-2024-11-22 (rust-toolchain.toml:2) allowed undefined wasm symbols by default. (2) The survey's gotcha that `#[rule]` cannot compile because cvlr_rule_location! names `std::file!()` (cvlr-log/src/core.rs:243-248) is true only for `#![no_std]` crates; an ordinary std crate compiles #[rule] fine, including to wasm. So the model crate must be std (or `extern crate std`).
237	
238	THE PIVOT — DUAL-MODE LINKING. CVLR's whole vocabulary is extern "C" symbols the Prover interprets (cvlr-asserts/src/core.rs:1-12, cvlr-nondet/src/scalars.rs:1-27). Symbolic mode: the Prover supplies them. Concrete mode: WE supply them. I wrote `#[no_mangle] extern "C-unwind" fn CVT_nondet_u64() -> u64` over an xorshift stream plus CVT_assume/CVT_assert, linked it into a test binary, and executed the *same* #[rule] functions 2000 times concretely. It works. Two findings that shape model style: (a) the `extern "C"` declaration means a panicking CVT_assume aborts the process rather than unwinding — declare the driver's copies `extern "C-unwind"`; (b) filtering with `cvlr_assume!(k < N)` rejected 2000/2000 concrete runs, while *drawing* `nondet::<u64>() % N` accepted 2000/2000. So the model must draw bounded values, never assume them — the same MAX=5 modulo idiom stellar already uses (smart_account/specs/nondet.rs:16-39). This single discipline is what makes one body of code both Prover-checkable and fuzzable.
239	
240	WHAT "FAITHFUL ENOUGH" MEANS, CONCRETELY.
241	 - Keys are opaque KeyId(u8) over 0..K, K=3..5. Justification: every property in scope is about key *equality*, not name bytes. Anything where names matter (KEYS globbing, cluster hash tags) is out of scope by construction.
242	 - Values: Str as a bounded ([u8;8], len) so APPEND/SETRANGE/GETRANGE are real; INCR family on i64 exactly (t_string.c:896-935). No f32/f64 anywhere — I grepped all of cvlr and there is not one occurrence — so ZSET scores become i64 and INCRBYFLOAT/GEO/HLL are excluded, stated on the cover page.
243	 - Time is an explicit `clock: Ms` field advanced only by a ClockTick step. Lazy expiry uses `now > when` and the active-expire step uses `now >= when`; these differ by one millisecond in the C and the model keeps them distinct (I re-read db.c:2946-2955 and expire.c:40-42 — the survey is right).
244	 - The observable interface is (Reply, Snapshot) where Snapshot is the csvdump projection (key -> type, value, pttl). Encoding, refcount, LRU, memory are absent from the model, not hidden in it.
245	 - Configuration is pinned in the model, not parameterised: standalone, cluster off, databases=1, maxmemory=0, appendonly=no, save='', active expire modeled as an explicit adversarial step.
246	
247	CORRESPONDENCE, THREE LEGS, HONESTLY RANKED.
248	 1. DIFFERENTIAL FUZZING AGAINST THE REAL BINARY (primary evidence, and available today). I built redis-server 8.9.241 here (`make -C src MALLOC=libc`, 17 MB at /home/apanda/code/certora/redis/src/redis-server) and drove it over raw RESP. The harness draws Vec<Step> from the byte stream, runs the model, replays the same commands against a server pinned to the model's assumptions, compares every reply, then compares the model Snapshot against a reconstruction via KEYS/TYPE/PTTL/per-type reads (tests/support/util.tcl:496-570 is the template). For the replication leg, SYNC to the server and diff its propagated stream against the model's ReplLog — the mechanised form of assert_replication_stream (tests/test_helper.tcl:787-864). I ran a miniature version of exactly this loop and it immediately reproduced three behaviours a naive model gets wrong: with DEBUG SET-ACTIVE-EXPIRE 0 and an elapsed PX, DBSIZE returns 1 and INFO keyspace says db0:keys=1; GET returns nil AND drops DBSIZE to 0; KEYS * returns [] and leaves DBSIZE at 1. One minute of work, three real facts.
249	 2. MACHINE-CHECKED CONFORMANCE TO src/commands/*.json (narrow, but exhaustive over what it covers). 464 files, 442 with command_flags, 433 with reply_schema, 240 with key_specs. A build script emits, per modeled command, the arity check, the declared key set with its RO/RW/ACCESS/UPDATE/INSERT/DELETE effect, and a reply-shape predicate; a CVLR rule per command asserts the model agrees. Caveats to carry: src/commands/README.md:3-9 disclaims the files as non-authoritative (cross-check against live COMMAND INFO in the same difftest harness), 7 key_specs are INCOMPLETE and 3 are VARIABLE_FLAGS — and SET is one of the VARIABLE_FLAGS ones, so it is hand-written.
250	 3. TRACEABILITY + TRANSCRIBED TCL (review-grade only). Model fns cite C line ranges; rules cite the tcl test that states the same contract. A short list of tcl tests state contracts rather than examples and get hand-transcribed: blocking FIFO (list.tcl:2306), nested-unblock order (list.tcl:2422), MULTI wrapping present vs absent (multi.tcl:412 vs :398), lazy-expire DELs never wrapped (expire.tcl:809, :830).
251	
252	SPEC STYLE. Hand-written `#[rule] pub fn ...` in the stellar `_integrity`/`_invariants`/`_errors` idiom (packages/access/src/ownable/specs/ is the model; 484 rules across 55 files, all raw cvlr_assert!/cvlr_assume!). Do NOT use cvlr-spec's cvlr_rules!/cvlr_invariant_rules!: they expand to `cvlr_impl_rule!{...}` (cvlr-macros/src/rule_for_spec.rs:97) and CVLR never defines that macro — I grepped the whole tree and the only two definitions are test stubs commented "this would be provided by the user in real usage". cvlr_def_predicate!/cvlr_lemma! are usable but take the context type as an `ident`, so model context structs must be bare, non-generic, in scope. Note also that the optional description argument to cvlr_assert! is parsed and silently discarded (cvlr-asserts/src/core.rs:69-75, confirmed) — use clog! for anything you want in a counterexample.
253	
254	VERIFIES:
255	  + Single-command integrity over the modeled subset: post-state and reply as a function of pre-state. Core: SET (NX/XX/GET/KEEPTTL/EX/PX/EXAT/PXAT and their mutual exclusion -- the exact bug this release fixed, 00-RELEASENOTES #15291), GET, GETEX, GETDEL, GETSET, DEL/UNLINK, EXISTS, TYPE, APPEND, SETRANGE, GETRANGE, INCR/DECR/INCRBY (i64 overflow errors included), RENAME/COPY, plus list/hash/set cores.
256	  + The expiry contract as it actually is, as four separate rules rather than one false one: (a) no command reaching its key through lookupKey* returns the value of a key with expire < clock; (b) GET on a logically-expired key deletes it and emits a DEL into the replication log; (c) KEYS filters expired keys WITHOUT deleting; (d) DBSIZE and INFO-keyspace do NOT filter at all. All four empirically confirmed against the built binary during this survey.
257	  + The lazy/active one-millisecond asymmetry: at clock == expire a lazy lookup sees the key as VALID (db.c:2953 `now > when`) while an ActiveExpireTick deletes it (expire.c:41 `now < expire` -> not expired). The model keeps both and a rule pins the difference.
258	  + Type discipline: any command against a key holding the wrong type yields WRONGTYPE and leaves the Snapshot unchanged (object.c:906-913).
259	  + The no-empty-collection invariant: no reachable state has a present key whose collection value has length 0. The C enforces this at ~20 scattered sites (t_list.c:981-986, t_set.c:751-756, t_zset.c:2121-2128, t_hash.c:6660-6667) and again on load (rdb.c:4502-4505); in the model it becomes one global invariant checked after every step.
260	  + MULTI/EXEC: (a) the clock is frozen for the whole EXEC, so no TTL can expire between sub-commands (server.c:1420-1432); (b) no other client's step interleaves; (c) a successful EXEC implies no other step wrote a watched key since WATCH, including the already-expired-at-WATCH exemption (multi.c:397-406); (d) a runtime error inside EXEC does NOT roll back preceding effects, only queue-time errors abort.
261	  + Blocking-command contracts: clients blocked on the same key are served in block order (FIFO, blocked.c:428 + 620-645; oracle list.tcl:2306); a wakeup on a key whose current type does not match leaves the client blocked (blocked.c:658-670); a woken client's re-executed command runs in its own execution unit after the waker's has closed (blocked.c:752-769, server.c:5036-5039); inside MULTI/EXEC or a script a blocking command never blocks and returns nil even with timeout 0 (multi.c:163-164, t_list.c:1364-1368).
262	  + Replication refinement: replaying the model's emitted ReplLog onto a fresh model World yields a Snapshot observationally equal to the producer's. This is the formal statement of what tests/integration/replication-psync.tcl:116-128 checks with DEBUG DIGEST under three concurrent writers.
263	  + Propagation wrapping, exactly: an execution unit that produced >1 effective write propagates wrapped in MULTI..EXEC; exactly 1 propagates unwrapped; 0 propagates nothing (server.c:4003-4041; oracles multi.tcl:412 and :398). Lazy-expire DELs are never wrapped (expire.tcl:809, :830).
264	  + Propagation determinism: SET with a relative TTL propagates as `SET k v PXAT <absolute-ms>` (t_string.c:178-195), so replaying the log against a different clock reproduces the same TTL -- the property that makes the refinement rule above non-vacuous.
265	  + Per-command conformance to src/commands/*.json for the modeled subset: arity, the declared key set, the declared RO/RW/ACCESS/UPDATE/INSERT/DELETE effect, and reply shape against reply_schema.
266	
267	DOES NOT:
268	  - Redis. Not one line of C is verified, compiled, or seen by the Prover. A misunderstanding the model author shares with nobody is caught by the difftest; a misunderstanding they wrote into both the model and their reading of the C is invisible. This is the whole correspondence gap and it does not shrink asymptotically -- differential testing is sampling, not proof.
269	  - Thread safety and the C memory model. The model AXIOMATISES the serialization point (one Step is atomic), justified by citation to networking.c:3927-3934 and iothread.c:664-687. That axiom is exactly what a real Redis concurrency bug would violate. The two races Redis itself documents -- the c->flags CLIENT_DIRTY_CAS write suppressed with REDIS_NO_SANITIZE("thread") at multi.c:381-388, and reply buffers holding refcounted pointers into live keyspace objects that IO threads dereference at write time (networking.c:605-631, lazyfree.c:215-232) -- are structurally out of reach. CVLR has no atomic, no lock, no happens-before, no scheduler primitive anywhere in its ~15k lines.
270	  - Unboundedness. K=3..5 keys, 8-byte values, small client and step counts. stellar's confs run loop_iter 1-4 with optimistic_loop:true, which ASSUMES AWAY every execution that iterates past the bound (stated in spending_limit_integrity.rs:27-30). 'For all keys' means 'for all three keys'. There is no cut-off or symmetry argument on offer.
271	  - Anything with floating point: real ZSET scores, INCRBYFLOAT, GEO, HyperLogLog error bounds. cvlr contains zero f32/f64 -- no Nondet, no CvlrLog, no conversion. Verified by grep over the whole cvlr tree.
272	  - Memory-driven behaviour: maxmemory, all 10 eviction policies (evict.c:543-833), the -OOM write rejection, lazyfree timing, BLESS/NO-EVICT, OBJECT ENCODING, MEMORY USAGE, active defrag. Encodings in particular are history-dependent (quicklist->listpack uses HALF the threshold when shrinking, t_list.c:80-83), so they are not even a function of the abstract state.
273	  - Cluster in all forms: MOVED/ASK/CROSSSLOT, slot ownership, and the ~3,942-line atomic-slot-migration subsystem in cluster_asm.c. Also Sentinel, modules, Lua/EVAL, FUNCTION, ACLs.
274	  - File formats and the wire: RDB, AOF (and MP-AOF), DUMP/RESTORE, and RESP2/RESP3 framing itself. The model produces a Reply value, not bytes.
275	  - Durability. appendfsync, WAIT, WAITAOF, min-replicas-to-write are about fsync and sockets. A state model can reproduce the offset arithmetic but cannot say anything about the guarantee; redis.conf:892-894 already disclaims min-replicas-to-write as a guarantee anyway.
276	  - Liveness of any kind. Every CVLR rule is a safety property over one bounded execution. 'A blocked BLPOP is eventually served', 'an acknowledged write eventually reaches the replica', 'active expiry eventually reclaims' are all inexpressible.
277	  - Admin/debug surface that deliberately breaks the abstraction: DEBUG DIGEST, DEBUG OBJECT, DEBUG SET-ALLOW-ACCESS-EXPIRED (db.c:2948 makes keyIsExpired return 0 unconditionally), RANDOMKEY's give-up-after-100-tries path (db.c:855-865), HLEN counting expired fields (t_hash.c:5602). Excluded explicitly rather than modeled wrongly.
278	
279	CONCURRENCY: Interleaving is made explicit and finite, and the model is honest about which half is axiom and which half is theorem.
280	
281	World holds C client states plus the Db, the blocked/ready structures and a bounded ReplLog. `enum Step { Cmd(ClientId, Cmd), Exec(ClientId), ActiveExpireTick, ClockTick(Ms) }`. A rule draws a nondeterministic `[Step; S]` (drawn, not assumed — `nondet::<u64>() % k` at every choice point) and folds `step` over it. Per-client program order holds by construction; every interleaving of the S steps across the C clients is explored by the Prover, as are the environment steps.
282	
283	AXIOM (not verified, justified by citation): `step` is atomic. Redis executes commands serially on the main thread — IO threads parse and refuse to execute, handing the client back (networking.c:3927-3934), and clients that cannot be handled off-main are pinned (iothread.c:286-292). The real atomic unit is the *execution unit*, not the command (server.c:1420-1432 nests and freezes server.cmd_time_snapshot; server.c:4060-4075 flushes propagation only at nesting 0), so a Step is an execution unit and EXEC is one Step. Because this is an axiom, no bug that breaks it can be found here.
284	
285	THEOREMS (actual proof obligations, and the reason the concurrency angle is worth anything at all):
286	 - WATCH/CAS soundness: a successful EXEC implies no Step by another client wrote a watched key between that client's WATCH and its EXEC — with the exemption that a key already logically expired at WATCH time does not dirty the CAS when later physically deleted (multi.c:397-406). That exemption is subtle enough that a model author will get it wrong, which is the point.
287	 - EXEC frozen clock: no ClockTick is observable between sub-commands of one EXEC, so a TTL cannot expire mid-transaction even though the surrounding schedule advances time.
288	 - Blocking fairness and the failed-wakeup case: FIFO among clients blocked on a key (blocked.c:428, 620-645), and a wakeup whose key now holds a mismatched type leaves the client blocked (blocked.c:658-670). A woken client's command is a *new* Step, ordered after the waker's, never nested inside it (blocked.c:752-769).
289	 - Blocking degradation: inside EXEC the same command is non-blocking and returns nil (multi.c:163-164, t_list.c:1364-1368) — i.e. one command with two contracts depending on schedule context.
290	 - Replication refinement across interleavings: for every schedule, replaying the ReplLog onto a fresh World reproduces the producer's Snapshot. This is where concurrency actually bites, because the log is produced by interleaved units and its MULTI-wrapping rule is schedule-dependent (server.c:4003-4041).
291	
292	ADVERSARIAL ENVIRONMENT. Active expiry is not a background detail to be assumed away — it is an adversarial Step the Prover inserts anywhere, which turns "a key may vanish at any moment" from prose into a hypothesis every other rule must survive. Eviction would be the same shape if it were ever brought in scope (it is not, in this plan).
293	
294	WHAT THIS BUYS AND WHAT IT DOES NOT. It gives machine-checked protocol-level concurrency contracts — the ones a Redis *user* can observe and depend on — over all interleavings of a small bounded schedule. It gives nothing about data races, memory ordering, or the IO-thread/bio-thread boundary. A reviewer must read the guarantee as "assuming Redis serialises commands as it claims, these client-visible concurrency contracts hold in the model", not as "Redis is thread-safe".
295	
296	BLOCKERS:
297	  ! GO/NO-GO, UNRESOLVED: nobody has shown the Certora Prover accepts a non-Soroban, non-Solana wasm. certoraRun is not installed on this machine; every stellar conf says "server": "production", meaning a cloud run needing credentials; and the one working example in the workspace imports `rule` from cvlr_soroban_derive (pausable_integrity.rs:3), a git dep on branch soroban-22.0.8 that is not vendored anywhere (no ~/.cargo/git checkout). I have proved the ARTIFACT is byte-shaped correctly — env::CVT_* imports, #[no_mangle] rules exported by name, 16.6 KB code section — but not that the tool ingests it. Settle this in day one by running the probe crate I already built (<scratchpad>/probe) through certoraRun. FALLBACK if it fails: wrap the same pure-Rust model in a trivial #[contract]/#[contractimpl] Soroban shell and use cvlr_soroban_derive::rule, putting it on the one path known to work. The cost is small precisely because the model is deliberately free of host dependencies — but it means the plan must not write 5,000 lines of model before testing this.
298	  ! Loop bounds cap the arity of every 'for all keys' statement. Model loops must be <= loop_iter or optimistic_loop:true silently discards the rest of the state space. stellar uses loop_iter 1-4 across all 56 confs. This forces K <= ~5 and forces unrolled fixed-size arrays over any growth.
299	  ! Scope discipline is the project's main failure mode. A reference model of Redis can absorb unlimited effort and become a re-implementation, which is not a specification. The scope boundary (standalone, one DB, no cluster, no modules, no scripting, maxmemory 0, appendonly no, no floats, no encodings, K<=5, 8-byte values) has to be written down and defended in review, not discovered.
300	  ! Version drift: this tree is 8.10-RC2 / v8.9.241 (00-RELEASENOTES:24-30), a pre-release whose surface includes brand-new features with no upstream analogue (compact hashes/HIMPORT, LMOVEM/BLMOVEM, the ARRAY type, BLESS, GCRA, SET IFEQ/IFDEQ) and whose object model differs from stock Redis (values are kvobj with the key and TTL embedded, object.h:31-66). Model the classic core, pin the exact binary in the difftest, and do not write the model from memory of upstream Redis.
301	  ! Ownership. A reference model only earns its keep if a difftest divergence is triaged as a bug report against the model, and a model-vs-C disagreement is escalated as a question to a Redis maintainer. Without a named owner on that loop the model silently rots into a second, wrong Redis, and the CVLR guarantee becomes a guarantee about a fiction.
302	  ! Prover-side cost of interleaving. The concurrency rules quantify over schedules; state-space growth in S and C is the obvious cliff. stellar's heaviest confs already need global_timeout 7200, smt_timeout 6000 and `-split false`. Expect S and C in the low single digits, and expect to discover the ceiling empirically rather than predict it.
303	
304	FIRST MILESTONE: A four-part go/no-go slice, roughly 1-2 weeks for one engineer. Stop and re-plan if part (c) fails.
305	
306	(a) MODEL. redis-cvlr/model with K=3 opaque keys, Str values as ([u8;8], len), an explicit clock, and SET/GET/DEL/EXISTS/TTL/PTTL/EXPIRE/PERSIST. One client, plus a ClockTick and an ActiveExpireTick step. Every fn annotated with its C line range. I have already written and compiled a skeleton of exactly this shape (<scratchpad>/probe/src/lib.rs) — it is ~90 lines.
307	
308	(b) SPEC. Five #[rule]s: set_then_get_returns_value; set_does_not_disturb_other_keys; get_never_returns_logically_expired_value; dbsize_does_not_filter_expired (the TRUE version — the empirical run above shows DBSIZE=1 for an elapsed key); and one deliberately FALSE rule that must produce a counterexample, so the pipeline is shown to be able to fail.
309	
310	(c) PROVER. Build with `RUSTFLAGS="-C strip=none -C link-arg=--import-undefined" cargo build --release --target wasm32-unknown-unknown --features certora`; wrap in certora_build.py + a .conf mirrored from packages/contract-utils/confs/pausable_non_panics.conf (loop_iter 3, optimistic_loop true, rule_sanity advanced so vacuity is caught). SUCCESS = four rules PASS, the fifth produces a counterexample, and sanity passes on all five. This is the step that answers the one unresolved blocker; do it before writing any more model.
311	
312	(d) CORRESPONDENCE. The concrete driver (own #[no_mangle] extern \"C-unwind\" CVT_* over a byte stream — verified working, 2000/2000 runs) drives >=10^6 randomly drawn step sequences, each replayed against /home/apanda/code/certora/redis/src/redis-server (already built here, 8.9.241 confirmed via INFO) over RESP with --save '' --appendonly no, DEBUG SET-ACTIVE-EXPIRE 0, databases 1. Compare every reply and the end-state Snapshot against a KEYS/TYPE/PTTL reconstruction. SUCCESS = zero divergences, AND at least one deliberately injected model bug (e.g. make DBSIZE filter expired keys) demonstrably caught — otherwise the harness is proving nothing.
313	
314	Deliverable of the milestone is not the spec; it is a one-page statement of exactly what the resulting guarantee does and does not cover, with parts (c) and (d) as its evidence, for a senior engineer to accept or reject before the expensive work starts.

        if not b&0x80: return r,p
