cat > /home/apanda/code/certora/CLAUDE.md <<'MDEOF'
# CVLR specification for Redis — working notes

## What this workspace is

Goal: **a CVLR specification for Redis focused on behavior visible to users, including
concurrency and correctness requirements.**

| path | what it is | role |
|---|---|---|
| `cvlr/` | Certora Verification Language for Rust, v0.6.1, 12 crates | the spec language |
| `stellar-contracts/` | OpenZeppelin Soroban contracts carrying ~484 real CVLR rules | the style corpus |
| `redis/` | Redis Open Source **8.9.241** (an 8.10 pre-release), C | the subject |
| `redis-cvlr/` | empty | where the spec goes |

Checkouts are jujutsu (`jj`), not git. `stellar-contracts` working copy is exactly
`port@origin` with no local edits — that is the branch to read.

## The central structural fact

**The Certora Prover, on this stack, ingests a `wasm32-unknown-unknown` module built by
`cargo`. CVLR is a Rust-only library. Redis is C.** There is no C frontend anywhere in
`cvlr/`. Everything else follows from this.

CVLR's entire semantics is a set of `extern "C"` `CVT_*` symbols the Prover interprets
symbolically (`cvlr/cvlr-asserts/src/core.rs:1-12`). `#[rule]` only adds `#[no_mangle]`,
prepends `cvlr_rule_location!()`, appends `cvlr_vacuity_check!()`
(`cvlr/cvlr-macros/src/lib.rs:20-36`) — the Prover finds rules by exported symbol name.

Build path used by every working example:

```
just build                       # cargo +nightly-2024-11-22 build --target=wasm32-unknown-unknown
                                 #   --release --features certora   (RUSTFLAGS="-C strip=none")
  -> target/wasm32-unknown-unknown/release/<crate>.wasm
certora_build.py                 # emits {project_directory, sources, executables, success, ...}
confs/<name>.conf                # {"build_script": "../certora_build.py", "rule": [...], ...}
```

### Verified in-session (2026-09-19), do not re-derive

- A plain Rust crate depending on local `cvlr` 0.6.1, built with
  `RUSTFLAGS="-C strip=none -C link-arg=--import-undefined" cargo build --release --target wasm32-unknown-unknown`,
  produces exactly the right artifact shape: imports `env::{CVT_assert, CVT_assume,
  CVT_sanity, CVT_rule_location, CVT_nondet_u64/i64/usize, CVT_calltrace_*}`, exports
  `memory` + each `#[rule]` symbol by name.
- **`-C link-arg=--import-undefined` is required on a modern nightly.** CVLR's
  `link(wasm_import_module="env")` is gated on `target_os = "none"`, but
  `wasm32-unknown-unknown` is `target_os = "unknown"`, so the `CVT_*` symbols are plain
  undefined and `rust-lld` errors without the flag. stellar never hit this because it
  pins `nightly-2024-11-22`, which allowed undefined wasm symbols by default.
- **`#[rule]` requires a `std` crate.** `cvlr_rule_location!()` expands to `std::file!()`
  (`cvlr/cvlr-log/src/core.rs:243-248`). In `#![no_std]` it will not compile. This is why
  all of stellar-contracts uses `cvlr_soroban_derive::rule` instead of `cvlr::rule`.
- Unmodified Redis C **does** compile to wasm: `zig cc --target=wasm32-wasi -mcpu=mvp`
  builds 104 of 127 `redis/src/*.c` with zero source changes; the 23 failures are exactly
  the files calling fork/exec/signal/pthread/dlopen. A module linking real `sds.c` and
  exporting a rule over `env::CVT_*` exists and parses correctly — but it still imports
  29 `wasi_snapshot_preview1` functions, so it is **not** freestanding yet.
- `redis/src/redis-server` is built here (8.9.241, `malloc=libc`) and usable as an oracle.
- `nightly-2024-11-22` is installed and active.
- **No Certora tooling is installed**: no `certoraRun`, no `certora_cli`, no `~/.certora`,
  `CERTORAKEY` unset. Nothing can be run through the Prover from this machine as-is.

## CVLR: what to use, what to avoid

Use the plain vocabulary — it is what all 484 rules in the corpus use:
`cvlr_assert!`, `cvlr_assume!`, `cvlr_assert_eq/ne/le/lt/ge/gt!`, the `_if` guarded
variants, `clog!`, `nondet::<T>()`, `#[derive(Nondet, CvlrLog)]`, `#[rule]`.

**Avoid / know the traps:**

- `cvlr_rules!`, `cvlr_invariant_rules!`, `cvlr_rule_for_spec!` expand to a call to
  `cvlr_impl_rule!`, which **CVLR does not define** — the user must supply it. It is
  defined only inside cvlr's own tests (`cvlr-spec/tests/test_spec.rs:1138`). Zero uses
  across all five stellar-contracts branches. Usable if you write the ~12-line macro, but
  you would be the first.
- The whole `cvlr-spec` DSL (`CvlrFormula`/`CvlrSpec`/`CvlrLemma`/`cvlr_def_predicate!`)
  has **zero production usage**: stellar pins `cvlr = "0.4.0"`, and cvlr-spec first shipped
  in 0.6.0.
- `chandra/invariant-macros@origin` in stellar-contracts looks like a better parametric
  invariant idiom (`CvlrProp` + `base_*` + `impl_cvlr_rule_for_bases!`). **It is WIP and
  does not work**: its workspace lists a `certora/merkle_distributor` member that does not
  exist (so cargo fails at manifest resolution), no `.conf` references any generated rule
  name, and several properties are stubbed `cvlr_assert!(false)` with the real `cvlr_inv!`
  formulations commented out. Read it for the *idea*; do not treat it as a working pattern.
- The `macro_rules!` spec macros take the context type as `$ctx: ident`, not a type — so
  no generics, no paths, no references. Context structs must be bare, non-generic, in scope.
- A two-state predicate's single-state methods are poisoned with `cvlr_assert!(false);
  panic!()`. Handing one to `cvlr_invar_spec!` (whose `check_ensures` calls `.assert()`)
  fails unconditionally. **Invariants must be single-state.**
- `CvlrLemma::apply()` is the dual of `verify_with_context()` — it *asserts* requires and
  *assumes* ensures. Mixing them up turns an obligation into an unsound assumption.
- The optional description argument to `cvlr_assert!`/`cvlr_assume!` is parsed and
  **silently discarded**. Use `clog!` for anything you want in a counterexample.
- `cvlr_assume!` emits no location info; `cvlr_assert!` does.
- CVLR has **no f32/f64 at all** — no `Nondet`, no `CvlrLog`, no conversion. ZSET scores,
  `INCRBYFLOAT`, GEO, HLL cannot be modeled directly.
- CVLR has **no threads, locks, memory ordering, happens-before, or scheduler primitive**
  anywhere in its ~15k lines. Concurrency must be reified into an explicit abstract state.
- `nondet::<bool>()` costs a full symbolic u64; `#[derive(Nondet)]` on an enum draws a u64
  per value. Nondet-heavy contexts get expensive fast.
- **Draw bounded values, do not assume them.** `nondet::<u64>() % K` keeps concrete-mode
  fuzzing viable; `cvlr_assume!(k < K)` rejects essentially every concrete run.

## Spec idioms from the corpus (`stellar-contracts`, branch `port`)

Taxonomy — one file per category, `<subject>_<category>.rs` under `src/**/specs/`:

| category | shape |
|---|---|
| `_integrity` | relate post-state to pre-state for one function; no invariant assumed |
| `_invariants` | one rule per (invariant × mutating function), via `assume_pre_X` / `assert_post_X` helpers |
| `_panics` | end in `cvlr_assert!(false)`; passes iff the call always traps |
| `_non_panics` | end in `cvlr_assert!(true)`; need `"prover_args": ["-trapAsAssert", "true"]` |
| `_contract` | not rules — a `#[contract]` harness exposing entry points |

Header comment convention (keep it, it is how properties stay reviewable):

```rust
// property: P-XX. <Name>.
// description: <what it says>
// status: verified | vacuity issue! | ...
```

**Rule names are global across the compiled wasm.** The corpus avoids collisions with
manual prefixes (`fungible_`, `wt_`, `sl_`, `nft_`). Pick a prefix convention up front.

**Corpus antipatterns — do not copy:**
- `cvlr_satisfy` is imported in 51 of 55 spec files and invoked **zero** times.
- `fungible_invariants.rs:110` places an assumption *after* the mutating call, which can
  silently exclude the counterexample the rule exists to find. The file admits the
  invariant is unprovable as written.
- `vault_64_solvency.rs` declares the vault's headline safety property in a comment,
  defines `assume_pre_solvency`, and contains **zero** `#[rule]`s. It is assumed, never proved.
- `math_rounding` is verified for a *simplified* program (`virtual_offset` hardcoded to 1).
- `.conf` files are **not strict JSON** (trailing commas); `global_timeout` is sometimes a
  string, sometimes an int. Confs and source drift — one conf names a rule that does not exist.
- Bounded nondet containers (MAX=5) combined with `loop_iter: 1..4` and
  `optimistic_loop: true` mean coverage is **narrower than it looks**: `optimistic_loop`
  *assumes away* every execution exceeding the bound.

## Redis 8.9.241: facts a spec author must not get wrong

**This is not stock Redis 7/8 OSS. Do not write the spec from memory of upstream Redis.**
The value is a `kvobj` embedding the key string *and* the expire timestamp
(`object.h:31-66`); `db->keys` and `db->expires` are `kvstore`s holding the *same*
pointers. There are new types (`OBJ_ARRAY`), encodings (`LISTPACK_EX`, `TMPL_LP`),
policies (`volatile-lrm`), and commands (`DELEX`, `HSETEX`, `SET IFEQ`, `BLESS`, `AR*`).

- **"A key past its TTL is never observed" is FALSE as stated.** It holds only for the
  `lookupKey*` path. `DBSIZE`, `INFO keyspace`, `DEBUG OBJECT/DIGEST`, `RANDOMKEY` after
  100 tries, and `HLEN` all report logically-expired keys. The correct statement names
  the lookup path explicitly.
- **Expiry depends on who is asking.** Master normal client: gone, and deleted. Read-only
  replica: gone, but *not* deleted. `CLIENT_MASTER` link: **valid**. Any `exists`
  predicate must be parameterized by (role, client flags, loading, pause state).
- Lazy expiry uses `now > when`; active expiry uses `now >= when`. They disagree at
  exactly `t == expire`. Hash-field TTL uses a third comparison.
- **The atomic unit is the execution unit, not the command** (`server.execution_nesting`,
  `server.c:1420-1432`): one top-level `call()`, one whole `EXEC`, or one whole script.
  Propagation flushes only at nesting 0. Modeling atomicity per-command gives wrong
  replication predicates.
- **Redis 8.9 is not single-threaded.** Up to 128 IO threads, each with its own event
  loop, do full RESP parsing, command lookup, arity checks and socket writes. They never
  execute commands (`networking.c:3927-3934`). The correct invariant is narrower: *command
  execution and keyspace mutation* are single-threaded on the main thread. `io-threads` is
  an IMMUTABLE config and defaults to 1.
- **Script atomicity is conditionally broken**: past `busy-reply-threshold` ms a script
  re-enters the event loop and other clients' commands run between its `redis.call()`s.
- **There is no rollback anywhere.** `MULTI/EXEC` is isolation, not rollback — only
  queue-time errors abort. A partially-applied script cannot be killed.
- `EXEC` has three distinct outcomes that must not be conflated: `-EXECABORT` (queue-time
  error), RESP null array (WATCH invalidated), and a normal array whose elements may be errors.
- **WATCH is key-name-based, not value-based** — writing a key back to its original value
  still invalidates. One exemption: a key already logically expired at WATCH time.
- **`FLUSHALL SYNC` is silently upgraded to ASYNC** (`db.c:1370-1373`): the keyspace empties
  immediately but the caller's `+OK` waits for the bio lazy-free queue. Not inside MULTI/script.
- **Blocking commands have two contracts**: inside MULTI/script they do not block —
  `BLPOP key 0` returns a null array.
- `server.dirty` is a hand-maintained global counter across ~135 scattered increments, and
  it alone decides propagation. `(keyspace changed) <=> (dirty delta > 0)` is arguably the
  single highest-value invariant to formalize.
- **Durability is a property of the event loop, not the command.** `flushAppendOnlyFile`
  runs at `server.c:2070`, `handleClientsWithPendingWrites` at `server.c:2117`. That
  ordering *is* the contract, and it only means "fsynced before reply" under
  `appendfsync always`.
- `WAIT` counts replicas that *applied* to memory, not fsynced, and says nothing about
  which replicas are eligible for election.
- **Eviction is user-visible nondeterminism**: `performEvictions()` runs synchronously
  *before every command* when `maxmemory != 0`, can delete keys across all DBs, and is
  explicitly approximate/randomized. Either pin `maxmemory=0` or model arbitrary key loss.
- **A read command can be a write**: `SCAN` and `GET` lazily expire and propagate `DEL`s.
- `cluster_asm.c` (~3,900 lines, Atomic Slot Migration) is new in this version and is a
  much larger concurrency surface than classic cluster. Treat as a separate project.

### Redis's own specification artifacts (reuse these)

- `redis/src/commands/*.json` — 464 entries, 442 with `command_flags`, 433 with
  `reply_schema`, 240 with `key_specs`. **Disclaimed as non-authoritative** by
  `src/commands/README.md`; 7 have INCOMPLETE key specs and 3 (including `SET`) have
  VARIABLE_FLAGS. Good for a coverage matrix and type signatures; supplies no
  preconditions, error contracts, or cross-command invariants.
- `tests/` (tcl) — the executable oracle. Three directly reusable predicates:
  `DEBUG DIGEST` / `debug_digest_value` (state equality), `csvdump` (canonical
  serialization), and `attach_to_replication_stream` / `assert_replication_stream` (the
  exact emitted command sequence). The last is the mechanised form of every propagation
  property. Note the suite runs under `tests/assets/default.conf`, **not** production defaults.
- `MANIFESTO` states explicit non-guarantees; `redis.conf` comments document the
  durability/consistency tradeoffs.
- There is **no** in-tree linearizability checker, Jepsen-style harness, or any formal-methods
  artifact of any kind. Any real concurrency property needs new machinery.

## Scope discipline

The main failure mode for this project is unbounded scope. Any spec must write down and
defend a fence, e.g.: standalone (cluster off), one DB, `maxmemory=0`, a named fsync mode,
no modules/Lua/ACL, no floats, K ≤ 5 keys, small client and step counts. `src/config.c`
defines 222 configs of which roughly 40 change observable semantics rather than performance.

Bounded means bounded: with `optimistic_loop: true`, "for all keys" means "for all three
keys", and a passing rule means "no counterexample within the bound".

## Open gate

Neither candidate path can be validated from this machine — there is no Prover access here.
The first milestone of *any* approach should be getting one trivial rule to a verdict
(and confirming a deliberately false rule produces a real counterexample), before writing
significant model or harness code.

## Decisions (2026-09-19)

**Target: Rust model + property catalog.** `redis-cvlr/` becomes a Cargo workspace holding
(a) `PROPERTIES.md`, a numbered catalog in the corpus `P-XX` style, every entry anchored to
cited Redis C; and (b) an explicit abstract Redis state machine in Rust with CVLR `#[rule]`s
over it. The C tree is read-only reference material and an oracle process — never compiled,
never linked, never seen by the Prover. State this on page one of `PROPERTIES.md`: **what is
proven is proven about the model.**

**Prover access is obtainable but not on a known timeline.** Two consequences, both binding:
1. The catalog must stand alone as a reviewable artifact. Its value does not depend on a
   Prover run, and it is the deliverable that lands first.
2. **Differential fuzzing is primary evidence, not a secondary leg.** Build the dual-mode
   driver early: our own `#[no_mangle] extern "C-unwind" CVT_*` over a seeded byte stream
   lets the *same* `#[rule]` functions execute concretely, replaying each drawn step
   sequence against the already-built `redis/src/redis-server` over RESP and comparing
   replies plus a `csvdump`-shaped snapshot. This is the discipline that forces
   "draw bounded values, never `cvlr_assume!` them".

**Scope, first iteration: keyspace + expiry.** Strings, `DEL`/`EXISTS`/`TYPE`, the full
`EXPIRE` family, and the expiry-visibility split. Pinned config: standalone, cluster off,
one DB, `maxmemory=0`, `appendonly no`, `save ''`, no modules/Lua/ACL, no floats, K ≤ 5
opaque keys, 8-byte string values, active expiry modeled as an explicit adversarial step.

**Concurrency: all four dimensions are in the catalog; only some are encodable.** The user
selected all four knowing CVLR cannot discharge two of them. Do not silently drop those —
carry each as a catalog entry with an explicit status and the tool that *could* discharge it.

| dimension | catalog status |
|---|---|
| Client-visible contracts (atomicity boundary, WATCH/CAS, blocking FIFO, expiry visibility, MULTI framing) | **encodable** — reify the schedule into the abstract state, quantify over bounded interleavings |
| Replication / durability anomalies | **partly encodable** — safety over an effect log; all liveness (eventual convergence) is not expressible |
| Thread-safety / data races | **NOT expressible in CVLR** — record the requirement, cite the specific race (`multi.c` `REDIS_NO_SANITIZE`, IO threads dereferencing live keyspace `robj`s), name TSan / a memory-model tool as what would discharge it |
| Cluster / atomic slot migration | **deferred** — record the surface and the new `-TRYAGAIN Slot is being trimmed` contract; `cluster_asm.c` is its own project |

A catalog entry that names an unmet requirement and why it is unmet is more useful than an
omission. Every entry carries: ID, English statement, CVLR form (`integrity` | `invariant` |
`panic` | `non_panic` | `interleaving` | `satisfy` | `NOT-EXPRESSIBLE`), the Redis `file:line`
evidence, scope caveats (role, caller kind, maxmemory, cluster), and a status.
MDEOF

Here's where things stand.

Done

CLAUDE.md at /home/apanda/code/certora/CLAUDE.md (268 lines) — workspace map, the build pipeline, CVLR's usable vocabulary and its traps, the corpus spec taxonomy and its antipatterns, the Redis-8.9.241-specific facts a spec author must not get wrong, scope discipline, and your decisions.

Examination, via 11 agents across CVLR, the spec corpus, and six Redis subsystems. Things I verified myself rather than relaying:

  • A Rust crate against local cvlr 0.6.1 builds to wasm32 with exactly the right shape — imports env::{CVT_assert, CVT_assume, CVT_sanity, CVT_rule_location, CVT_nondet_*, CVT_calltrace_*}, exports memory plus each #[rule] by name. I parsed the section tables. The Rust path's toolchain works.
  • Two real gotchas on that path: -C link-arg=--import-undefined is now required (CVLR's wasm_import_module is gated on target_os = "none", but wasm32-unknown-unknown is "unknown"), and #[rule] needs a std crate because cvlr_rule_location! expands to std::file!().
  • The C path is further along than I expected — a module built from unmodified redis/src/sds.c exporting a rule over env::CVT_* exists on disk. But it still imports 29 WASI functions, so it isn't freestanding yet.
  • No Certora tooling here at all. nightly-2024-11-22 — the toolchain stellar-contracts pins — is installed and active.

Two corrections to things I said earlier

You were right to push on the invariant macros: that branch doesn't build (its workspace names a member directory that doesn't exist), no .conf runs any generated rule, and several properties are stubbed cvlr_assert!(false). I've recorded it in CLAUDE.md as an idea to learn from, not a pattern to copy.

And a claim from my survey that turned out overstated: cvlr_rules! isn't unusable, it just needs a ~12-line cvlr_impl_rule! you supply yourself. Still no production example anywhere — you'd be first.

What your answers change

Prover access being uncertain is the load-bearing one. It promotes differential fuzzing against the redis-server already built here from a nice-to-have to the primary evidence, and it means the catalog has to stand on its own. That in turn forces a style rule from the start: draw bounded values (nondet::<u64>() % K), never cvlr_assume! them — otherwise the same rules can't execute concretely.

On concurrency, you picked all four including the two I flagged. Taking that as deliberate: all four go in the catalog, with thread-safety carried as an explicit NOT-EXPRESSIBLE entry naming the specific races and pointing at TSan, and cluster recorded but deferred.

The critic agent is still running — it's verifying the load-bearing claims from the other ten and may flag contradictions. I'll fold anything material into CLAUDE.md, then start on PROPERTIES.md and the model skeleton for keyspace + expiry unless you'd rather redirect.

1	RECOMMENDED: Hybrid, catalog-first, with the C path demoted to a time-boxed spike that is gated on an answer from Certora — and with nothing committed until the user answers Q1 and Q2.
2	
3	Direction, in priority order:
4	
5	(1) WRITE THE PROPERTY CATALOG FIRST, before any Rust. `/home/apanda/code/certora/redis-cvlr/PROPERTIES.md`, numbered P-NN in the stellar header style (`// property:` / `// description:` / `// status:`, as in packages/access/src/ownable/specs/ownable_invariants.rs:11-13), each entry carrying: the English statement, the intended CVLR form (integrity | invariant | panic | non-panic | interleaving | satisfy | NOT-EXPRESSIBLE), the exact redis file:line evidence, the scope conditions (role, caller kind, cluster, maxmemory, config), and `status: unproven`. This is approach 3's core idea and it is the right one for three reasons: it is literally what the user asked for ("a CVLR specification ... focused on behavior visible to users"); it is reviewable by a Redis engineer who never reads Rust; and it is the only deliverable whose value survives a "no" on the prover question. Note that stellar-contracts has no such document at all — 41 of its 55 property headers say the placeholder `P-XX` — so this is the piece the reference corpus is missing, not a copy of it.
6	
7	(2) STRUCTURE THE ABSTRACT STATE AROUND THE USER'S OWN PHRASE. "Behavior visible to users" forces two design decisions that must be made on day one or retrofitted painfully: the keyspace must be PHYSICAL (`stored`) with visibility as a DERIVED, role-and-config-parameterized function, because DBSIZE/INFO/DEBUG and GET/EXISTS provably disagree (I measured dbsize=1 with KEYS returning [] on the built binary); and the replication/effect log must be part of the observable state, because that is the only way "atomic" becomes a checkable predicate rather than an adjective, and it gives every such property a free differential twin via the existing `assert_replication_stream` harness (tests/test_helper.tcl:787-864).
8	
9	(3) ADOPT APPROACH 1'S DUAL-MODE DISCIPLINE AS THE FIDELITY ARGUMENT. The model must be written so the same `#[rule]` bodies run (a) symbolically under the Prover and (b) concretely against a hand-written `#[no_mangle] extern "C-unwind"` implementation of the CVT_* ABI driven by a byte stream, replayed command-for-command against the already-built `/home/apanda/code/certora/redis/src/redis-server` over RESP. This is the only proposed answer to "why should anyone believe the model is Redis", and it is cheap because the binary already exists. It imposes one style rule from day one: DRAW bounded values (`nondet::<u64>() % K`) rather than FILTER them (`cvlr_assume!(k < K)`), or the concrete mode rejects essentially every run.
10	
11	(4) TIME-BOX THE C PATH AS A SPIKE, DO NOT BUILD ON IT. Approach 2's work is genuinely impressive and its artifact is real, but the honest status is: it proves clang can compile Redis's data-structure tier to wasm and emit the right import/export shape; it does not prove the Prover accepts it, and the module still drags 29 wasi imports. Its unique value — proofs about the ACTUAL shipped C, plus near-free UB detection by routing `__ubsan_handle_*` into `CVT_assert(0)`, plus meaningful `-trapAsAssert` memory-safety rules — is large enough that the spike is worth one engineer-day IF and ONLY IF Certora confirms a language-agnostic wasm or LLVM frontend. Pursuing it on spec is a research project (approach 2's own word) with low confidence.
12	
13	(5) BE EXPLICIT IN THE CATALOG ABOUT WHAT CONCURRENCY CAN AND CANNOT MEAN. CVLR supplies no concurrency vocabulary whatsoever, so client-visible interleaving must be reified into the state (an explicit `Step` sequence drawn nondeterministically, a `block_seq` ghost field as the FIFO witness) and memory-level races must be catalogued as NOT-EXPRESSIBLE with citations. Writing that distinction down is itself a deliverable, and it is the thing most likely to prevent a misunderstanding about what was promised.
14	
15	Two concrete targets I would put at the front of the catalog because verification found them and they are exactly "visible to users": the six-way conditional structure of expiry deletion on a MASTER (src/db.c:3003-3062, which the survey got wrong), and the `break` at src/multi.c:415 inside the `wk->expired` branch of `touchWatchedKey` — it exits the whole watcher list rather than `continue`ing, so when a key that was logically expired at WATCH time is later overwritten and is still present, watchers appended after that entry (`listAddNodeTail`, multi.c:332) appear never to receive CLIENT_DIRTY_CAS. I have confirmed the control flow and the list ordering but not constructed the end-to-end scenario, so treat it as a strong candidate, not a finding. If it holds, it is a WATCH/EXEC soundness bug and the single best advertisement for doing this work at all.
16	
17	RATIONALE: The user asked for a specification, not a proof, and asked to begin by examining code and asking questions — so the recommendation has to be robust to the one fact that dominates everything: nothing in this workspace can currently be verified, because no Certora tooling is installed and every reference .conf targets a cloud prover behind credentials. Under that constraint, any plan whose value is contingent on the Prover accepting an artifact is a gamble; a plan whose primary deliverable is a cited, reviewable property catalog is not.
18	
19	Catalog-first also directly serves "behavior visible to users". My own probing showed the naive statement of the headline property is false in this tree (DBSIZE reports a logically-expired key while KEYS hides it and EXISTS deletes it), and reading src/db.c:3003-3062 showed the survey's version of the expiry rule is wrong on a master in at least four additional ways. Those are catalog-level errors — they would have been baked into a model and silently "verified" if code had been written first. A catalog reviewed against cited C by someone who knows Redis catches them for free, before any prover run.
20	
21	The Rust-model target is chosen over the C target not because it is better — it is strictly weaker, since it proves nothing about the shipped C — but because it is the only one with a verified end-to-end artifact today. I parsed probe.wasm myself: pure `env::CVT_*` imports, rule symbols exported by name, exactly the shape stellar ships. The C artifact still carries 29 wasi imports and its author says so. Between a weaker target that demonstrably produces the right bytes and a stronger target that does not yet, the right move is to start on the weaker one while asking the question that could promote the stronger one.
22	
23	Approach 1's differential-testing leg is what makes the weaker target defensible rather than a fiction. A Rust reference model with no correspondence evidence is just a second, wrong Redis; the same model checked against the real binary on every commit is a specification with an empirical fidelity claim. redis-server is already built here, so this costs a harness, not a build.
24	
25	Finally, the concurrency half of the request needs its meaning fixed before any estimate is credible. If the user means client-visible interleaving, the reified-schedule encoding works and the catalog can state real theorems about WATCH, execution-unit atomicity and blocking FIFO. If they mean data races, no amount of engineering inside CVLR delivers it and they should be told immediately rather than after a milestone. That is why Q2 ranks just behind Q1, and why the recommendation is a direction with two gates rather than a plan.

39	 - `cvlr_rules!` usability. Survey agent 1 calls it 'UNUSABLE out of the box'; approach 3 calls that an overstatement and supplies a ~12-line `cvlr_impl_rule!`. VERDICT: both facts are right, the characterization is agent 1's error. VERDICT: approach 1's result is correct, their method description is incomplete in a way that would mislead the next agent. `DEBUG` is protected by default in 8.9.241, so `DEBUG SET-ACTIVE-EXPIRE 0` errors and the active cycle reaps the key within ~100ms. Re-running with `--enable-debug-command yes` I reproduce it exactly: dbsize=1, `db0:keys=1,expires=1,avg_ttl=0,subexpiry=0`, `KEYS *` returns `[]` while dbsize STAYS 1, and then dbsize drops to 0. Two additions neither approach reported: it is `EXISTS` (not only `GET`) that triggers the lazy delete, and `HLEN`=2 while `HGETALL` returns one field, confirming src/t_hash.c:5596-5603 live.

Minor, but it is the kind of drift that makes an agent distrust the rest of the survey.

VERDICT: agents 5/6/approach 3 are right, and the difference is observable, not academic — I confirmed src/server.c:4005-4019 wraps a unit in MULTI/EXEC iff it emitted >1 op and the direct command lacks CMD_TOUCHES_ARBITRARY_KEYS. A spec built on 'command = atomic' cannot state the headline atomicity property correctly.

51	 - VERSION TRAP. Local cvlr is 0.6.1 (cvlr/Cargo.toml:19). stellar-contracts pins `cvlr = "0.4.0"` from crates.io (stellar-contracts/Cargo.toml:73-75) and its Cargo.lock contains ZERO cvlr entries. 52	 - BUILD RECIPE THAT PRODUCES A VERIFIED-CORRECT ARTIFACT (Rust path). From a std crate depending on cvlr by path: `RUSTFLAGS="-C strip=none -C link-arg=--import-undefined" cargo build --release --target wasm32-unknown-unknown`. `-C strip=none` is mandatory (rules are located by exported symbol name). `--import-undefined` is needed on modern nightly because cvlr's `link(wasm_import_module="env")` is gated on `all(target_family="wasm", target_os="none")` and wasm32-unknown-unknown has target_os="unknown"; stellar avoids this only by pinning nightly-2024-11-22 (stellar-contracts/rust-toolchain.toml:2). Verified result: imports are ONLY `env::CVT_*` (9 symbols incl. CVT_rule_location), exports are `memory` plus each `#[no_mangle]` rule name.
53	 - cvlr's own `#[rule]` DOES work — but only in a `std` crate. It expands to exactly three things (cvlr/cvlr-macros/src/lib.rs:20-36): push `#[no_mangle]`, insert `cvlr::log::cvlr_rule_location!()` as statement 0, append `cvlr::cvlr_vacuity_check!()`. `cvlr_rule_location!` names `std::file!()` (cvlr-log/src/core.rs:243-248), so it fails in `#![no_std]` — that is the ONLY reason stellar uses `cvlr_soroban_derive::rule` instead. A std redis-cvlr crate can use `cvlr::rule` directly; this was built and byte-verified.
54	 - DO NOT USE `cvlr_rules!` / `cvlr_invariant_rules!` / `cvlr_rule_for_spec!` without first defining `cvlr_impl_rule!` YOURSELF. They expand to `cvlr_impl_rule!{name, spec, base}` (cvlr/cvlr-macros/src/rule_for_spec.rs:97) and CVLR never defines it — the only two definitions in the entire tree are test stubs commented 'this would be provided by the user in real usage' (cvlr-macros/tests/expand/test_cvlr_rule_for_spec.rs:5 and cvlr-spec/tests/test_spec.rs:1138). It receives (ident, CvlrSpec expr, base fn ident) and must build a pre-state, call `spec.assume_requires(&pre)`, run the base, then call `spec.check_ensures(&post, &pre)` (cvlr-spec/src/spec.rs:32-78). Every real example in this workspace instead writes `#[rule] pub fn ...` by hand — prefer that.
55	 - CVLR MACRO GOTCHAS, ALL VERIFIED: (1) the optional trailing `$desc: literal` on cvlr_assert!/cvlr_assume!/cvlr_satisfy! and on every comparison macro is parsed and SILENTLY DISCARDED (cvlr-asserts/src/core.rs:69-83) — use `clog!` for anything you want in a counterexample. (2) `cvlr_assume!` emits no `add_loc!()`, so a failing path never points at the assumption that constrained it. (3) The macro_rules spec macros take the context type as `$ctx: ident` (cvlr-spec/src/macros.rs:51,129) — no generics, no paths; context structs must be bare, non-generic, in scope. (4) `cvlr_def_states_predicate!` POISONS its single-state methods with `cvlr_assert!(false); panic!()` (macros.rs:155-166), so an invariant handed to `cvlr_invar_spec!` MUST be single-state or it fails unconditionally. (5) `cvlr_and!` handles only 2..=6 arguments. (6) `CvlrLemma::apply()` is the logical DUAL of `verify()`: it ASSERTS requires and ASSUMES ensures (spec.rs:311-314).
56	 - NO FLOATS ANYWHERE IN CVLR. There is no `Nondet`, no `CvlrLog` and no conversion for f32/f64 (complete impl list at cvlr-nondet/src/scalars.rs:84-97). ZSET scores, INCRBYFLOAT, GEO and HyperLogLog cannot be modelled directly — use integer surrogates and say so, or exclude.
57	 - DRAW, DO NOT FILTER, when bounding nondeterminism: write `nondet::<u64>() % K`, not `cvlr_assume!(k < K)`. The assume form makes the same rule bodies unusable in a concrete/fuzzing driver (it rejects essentially every run) and it prunes state rather than shaping it. This is also the idiom stellar uses (packages/accounts/src/smart_account/specs/nondet.rs:16-39, MAX=5 modulo loop). Note `nondet::<bool>()` costs a full symbolic u64 (`cvlr_nondet_u64() > 0`, scalars.rs:85).
58	 - RULE NAMES ARE GLOBAL SYMBOLS across the whole compiled wasm. Adopt a prefix convention from day one (stellar does this manually: `fungible_`, `wt_`, `sl_`, `rwa_`, `nft_`). For redis-cvlr use a per-area prefix such as `expire_`, `string_`, `multi_`, `blocked_`.
59	 - .CONF FILES ARE NOT STRICT JSON — several stellar confs have trailing commas inside arrays, and timeouts are inconsistently typed (`"global_timeout": "7200"` string in packages/access/confs/access_control_invariants.conf:26 vs `7200` int in packages/tokens/confs/vault_64_invariants.conf:6). Use a relaxed/JSON5 parser. Start from packages/contract-utils/confs/pausable_non_panics.conf (minimal) or packages/tokens/confs/vault_64_invariants.conf (richest).
60	 - `loop_iter` (1-4 in every stellar conf) combined with `optimistic_loop: true` (30 of 56 confs) ASSUMES AWAY every execution that iterates past the bound — stated explicitly at packages/accounts/src/policies/specs/spending_limit_integrity.rs:27-30. Any 'for all keys' claim is really 'for all <= loop_iter keys'. Never report a bounded result as unbounded. `rule_sanity: "advanced"` is what catches the resulting vacuity; `#[rule]` already appends `cvlr_vacuity_check!()`.
61	 - `-trapAsAssert true` is REQUIRED for any non-panic rule (`prover_args`). Without it a Rust trap is treated as an assumed-away path and `cvlr_assert!(true)` holds vacuously. Panic rules end in `cvlr_assert!(false)` and PASS iff the call always traps — the inversion is documented at packages/access/src/ownable/specs/ownable_panics.rs:15-16.
62	 - STELLAR FILE TAXONOMY TO COPY: `_integrity` (post vs pre for one function, no invariant assumed), `_invariants` (one `assume_pre_X` / `assert_post_X` pair, one rule per (invariant x mutating-fn)), `_panics`, `_non_panics`, plus `_contract`/`helper.rs`/`nondet.rs`/`ghosts.rs` support files. The style model is packages/access/src/ownable/specs/ (complete 7-file quartet, real P-NN numbers). The smallest complete example is packages/contract-utils/src/pausable/specs/ (10 rules). DO NOT use packages/tokens/src/rwa/specs/ as a reference — it is self-labelled `status: violated` and `// DUPLICATE CODE`.
63	 - DO NOT COPY packages/tokens/src/fungible/specs/fungible_invariants.rs:110 — it places an assumption AFTER the mutating call, which can silently exclude exactly the counterexample the rule was written to find. The file itself admits the invariant is unprovable as written (lines 12-13).
64	 - `cvlr_satisfy!` is imported in 51 of 55 stellar spec files and invoked ZERO times. It is cargo-cult boilerplate in the use-statement; do not copy it blindly, and be aware there is no local precedent for how the Prover reports satisfy outcomes.
65	 - REDIS IS NOT STOCK REDIS. src/version.h says 8.9.241 = Redis 8.10-RC2 (00-RELEASENOTES:24-30). The value stored under a key is a `kvobj` embedding the key string AND the expire timestamp as object metadata (src/object.h:31-66); `db->keys` and `db->expires` are kvstores holding the SAME pointers. There are new types (OBJ_ARRAY), encodings (LISTPACK_EX, TMPL_LP, TMPL_ARRAY, SLICED_ARRAY), policies (volatile-lrm, allkeys-lrm) and commands (DELEX, INCREX, HSETEX, HIMPORT, BLESS, GCRA, AR*). NEVER write a spec from memory of upstream Redis — read the file and cite file:line.
66	 - THE HEADLINE EXPIRY PROPERTY, STATED CORRECTLY. 'A key past its TTL is never observed' is FALSE as an unconditional invariant. It holds only for the `lookupKey*` path. `dbSize` returns `kvstoreSize(db->keys)` with NO expiry filter (src/db.c:3148-3173); so does INFO keyspace (src/server.c:7272-7283), DEBUG OBJECT and DEBUG DIGEST. `KEYS` filters with `keyIsExpired` and does NOT delete (db.c:1638-1645); `GET`/`EXISTS` go through `lookupKey*` and DO delete. `HLEN` deliberately counts logically-expired hash fields while HGETALL subtracts them (t_hash.c:5596-5603).
67	 - EXPIRY IS SIX-WAY CONDITIONAL, NOT TWO-WAY. On a plain standalone MASTER, `expireIfNeeded` (src/db.c:3003-3062) can return without deleting via: `asmIsKeyInTrimJob` (3011-3019), `flags & EXPIRE_ALLOW_ACCESS_EXPIRED` (3022), `server.allow_access_expired` inside keyIsExpired (2948), `!confAllowsExpireDel()` gated on the `lazyexpire-nested-arbitrary-keys` config (3049-3051), `flags & EXPIRE_AVOID_DELETE_EXPIRED` (3055), and `isPausedActionsWithUpdate(PAUSE_ACTION_EXPIRE)` (3062) — in ADDITION to the replica and CLIENT_MASTER cases (3043-3045). Any spec of 'exists' must be parameterized by (role, client flags, loading, pause state, config).
68	 - TWO DIFFERENT EXPIRY COMPARISONS COEXIST, ONE MILLISECOND APART. Lazy: `return now > when;` (src/db.c:2951, strict). Active: `if (now < kvobjGetExpire(kv)) return 0;` i.e. expires at now >= when (src/expire.c:40-41). Hash fields expire when `expiredAt < now` (t_hash.c:2118-2120). At exactly t == expire, a lazy lookup sees VALID while the active cycle deletes.
69	 - THE ATOMIC UNIT IS THE EXECUTION UNIT, NOT THE COMMAND. `server.execution_nesting` (src/server.c:1420-1432) also freezes `server.cmd_time_snapshot` at nesting 0, which is why an EXEC or a script sees one consistent clock. Propagation, post-notification jobs and replica flushes happen only at nesting 0 (src/server.c:4060-4075). A unit is wrapped in MULTI/EXEC on the wire iff it emitted >1 op AND the direct command lacks CMD_TOUCHES_ARBITRARY_KEYS (src/server.c:4005-4019; only SCAN and RANDOMKEY carry that flag). Lazy-expire DELs are deliberately NOT wrapped (oracles: tests/unit/expire.tcl:809 and :830; contrast tests/unit/multi.tcl:398 vs :412).
70	 - COMMANDS EXECUTE ONLY ON THE MAIN THREAD. IO threads parse fully but refuse to execute and hand the client back (src/networking.c:3929-3935); clients that cannot be handled off-main are pinned (src/iothread.c:286-292). `io-threads` is IMMUTABLE and defaults to 1. Modelling one step as atomic is an AXIOM justified by these citations, not a theorem — say so.
71	 - WATCH IS VALUE-BLIND. `touchWatchedKey` never inspects the value (src/multi.c:387-425); writing a key back to its original value still sets CLIENT_DIRTY_CAS. It carries `REDIS_NO_SANITIZE("thread")` because IO threads read other bits of the same `c->flags` word. SUSPECTED BUG WORTH INVESTIGATING: the `break` at src/multi.c:415, inside the `wk->expired` branch, exits the ENTIRE watcher list rather than `continue`ing, so when a key that was logically expired at WATCH time is later overwritten and is still present, watchers appended after that entry (`listAddNodeTail`, multi.c:332) appear never to be dirtied. Control flow and list ordering confirmed; end-to-end reachability NOT yet constructed.
72	 - RUNNING THE REAL SERVER AS AN ORACLE. `/home/apanda/code/certora/redis/src/redis-server` is already built (8.9.241, 17MB). redis-cli was NOT built — speak RESP directly (a ~15-line python socket client works). CRITICAL: DEBUG is a PROTECTED command by default in this version, so `DEBUG SET-ACTIVE-EXPIRE 0` silently ERRORS and active expiry reaps your test keys within ~100ms, producing wrong answers. Always launch with `--enable-debug-command yes --save '' --appendonly no --logfile /tmp/r.log`. Measured under that config: `SET k v PX 50`, wait, then DBSIZE=1 and `db0:keys=1,expires=1`; `KEYS *` returns [] and DBSIZE STAYS 1; `EXISTS k` returns 0 and DBSIZE drops to 0.

=== expireIfNeeded (db.c:3003-3065) ===
 */
keyStatus expireIfNeeded(redisDb *db, robj *key, kvobj *kv, int flags) {
    debugAssert(key != NULL || kv != NULL);

    /* NOTE: Keys in slots scheduled for trimming can still exist for a while.
     * We don't delete it here, return KEY_VALID if allowing access to trimmed
     * keys, and return KEY_TRIMMED otherwise. */
    sds key_name = key ? key->ptr : kvobjGetKey(kv);
    if (asmIsKeyInTrimJob(key_name)) {
        if (server.allow_access_trimmed || (flags & EXPIRE_ALLOW_ACCESS_TRIMMED))
            return KEY_VALID;

        /* If the slot is not served by this node, we should not allow access
         * to the key, we consider it as trimmed. */
        if (!clusterCanAccessKeysInSlot(getKeySlot(key_name)))
            return KEY_TRIMMED;
    }

    if ((flags & EXPIRE_ALLOW_ACCESS_EXPIRED) ||
        (!keyIsExpired(db,  key ? key->ptr : NULL, kv)))
        return KEY_VALID;

    /* If we are running in the context of a replica, instead of
     * evicting the expired key from the database, we return ASAP:
     * the replica key expiration is controlled by the master that will
     * send us synthesized DEL operations for expired keys. The
     * exception is when write operations are performed on writable
     * replicas.
     *
     * In cluster mode, we also return ASAP if we are importing data
     * from the source, to avoid deleting keys that are still in use.
     * We create a fake master client for data import, which can be
     * identified using the CLIENT_MASTER flag.
     *
     * Still we try to return the right information to the caller,
     * that is, KEY_VALID if we think the key should still be valid,
     * KEY_EXPIRED if we think the key is expired but don't want to delete it at this time.
     *
     * When replicating commands from the master, keys are never considered
     * expired. */
    if (server.masterhost != NULL || server.cluster_enabled) {
        if (server.current_client && (server.current_client->flags & CLIENT_MASTER)) return KEY_VALID;
        if (server.masterhost != NULL && !(flags & EXPIRE_FORCE_DELETE_EXPIRED)) return KEY_EXPIRED;
    }

    /* Check if user configuration disables lazy-expire deletions in current state.
     * This will only apply if the server doesn't mandate key deletion to operate correctly (write commands). */
    if (!(flags & EXPIRE_FORCE_DELETE_EXPIRED) && !confAllowsExpireDel())
        return KEY_EXPIRED;

    /* In some cases we're explicitly instructed to return an indication of a
     * missing key without actually deleting it, even on masters. */
    if (flags & EXPIRE_AVOID_DELETE_EXPIRED)
        return KEY_EXPIRED;

    /* If 'expire' action is paused, for whatever reason, then don't expire any key.
     * Typically, at the end of the pause we will properly expire the key OR we
     * will have failed over and the new primary will send us the expire. */
    if (isPausedActionsWithUpdate(PAUSE_ACTION_EXPIRE)) return KEY_EXPIRED;

    /* Perform deletion */
    if (key) {
        deleteExpiredKeyAndPropagate(db, key);

=== keyIsExpired allow_access_expired (db.c:2944-2955) ===
 * Provide either the key name for a lookup or KV object (to save lookup)
 */
int keyIsExpired(redisDb *db, sds key, kvobj *kv) {
    /* Don't expire anything while loading. It will be done later. */
    if (server.loading || server.allow_access_expired) return 0;
    mstime_t when = getExpire(db, key, kv);
    if (when < 0) return 0; /* No expire for this key */
    const mstime_t now = commandTimeSnapshot();
    /* The key expired if the current (virtual or real) time is greater
     * than the expire time of the key. */
    return now > when;
}

150	- **"A key past its TTL is never observed" is FALSE as stated.** It holds only for the
151	  `lookupKey*` path. `DBSIZE`, `INFO keyspace`, `DEBUG OBJECT/DIGEST`, `RANDOMKEY` after
152	  100 tries, and `HLEN` all report logically-expired keys. The correct statement names
153	  the lookup path explicitly.
154	- **Expiry depends on who is asking.** Master normal client: gone, and deleted. Read-only
155	  replica: gone, but *not* deleted. `CLIENT_MASTER` link: **valid**. Any `exists`
156	  predicate must be parameterized by (role, client flags, loading, pause state).
157	- Lazy expiry uses `now > when`; active expiry uses `now >= when`. They disagree at
158	  exactly `t == expire`. Hash-field TTL uses a third comparison.
159	- **The atomic unit is the execution unit, not the command** (`server.execution_nesting`,
160	  `server.c:1420-1432`): one top-level `call()`, one whole `EXEC`, or one whole script.
161	  Propagation flushes only at nesting 0. Modeling atomicity per-command gives wrong
162	  replication predicates.
163	- **Redis 8.9 is not single-threaded.** Up to 128 IO threads, each with its own event
164	  loop, do full RESP parsing, command lookup, arity checks and socket writes. They never
165	  execute commands (`networking.c:3927-3934`). The correct invariant is narrower: *command
166	  execution and keyspace mutation* are single-threaded on the main thread. `io-threads` is
167	  an IMMUTABLE config and defaults to 1.
168	- **Script atomicity is conditionally broken**: past `busy-reply-threshold` ms a script
169	  re-enters the event loop and other clients' commands run between its `redis.call()`s.
170	- **There is no rollback anywhere.** `MULTI/EXEC` is isolation, not rollback — only
171	  queue-time errors abort. A partially-applied script cannot be killed.
172	- `EXEC` has three distinct outcomes that must not be conflated: `-EXECABORT` (queue-time
173	  error), RESP null array (WATCH invalidated), and a normal array whose elements may be errors.
174	- **WATCH is key-name-based, not value-based** — writing a key back to its original value
175	  still invalidates. One exemption: a key already logically expired at WATCH time.
176	- **`FLUSHALL SYNC` is silently upgraded to ASYNC** (`db.c:1370-1373`): the keyspace empties
177	  immediately but the caller's `+OK` waits for the bio lazy-free queue. Not inside MULTI/script.
178	- **Blocking commands have two contracts**: inside MULTI/script they do not block —
179	  `BLPOP key 0` returns a null array.
180	- `server.dirty` is a hand-maintained global counter across ~135 scattered increments, and
181	  it alone decides propagation. `(keyspace changed) <=> (dirty delta > 0)` is arguably the
182	  single highest-value invariant to formalize.
183	- **Durability is a property of the event loop, not the command.** `flushAppendOnlyFile`
184	  runs at `server.c:2070`, `handleClientsWithPendingWrites` at `server.c:2117`. That
185	  ordering *is* the contract, and it only means "fsynced before reply" under
186	  `appendfsync always`.
187	- `WAIT` counts replicas that *applied* to memory, not fsynced, and says nothing about
188	  which replicas are eligible for election.
189	- **Eviction is user-visible nondeterminism**: `performEvictions()` runs synchronously
190	  *before every command* when `maxmemory != 0`, can delete keys across all DBs, and is
191	  explicitly approximate/randomized. Either pin `maxmemory=0` or model arbitrary key loss.
192	- **A read command can be a write**: `SCAN` and `GET` lazily expire and propagate `DEL`s.
193	- `cluster_asm.c` (~3,900 lines, Atomic Slot Migration) is new in this version and is a
194	  much larger concurrency surface than classic cluster. Treat as a separate project.

- **"A key past its TTL is never observed" is FALSE as stated.** It holds only for the
  `lookupKey*` path. The correct statement is: *no command that reaches its key through

  **Measured on the built binary** (see the oracle recipe below), for one key with an

  | observation | result | deletes? |
  |---|---|---|
  | `DBSIZE` | `1` | no — no expiry filter at all (`db.c:3148-3173`) |
  | `INFO keyspace` | `db0:keys=1,expires=1` | no (`server.c:7272-7283`) |
  | `KEYS *` | `[]` | **no** — filters with `keyIsExpired`, leaves the key (`db.c:1638-1645`) |
  | `EXISTS k` | `0` | **yes** — goes through `lookupKey*`, DBSIZE then drops to 0 |
  | `HLEN h` | `2` | no — counts expired fields, while `HGETALL` returns 1 (`t_hash.c:5596-5603`) |

- **Expiry is ~seven-way conditional, not two-way.** `expireIfNeeded` (`db.c:3004-3065`)
  can return *without deleting* via: `asmIsKeyInTrimJob` → KEY_VALID or KEY_TRIMMED
  (3011-3019); `flags & EXPIRE_ALLOW_ACCESS_EXPIRED` (3022); `server.loading ||
  server.allow_access_expired` inside `keyIsExpired` (2948); the replica / cluster branch
  — note the guard is `server.masterhost != NULL || server.cluster_enabled`, so **cluster
  mode alone triggers it** — with `CLIENT_MASTER` → KEY_VALID (3043-3045);
  `!confAllowsExpireDel()`, gated on the `lazyexpire-nested-arbitrary-keys` config
  (3050-3051); `flags & EXPIRE_AVOID_DELETE_EXPIRED` (3055); and
  `isPausedActionsWithUpdate(PAUSE_ACTION_EXPIRE)` (3062). Several fire on a plain
  standalone master. Any `exists` predicate must be parameterized by (role, client flags,
  loading, pause state, cluster_enabled, config).
- `RANDOMKEY`'s give-up-after-100-tries branch is **conditional**, not unconditional: it
  requires `allvolatile && (server.masterhost || isPausedActions(PAUSE_ACTION_EXPIRE))`
  (`db.c:855-865`). Measured on a standalone master with one expired volatile key:
  `RANDOMKEY` → nil, `DBSIZE` → 0. Do not cite it as a general counterexample.

## Running the real server as an oracle

`redis/src/redis-server` is already built (8.9.241, 17 MB). **`redis-cli` is NOT built** —
speak RESP directly (a ~15-line python socket client is enough; there is one in the
scratchpad). Launch it so the keyspace is quiet and `DEBUG` is available:

```
redis/src/redis-server --port 7799 --save '' --appendonly no \
    --enable-debug-command yes --logfile /tmp/r.log
```

`--enable-debug-command yes` matters: without it `DEBUG SET-ACTIVE-EXPIRE 0` fails and the
active expire cycle reaps test keys within ~100 ms, so every expiry experiment silently
gives the wrong answer. The table above was measured under exactly this configuration.

## Risks that are not yet retired

- **The "known-good" Rust path is unexercised with this cvlr.** stellar-contracts pins
  `cvlr = "0.4.0"` from crates.io and its `Cargo.lock` contains **zero** cvlr entries; it
  imports `rule` from `cvlr_soroban_derive`, never `cvlr::rule`. So nothing in this
  workspace has ever been built against the local cvlr 0.6.1, and cvlr's own `#[rule]` has
  never reached the Prover from here. (It *does* compile and link correctly to wasm — that
  part is byte-verified.)
- **The Soroban fallback may not be reachable offline.** If the answer to the Prover
  question is "wrap it in a Soroban shell and use `cvlr_soroban_derive::rule`", that crate
  is a git dependency on branch `soroban-22.0.8` that is not vendored and has no
  `~/.cargo/git` checkout here. Nobody has checked whether it can be fetched.
- **Prover capacity for this shape of problem is completely unestimated.** Every
  bounded-interleaving plan is K keys × C clients × N steps, `nondet::<bool>()` costs a
  full symbolic u64, and stellar's heaviest confs already need `global_timeout 7200` /
  `smt_timeout 6000` / `-split false` for far simpler contracts. Expect to find the ceiling
  empirically.
- **The 8.9.241-only surface was excluded by default, and that may be wrong.** `DELEX`,
  `INCREX`, `HSETEX`, `HIMPORT`, `BLESS`, `GCRA`, `OBJ_ARRAY`, `SET IFEQ`, compact hashes —
  none have an upstream analogue. If this work is *for* the pre-release, that surface may be
  exactly what needs specifying. Confirm before excluding.

## Style models and starting points

- **Style model:** `stellar-contracts/packages/access/src/ownable/specs/` — the complete
  integrity/invariants/panics/non_panics quartet with real `P-NN` numbers.
- **Smallest complete example:** `packages/contract-utils/src/pausable/specs/` (10 rules).
- **Do NOT use as a reference:** `packages/tokens/src/rwa/specs/` — self-labelled
  `status: violated` and `// DUPLICATE CODE`.
- **Conf starting points:** `packages/contract-utils/confs/pausable_non_panics.conf`
  (minimal) or `packages/tokens/confs/vault_64_invariants.conf` (richest).
- **Rule-name prefixes for `redis-cvlr`** (names are global symbols): `expire_`, `string_`,
  `keyspace_`, `multi_`, `blocked_`, `propagate_`.
- `-trapAsAssert true` is **required** for any non-panic rule. Without it a Rust trap is
  treated as an assumed-away path and `cvlr_assert!(true)` holds vacuously.

## Leads worth chasing

- **Suspected WATCH/EXEC soundness bug at `src/multi.c:415`.** Inside the `wk->expired`
  branch of `touchWatchedKey`, the code `break`s out of the entire watcher list rather than
  `continue`ing to the next watcher. Watchers are appended with `listAddNodeTail`
  (`multi.c:332`), so when a key that was logically expired at WATCH time is later
  overwritten and is still present, watchers positioned *after* that entry appear never to
  receive `CLIENT_DIRTY_CAS`. Control flow and list ordering confirmed; **the end-to-end
  scenario has not been constructed.** Treat as a strong candidate, not a finding. If it
  holds it is the single best advertisement for doing this work.
- `(keyspace changed) <=> (server.dirty delta > 0)` — ~135 hand-maintained increments decide
  all propagation. Highest-value single invariant to formalize.
MDEOF

Corrections it forced

Expiry is ~seven-way conditional, not two-way. I'd written "master deletes, replica hides, master-link sees valid." Reading db.c:3004-3065 myself: there are seven paths that return without deleting, several firing on a plain standalone master — asmIsKeyInTrimJob, EXPIRE_ALLOW_ACCESS_EXPIRED, server.allow_access_expired, !confAllowsExpireDel() (gated on a config), EXPIRE_AVOID_DELETE_EXPIRED, and PAUSE_ACTION_EXPIRE. Also the replica branch guard is masterhost != NULL || server.cluster_enabled, so cluster mode alone triggers it. This is the property everyone proposed to specify first, and the survey's version was wrong.

My RANDOMKEY claim was wrong. I'd listed it as an unconditional counterexample. The give-up branch requires allvolatile && (masterhost || paused). Measured: standalone master, one expired volatile key → RANDOMKEY returns nil, DBSIZE 0.

I ran the expiry experiments against the built server rather than trusting the report:

result deletes?
DBSIZE 1 no
KEYS * [] no — filters, doesn't delete
EXISTS k 0 yes
HLEN h 2 no (HGETALL returns 1)

EXISTS triggering the delete — not just GET — is new, and so is KEYS filtering without deleting.

Two risks that weren't visible before

The "known-good" Rust path is itself unexercised: stellar pins cvlr = "0.4.0" and its Cargo.lock has zero cvlr entries, and it always imports rule from cvlr_soroban_derive, never cvlr::rule. So nothing here has been built against the local 0.6.1. It compiles and links correctly to wasm — I byte-verified that — but "compiles" isn't "the Prover accepts it." And the obvious fallback (wrap in a Soroban shell) depends on an unvendored git branch with no local checkout, so it may not even fetch.

A lead

Possible WATCH/EXEC soundness bug at src/multi.c:415: inside the wk->expired branch, touchWatchedKey does break rather than continue, exiting the whole watcher list. Watchers are appended tail-first, so watchers positioned after that entry may never get CLIENT_DIRTY_CAS when a key that was logically expired at WATCH time is later overwritten. Control flow and list ordering confirmed; the end-to-end scenario is not constructed, so it's a candidate, not a finding. Worth attacking early — if it holds, it's the best possible argument for this project.

One thing I should have asked

All three approach agents silently excluded the 8.9.241-only surface — DELEX, INCREX, HSETEX, HIMPORT, BLESS, GCRA, OBJ_ARRAY, SET IFEQ, compact hashes. None have upstream analogues. If this work is for the pre-release, that surface may be exactly what needs specifying, and excluding it is the wrong call to make on your behalf. Worth settling before I pick commands.

cat > src/model/state.rs <<'EOF'
//! Abstract Redis state.
//!
//! DESIGN DECISION 1 (load-bearing): the keyspace is PHYSICAL. `Slot::present` mirrors
//! membership in `db->keys`. Whether a client can *see* a key is a DERIVED function
//! (`visible()`), parameterized by role, caller and config -- it is not a field.
//!
//! This is forced by the C. `dbSize` returns `kvstoreSize(db->keys)` with no expiry
//! filter at all (redis/src/db.c:3148-3173); `KEYS` filters with `keyIsExpired` but does
//! NOT delete (db.c:1638-1645); `EXISTS`/`GET` reach the key through `lookupKey*` and DO
//! delete. Measured on redis-server 8.9.241 with one elapsed `PX`:
//!   DBSIZE -> 1, INFO db0:keys=1, KEYS * -> [] (DBSIZE stays 1), EXISTS -> 0 (DBSIZE -> 0).
//! A model with a single `HashMap<Key, Value>` cannot state that at all.
//!
//! DESIGN DECISION 2: the propagated effect stream (`ReplLog`) is part of observable
//! state. That is what turns "atomic" from an adjective into a checkable predicate, and
//! it gives every such property a free differential twin against Redis's own
//! `assert_replication_stream` (tests/test_helper.tcl:787-864).

use cvlr::nondet::nondet;

/// Bounds. Keep tiny: `optimistic_loop` + `loop_iter` in the conf silently assume away
/// every execution that iterates past the bound, so "for all keys" means "for all K".
pub const K: usize = 3; // distinct keys
pub const C: usize = 2; // clients
pub const VLEN: usize = 8; // max string value length
pub const REPL_CAP: usize = 8; // bounded propagation log

pub type KeyId = usize;
pub type ClientId = usize;
pub type Ms = i64;

/// No TTL. Mirrors `kvobjGetExpire(kv) == -1` (redis/src/object.h).
pub const NO_EXPIRE: Ms = -1;

// ---------------------------------------------------------------- values

#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Str {
    pub bytes: [u8; VLEN],
    pub len: usize,
}

impl Str {
    pub fn empty() -> Self {
        Str { bytes: [0u8; VLEN], len: 0 }
    }
    pub fn as_slice(&self) -> &[u8] {
        &self.bytes[..self.len]
    }
}

/// Only STRING in iteration 1. Aggregates land with the no-empty-collection invariant.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Value {
    Str(Str),
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Debug)]
pub enum VType {
    Str,
}

impl Value {
    pub fn vtype(&self) -> VType {
        match self {
            Value::Str(_) => VType::Str,
        }
    }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------- keyspace

/// One keyspace entry. `present` is PHYSICAL membership in `db->keys`.
///
/// In 8.9.241 the value is a `kvobj` embedding the key AND the expire timestamp as object
/// metadata (redis/src/object.h:31-66), and `db->keys` / `db->expires` are kvstores over
/// the SAME pointers. So `key in expires <=> expire != NO_EXPIRE` is structural here, not
/// an invariant to prove -- we get it free by storing the expire in the slot.
#[derive(Clone, Copy)]
pub struct Slot {
    pub present: bool,
    pub value: Value,
    pub expire_at: Ms,
}

impl Slot {
    pub fn absent() -> Self {
        Slot { present: false, value: Value::Str(Str::empty()), expire_at: NO_EXPIRE }
    }
    pub fn has_ttl(&self) -> bool {
        self.expire_at != NO_EXPIRE
    }
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Role {
    Master,
    ReadOnlyReplica,
}

/// Who is issuing the command. `MasterLink` is the replication-link client
/// (`CLIENT_MASTER`), for which keys are NEVER considered expired (db.c:3044).
#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Caller {
    Normal,
    MasterLink,
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------- effects

/// A propagated effect, as it appears on the wire to a replica / in the AOF.
/// This is the model's analogue of `assert_replication_stream`.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Effect {
    Multi,
    Exec,
    /// `SET k v` with an ABSOLUTE expire. Redis rewrites relative TTLs to `PXAT` so the
    /// log replays identically against a different clock (t_string.c:178-195).
    Set { key: KeyId, val: Str, pxat: Ms },
    Del { key: KeyId },
    PExpireAt { key: KeyId, at: Ms },
    Persist { key: KeyId },
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy)]
pub struct ReplLog {
    pub ops: [Option<Effect>; REPL_CAP],
    pub len: usize,
}

impl ReplLog {
    pub fn new() -> Self {
        ReplLog { ops: [None; REPL_CAP], len: 0 }
    }
    pub fn push(&mut self, e: Effect) {
        if self.len < REPL_CAP {
            self.ops[self.len] = Some(e);
            self.len += 1;
        }
    }
    pub fn get(&self, i: usize) -> Option<Effect> {
        if i < self.len { self.ops[i] } else { None }
    }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------- clients

#[derive(Clone, Copy)]
pub struct ClientState {
    /// WATCHed keys, by id.
    pub watching: [bool; K],
    /// Whether the key was ALREADY logically expired when WATCH ran. Mirrors `wk->expired`
    /// (multi.c:330) -- the exemption that makes naive CAS models wrong.
    pub watched_expired: [bool; K],
    pub dirty_cas: bool,
    pub in_multi: bool,
    pub dirty_exec: bool,
}

impl ClientState {
    pub fn new() -> Self {
        ClientState {
            watching: [false; K],
            watched_expired: [false; K],
            dirty_cas: false,
            in_multi: false,
            dirty_exec: false,
        }
    }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------- world

#[derive(Clone, Copy)]
pub struct World {
    pub slots: [Slot; K],
    /// Frozen for a whole execution unit (`server.cmd_time_snapshot`, server.c:1420-1432).
    pub clock: Ms,
    pub role: Role,
    pub caller: Caller,
    /// `server.allow_access_expired` (db.c:2948) -- DEBUG SET-ALLOW-ACCESS-EXPIRED.
    pub allow_access_expired: bool,
    /// `isPausedActionsWithUpdate(PAUSE_ACTION_EXPIRE)` (db.c:3062).
    pub expire_paused: bool,
    /// `server.cluster_enabled`. NOTE: the replica escape in `expireIfNeeded` is guarded by
    /// `server.masterhost != NULL || server.cluster_enabled`, so cluster mode ALONE changes
    /// expiry behaviour on a master (db.c:3042).
    pub cluster_enabled: bool,
    /// `confAllowsExpireDel()`, gated on the `lazyexpire-nested-arbitrary-keys` config
    /// (db.c:3050-3051).
    pub conf_allows_expire_del: bool,
    pub clients: [ClientState; C],
    pub repl: ReplLog,
    /// `server.dirty` delta within the current execution unit.
    pub dirty: u64,
    /// `server.execution_nesting` (server.c:1420-1432).
    pub nesting: u32,
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------- nondet
//
// DRAW, DO NOT FILTER. `nondet::<u64>() % K` keeps these same bodies usable in the
// concrete/fuzzing driver; `cvlr_assume!(k < K)` rejects essentially every concrete run.

pub fn draw_key() -> KeyId {
    (nondet::<u64>() % (K as u64)) as usize
}

pub fn draw_client() -> ClientId {
    (nondet::<u64>() % (C as u64)) as usize
}

pub fn draw_bool() -> bool {
    nondet::<u64>() % 2 == 0
}

pub fn draw_len() -> usize {
    (nondet::<u64>() % ((VLEN + 1) as u64)) as usize
}

pub fn draw_str() -> Str {
    let mut s = Str::empty();
    s.len = draw_len();
    let mut i = 0;
    while i < VLEN {
        if i < s.len {
            s.bytes[i] = (nondet::<u64>() % 256) as u8;
        }
        i += 1;
    }
    s
}

/// A clock bounded well away from i64 extremes so TTL arithmetic cannot overflow.
pub fn draw_clock() -> Ms {
    (nondet::<u64>() % 1_000_000) as Ms
}

pub fn draw_expire_at() -> Ms {
    if draw_bool() { NO_EXPIRE } else { (nondet::<u64>() % 2_000_000) as Ms }
}

impl World {
    /// An arbitrary but WELL-FORMED world. Anything that is structural in the C (e.g.
    /// `expires` being a subset of `keys`) must be structural here too -- an absent slot
    /// carries no TTL.
    pub fn nondet_world() -> Self {
        let mut w = World {
            slots: [Slot::absent(); K],
            clock: draw_clock(),
            role: if draw_bool() { Role::Master } else { Role::ReadOnlyReplica },
            caller: if draw_bool() { Caller::Normal } else { Caller::MasterLink },
            allow_access_expired: false,
            expire_paused: false,
            cluster_enabled: false,
            conf_allows_expire_del: true,
            clients: [ClientState::new(); C],
            repl: ReplLog::new(),
            dirty: 0,
            nesting: 0,
        };
        let mut i = 0;
        while i < K {
            let present = draw_bool();
            w.slots[i] = if present {
                Slot { present: true, value: Value::Str(draw_str()), expire_at: draw_expire_at() }
            } else {
                Slot::absent()
            };
            i += 1;
        }
        w
    }

    /// The default scope fence for iteration 1: standalone master, normal client, no
    /// debug escapes, no pause, cluster off. Rules that want the general case skip this.
    pub fn pin_standalone_master(&mut self) {
        self.role = Role::Master;
        self.caller = Caller::Normal;
        self.allow_access_expired = false;
        self.expire_paused = false;
        self.cluster_enabled = false;
        self.conf_allows_expire_del = true;
    }

    /// Observable projection: the `csvdump`-shaped snapshot
    /// (redis/tests/support/util.tcl:496-570). Encoding, refcount, LRU and memory are
    /// ABSENT from the model, not hidden in it.
    pub fn snapshot_visible(&self) -> [Option<(VType, Str, Ms)>; K] {
        let mut out = [None; K];
        let mut i = 0;
        while i < K {
            if self.slots[i].present && !crate::model::expire::key_is_expired(self, i) {
                if let Value::Str(s) = self.slots[i].value {
                    out[i] = Some((VType::Str, s, self.slots[i].expire_at));
                }
            }
            i += 1;
        }
        out
    }
}
EOF

/* Callback passed to kvstoreExpand. Skip expansion for slots not owned by this
 * node, or by its master when this node is a replica. */
static int dbExpandSkipSlot(int slot) {
    return !clusterNodeCoversSlot(clusterNodeGetMaster(getMyClusterNode()), slot);
}

/*
 * This functions increases size of the main/expires db to match desired number.
 * In cluster mode resizes all individual dictionaries for slots that this node owns.
 *
 * Based on the parameter `try_expand`, appropriate dict expand API is invoked.
=== deleteExpiredKeyAndPropagate ===
2898:void deleteExpiredKeyAndPropagate(redisDb *db, robj *keyobj) {
void deleteExpiredKeyAndPropagate(redisDb *db, robj *keyobj) {
    deleteKeyAndPropagate(db, keyobj, NOTIFY_EXPIRED, NULL);
}

/* Delete the specified evicted key and propagate. */
void deleteEvictedKeyAndPropagate(redisDb *db, robj *keyobj, long long *key_mem_freed) {
    deleteKeyAndPropagate(db, keyobj, NOTIFY_EVICTED, key_mem_freed);
}

/* Propagate an implicit key deletion into replicas and the AOF file.
 * When a key was deleted in the master by eviction, expiration or a similar
 * mechanism a DEL/UNLINK operation for this key is sent
 * to all the replicas and the AOF file if enabled.
 *
 * This way the key deletion is centralized in one place, and since both
 * AOF and the replication link guarantee operation ordering, everything
 * will be consistent even if we allow write operations against deleted
 * keys.
 *
 * This function may be called from:
 * 1. Within call(): Example: Lazy-expire on key access.
 *    In this case the caller doesn't have to do anything
 *    because call() handles server.also_propagate(); or
Assistant2026-09-19T18:28:47.320Z

cat > src/model/expire.rs <<'EOF'
//! Expiry: a faithful transcription of `keyIsExpired` and `expireIfNeeded`.
//!
//! This is the module the whole specification turns on, and it is the one every informal
//! account of Redis gets wrong. The headline folklore -- "a key past its TTL is never
//! observed" -- is FALSE as an unconditional statement. It holds only for the
//! `lookupKey*` path.
//!
//! Transcribed from redis/src/db.c:2946-2073 (`keyIsExpired`) and db.c:3004-3073
//! (`expireIfNeeded`) as of 8.9.241. Every branch below cites its line.

use super::state::*;

/// Mirrors `keyStatus` (redis/src/db.c:39-45).
#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Debug)]
pub enum KeyStatus {
    /// Key is valid (or does not exist).
    Valid,
    /// Logically expired, but deliberately NOT deleted here.
    Expired,
    /// Logically expired and physically removed by this call.
    Deleted,
}

/// Flags to `expireIfNeeded`. Mirrors the `EXPIRE_*` constants in db.c.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Default)]
pub struct ExpireFlags {
    /// `EXPIRE_ALLOW_ACCESS_EXPIRED` (db.c:3022).
    pub allow_access_expired: bool,
    /// `EXPIRE_FORCE_DELETE_EXPIRED` (db.c:3044, 3050) -- set on write lookups.
    pub force_delete_expired: bool,
    /// `EXPIRE_AVOID_DELETE_EXPIRED` (db.c:3055).
    pub avoid_delete_expired: bool,
}

impl ExpireFlags {
    pub fn read() -> Self {
        ExpireFlags::default()
    }
    /// `lookupKeyWrite` forces deletion so writes never operate on a stale value.
    pub fn write() -> Self {
        ExpireFlags { force_delete_expired: true, ..Default::default() }
    }
}

/// `keyIsExpired` -- db.c:2946-2955.
///
/// ```text
/// if (server.loading || server.allow_access_expired) return 0;
/// mstime_t when = getExpire(db, key, kv);
/// if (when < 0) return 0;
/// const mstime_t now = commandTimeSnapshot();
/// return now > when;              <-- STRICT
/// ```
///
/// The comparison is STRICT (`now > when`). The active expire cycle uses
/// `if (now < expire) return 0;` i.e. expires at `now >= when` (expire.c:40-41). At
/// exactly `now == when` a lazy lookup sees the key as VALID while the active cycle
/// deletes it. Hash-field TTL uses a third comparison (`expiredAt < now`,
/// t_hash.c:2118-2120). A model that picks one comparison disagrees with the others.
pub fn key_is_expired(w: &World, k: KeyId) -> bool {
    if w.allow_access_expired {
        return false; // db.c:2948
    }
    let when = w.slots[k].expire_at;
    if when == NO_EXPIRE {
        return false; // db.c:2950 -- `when < 0`
    }
    w.clock > when // db.c:2954 -- STRICT
}

/// The active expire cycle's comparison -- expire.c:40-41. Deliberately distinct from
/// `key_is_expired`; the one-millisecond disagreement is a real, pinned property.
pub fn active_cycle_would_expire(w: &World, k: KeyId) -> bool {
    let when = w.slots[k].expire_at;
    if when == NO_EXPIRE {
        return false;
    }
    w.clock >= when
}

/// `expireIfNeeded` -- db.c:3004-3073.
///
/// SEVEN paths return without deleting, several of which fire on a plain standalone
/// master. Enumerated here in source order; `asmIsKeyInTrimJob` (db.c:3011-3019) is the
/// eighth and is modeled as always-false because atomic slot migration is out of scope.
pub fn expire_if_needed(w: &mut World, k: KeyId, flags: ExpireFlags) -> KeyStatus {
    // (1) db.c:3022 -- explicit caller opt-out, and (2) db.c:3023 -- not expired at all.
    if flags.allow_access_expired || !key_is_expired(w, k) {
        return KeyStatus::Valid;
    }

    // (3) db.c:3042-3045. NOTE the guard is `masterhost != NULL || cluster_enabled`, so
    // enabling cluster changes expiry behaviour even on a master.
    if w.role == Role::ReadOnlyReplica || w.cluster_enabled {
        // A command arriving FROM the master never sees keys as expired.
        if w.caller == Caller::MasterLink {
            return KeyStatus::Valid; // db.c:3043
        }
        if w.role == Role::ReadOnlyReplica && !flags.force_delete_expired {
            // The replica hides the key but does NOT delete it: expiry on a replica is
            // driven by synthesized DELs from the master. This is a genuine
            // master/replica observable divergence.
            return KeyStatus::Expired; // db.c:3044
        }
    }

    // (4) db.c:3050-3051 -- user config disables lazy-expire deletion.
    if !flags.force_delete_expired && !w.conf_allows_expire_del {
        return KeyStatus::Expired;
    }

    // (5) db.c:3055 -- caller wants "missing" reported without a delete, even on a master.
    if flags.avoid_delete_expired {
        return KeyStatus::Expired;
    }

    // (6) db.c:3062 -- expiry action paused (e.g. during failover or slot handoff).
    if w.expire_paused {
        return KeyStatus::Expired;
    }

    // db.c:3063-3072 -- perform deletion and propagate a bare DEL.
    delete_expired_key_and_propagate(w, k);
    KeyStatus::Deleted
}

/// `deleteExpiredKeyAndPropagate` -> `deleteKeyAndPropagate` (db.c:2898-2900).
///
/// The propagated DEL is a standalone op. It is deliberately NOT wrapped in MULTI/EXEC --
/// pinned by redis/tests/unit/expire.tcl:809 and :830.
pub fn delete_expired_key_and_propagate(w: &mut World, k: KeyId) {
    w.slots[k] = Slot::absent();
    w.repl.push(Effect::Del { key: k });
    w.dirty += 1;
    // A physical delete of a watched key dirties the CAS of every watcher -- EXCEPT the
    // watchers that saw it already logically expired at WATCH time (multi.c:397-406).
    super::watch::touch_watched_key(w, k, /* from_expiry = */ true);
}

/// `lookupKeyRead` -- the read path. Returns the value only if the key survives
/// `expire_if_needed`.
pub fn lookup_key_read(w: &mut World, k: KeyId) -> Option<Value> {
    if !w.slots[k].present {
        return None;
    }
    match expire_if_needed(w, k, ExpireFlags::read()) {
        KeyStatus::Valid => Some(w.slots[k].value),
        // Both Expired and Deleted are INVISIBLE to the caller. The difference is whether
        // the key is still physically there -- which DBSIZE can see and GET cannot.
        KeyStatus::Expired | KeyStatus::Deleted => None,
    }
}

/// `lookupKeyWrite` -- forces deletion so a write never lands on a stale value.
pub fn lookup_key_write(w: &mut World, k: KeyId) -> Option<Value> {
    if !w.slots[k].present {
        return None;
    }
    match expire_if_needed(w, k, ExpireFlags::write()) {
        KeyStatus::Valid => Some(w.slots[k].value),
        KeyStatus::Expired | KeyStatus::Deleted => None,
    }
}

/// An adversarial active-expire step. Not a background detail to be assumed away: it is a
/// `Step` the prover may insert anywhere, which turns "a key may vanish at any moment"
/// from prose into a hypothesis every other rule must survive.
///
/// On a replica the cycle does not delete (expire.c) -- expiry is master-driven.
pub fn active_expire_cycle(w: &mut World) {
    if w.role == Role::ReadOnlyReplica || w.expire_paused {
        return;
    }
    let mut i = 0;
    while i < K {
        if w.slots[i].present && active_cycle_would_expire(w, i) {
            delete_expired_key_and_propagate(w, i);
        }
        i += 1;
    }
}

/* "Touch" a key, so that if this key is being WATCHed by some client the
 * next EXEC will fail.
 *
 * Sanitizer suppression: IO threads also read c->flags, but never modify
 * it or read the CLIENT_DIRTY_CAS bit, main thread just only modifies
 * this bit, so there is actually no real data race. */
REDIS_NO_SANITIZE("thread")
void touchWatchedKey(redisDb *db, robj *key) {
    list *clients;
    listIter li;
    listNode *ln;

    if (dictSize(db->watched_keys) == 0) return;
    clients = dictFetchValue(db->watched_keys, key);
    if (!clients) return;

    /* Mark all the clients watching this key as CLIENT_DIRTY_CAS */
    /* Check if we are already watching for this key */
    listRewind(clients,&li);
    while((ln = listNext(&li))) {
        watchedKey *wk = redis_member2struct(watchedKey, node, ln);
        client *c = wk->client;

        if (wk->expired) {
            /* The key was already expired when WATCH was called. */
            if (db == wk->db &&
                equalStringObjects(key, wk->key) &&
                dbFind(db, key->ptr) == NULL)
            {
                /* Already expired key is deleted, so logically no change. Clear
                 * the flag. Deleted keys are not flagged as expired. */
                wk->expired = 0;
                goto skip_client;
            }
            break;
        }

        c->flags |= CLIENT_DIRTY_CAS;
        /* As the client is marked as dirty, there is no point in getting here
         * again in case that key (or others) are modified again (or keep the
         * memory overhead till EXEC). */
        unwatchAllKeys(c);

    skip_client:
        continue;
    }
}

/* Set CLIENT_DIRTY_CAS to all clients of DB when DB is dirty.
 * It may happen in the following situations:
 * - FLUSHDB, FLUSHALL, SWAPDB, end of successful diskless replication.

# Add an ordered per-key watcher list: touchWatchedKey's `break` depends on list position.
s = s.replace('''#[derive(Clone, Copy)]
pub struct World {
    pub slots: [Slot; K],''','''/// Per-key ORDERED watcher list, mirroring `db->watched_keys` mapping a key to a `list*`
/// of `watchedKey` in insertion order (`listAddNodeTail`, multi.c:332).
///
/// The order is load-bearing, not an implementation detail: `touchWatchedKey` contains a
/// `break` (multi.c:415) that abandons the REST of the list, so which watchers get dirtied
/// depends on their position. A model keyed only by client id cannot express that.
#[derive(Clone, Copy)]
pub struct WatchTable {
    pub order: [[Option<ClientId>; C]; K],
    pub len: [usize; K],
}

impl WatchTable {
    pub fn new() -> Self {
        WatchTable { order: [[None; C]; K], len: [0usize; K] }
    }
    /// `listAddNodeTail` (multi.c:332).
    pub fn add_tail(&mut self, k: KeyId, c: ClientId) {
        if self.len[k] < C {
            self.order[k][self.len[k]] = Some(c);
            self.len[k] += 1;
        }
    }
    pub fn remove(&mut self, k: KeyId, c: ClientId) {
        let mut i = 0;
        let mut w = 0;
        let n = self.len[k];
        while i < n {
            if self.order[k][i] != Some(c) {
                self.order[k][w] = self.order[k][i];
                w += 1;
            }
            i += 1;
        }
        let mut j = w;
        while j < C {
            self.order[k][j] = None;
            j += 1;
        }
        self.len[k] = w;
    }
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy)]
pub struct World {
    pub slots: [Slot; K],
    pub watched: WatchTable,''')

s = s.replace('''            clients: [ClientState::new(); C],
            repl: ReplLog::new(),''','''            clients: [ClientState::new(); C],
            watched: WatchTable::new(),
            repl: ReplLog::new(),''')
